# Update log

## 2026-10-30
* **Update**: Left by the one row list (the deferred list's entry), three of its four: a long list (more than 30 rows: a 2DA's) lays out only the rows in sight while open (`show_rows`, the chosen one put in the middle when opened), finds its rows once while open and again only when the filter changes, measures its width by its 64 longest rows, and is put in order for the arrow keys only when a key or the wheel asks (a box with the keys was sorted every frame): 62 frames of a list of 50,000 rows open took 5.8 s in a release build (93 ms a frame) and take 20 ms (`a_long_list_lays_out_only_the_rows_in_sight`; the 2DA editor's "a row of spells.2da" opened, `table_row_list_opened` in `screens.rs`, looks as it did); `mg_rules::name_order` gives `by_name`'s order as indexes. Advanced Search's Value list of an item property keeps the cost table's order, as the item editor's Cost Parameter does (`advanced_search_lists_a_property_s_values_in_the_cost_table_s_order`, which fails without it: +10 above +2); the manual's Blueprints says both. Verified: the Appearance and loading screen lists have the "≠" mark since 2026-10-28 (`Form::appearance_choice` and `Form::load_screen` in `blueprint/mod.rs` call `mark_mixed`; `fields_that_differ_among_blueprints_edited_together_are_marked` counts the appearance's). The parameter list's order waits on the Aurora batch, which the entry tells what to capture.
* **Fix**: `rename`'s `usages`, `tag_usages` and `mentions` restated what `UsageIndex` answers (the deferred list's entry): each is now one question of an index read for it, of the resources it can find something in (scripts and GFFs, GFFs, scripts), so where a resource is used, a tag and a script's string are read one way; the rename's warning of the strings that still spell the old name asks the window's index where it is at hand (the scripts the rename changed read again), else the scripts. Tests: `the_index_answers_as_the_scan_and_reads_only_what_changed` (the index read bit by bit against one read anew), `usages_have_readable_places`, `a_rename_says_which_strings_still_spell_the_old_name` (`--test app`).
* **Update**: A copy's resref, what is left (the deferred list's entry): the numbering Moonglow names new resources by is one function, `Naming::numbered` (the first of `name001`, `name002`... free, in the project's pattern, cut to 16; four digits past 999, none past 9999), which Add to Palette's and Edit Copy's names (`palette_add`), a copy's (`palette_view::copy_resref_named`), a wizard's blueprint (`blueprints::resref_named`), a store's conversation (`store_setup::next_name`) and a conversation imported take; a name from a file's stem is `ResRef::name_of` (lower case, `_` for what the rule of new names doesn't take: an imported conversation's `-` is kept now, as a NUI picture's was). The Area Wizard's unpadded `area1` and a NUI picture's `_2` are other rules, Aurora's and the NUI Creator's, and stay. The `002` of Add to Palette's copies of unnumbered templates is not the bare template's being in the module (`mgp_utp.utp` was in `placement-doors.mod` too, which gave `mgp_utp001`): the entry now says what the Aurora batch should capture. Tests: `a_builder_s_code_goes_into_the_team_s_pattern`'s numbering, `a_name_moonglow_makes_is_letters_digits_underscores_and_hyphens`'s `name_of`, the Aurora comparisons (`--test aurora`) unchanged.
* **Update**: The two portrait filters, the Creature Wizard's (the deferred list's entry): waits on the Aurora batch, which the entry now tells what to read (the wizard's portrait page for Human and Male: rows of `Sex` 2 to 4, Plot rows, the module's own portrait, the order).
* **Fix**: A valid resource name, one rule (the deferred list's "A valid resource name, three rules"): the names Moonglow makes for resources are `mg_core::ResRef::new_name`'s, 1 to 16 ASCII letters, digits, `_` and `-`, lowered (the union of what Moonglow's own places took, so no name a builder could give before is refused but a new tileset's with a space or a dot, and a plugin's sound so), narrower than the game's (`ResRef::from_str`: any 16 bytes but NUL) because the name is a project's file's too. It names a line's sound in the talk-table editor and `mg tlk` (`-` now taken) and from a plugin's `tlk:set` (which took any 16 ASCII characters: a sound `mg tlk` refused, the entry's case, is now refused there too), a hak's resource (`hak_edit::key_for`, a plugin's `ctx.hak:write` with it), a new talk table (capitals lowered, where they were refused) and a new tileset (a space refused, where any 16 characters were taken); a NUI window's name is `new_name_of(name, 14)` in lower case without `-` (its scripts' functions are named after it). The places that check a name the builder gives against the game's limit (a grass texture, a material's textures, a talk table attached, the doctor's hint for the module's table, a terrain's name) keep the game's 16; a hak's name is a file's, not a resource's (a plugin's 32, a Custom Light's new hak 16 without `-`: left as they are). Tests: `a_name_moonglow_makes_is_letters_digits_underscores_and_hyphens` (mg-core), `a_line_s_sound_is_named_by_the_rule_of_new_names`, the plugins' tables test's refused sound, `a_new_tileset_is_named_by_the_rule_of_new_names` and the project's new talk table's (`--test app`); the manual's Modules (the talk table) and Plugin API (`tlk:set`).

The entry is rewritten to what the client should read. * **Fix**: Copy From Area leaves the tiles' lights alone (the owner's word on m02's question: "It should leave the lights alone"); it re-picked every tile's lights from the other area's, so an exterior without tile lights copied over an interior put its tiles out (`an_area_s_lighting_is_copied_and_kept_for_another`). Saved Lighting leaves them too, unless **Tiles' Lights Too**, unticked until ticked, is ticked in its menu; `mg areas --lighting` likewise, unless `--tile-lights`. ([Areas](manual/04-areas.md#area-properties), [the command line](manual/11-command-line.md))
* **Fix**: The UI tests (`--test app`) pass on a loaded machine, as CI's runners are. Most of the 1 to 37 failures a run seen when two full runs shared this machine were the two runs themselves: both wrote the same `target/test-output/` folders, each run's `scratch_dir` emptying them under the other's tests (files gone, an NWSync upload's parts sent twice, a project folder holding the other's claims table). `mg_testkit::test_output` now honors `MOONGLOW_TEST_OUTPUT` for a run beside another (CLAUDE.md says so); with it, pairs on the whole machine, or on 4 or 2 CPUs with a test thread a CPU, failed only on a test that spelled the folder out (now through `test_output`). Under CI's kind of load (both runs on 4 or 2 CPUs, 32 test threads each) 6 of 12 runs failed: the tile search in Tile Properties' All tiles 5 times and the talk table test once, `Harness::run exceeded max_steps (4)`, and the test of a game Moonglow did not start once. Work finished on a thread or timed by the clock asked for frames at once in the middle of a test's run (kittest takes a frame asked for sooner than its 250 ms step as one at once): the look at the module's files polled every 50 ms while it went on, a watch's change, a finished look's or the game's programs' wake each added two or three frames, the script editor's live check spun out its half-second pause, and a gallery's pictures, a few milliseconds' worth a frame, met the area view's animation step. The window now asks for those frames at once only where it keeps going (`background_jobs`, the desktop app: `jobs::wake`, `Moonglow::frame_after`), and in the tests at the next frame they draw; the look's thread asks for its frame when it is done instead of the 50 ms poll. `Moonglow::under_way` says what of the window's own work is going on (a job, a look, the scripts it read to compile, a watch hearing a burst of changes, `Watch::hearing`, pictures to make or being read, an area's reading, the lit check, the references, an update check), and the tests' `settle` waits for it and for the frames to stop, with a 30-second deadline instead of 400 tries; the helpers that type into a filter and choose a palette's type settle, and the talk table test settles after its save. The test of a game Moonglow did not start names its module by the process (another run's stand-in game was found on `mgtest`), and the armor part colors test reads its saved module through `test_output`. After: 24 of 24 full runs, each with another beside it, passed: 18 under that load (12 on 4 CPUs, 6 on 2) and 6 on the whole machine. No assertion weakened, none ignored; `frame_perf`, `world_perf` and the world tests, run by hand, keep their own fixed folders
* **Fix**: The NUI preview does three things as the second client batch read them in the game (`nui_readings_in_the_client`, the deferred list's "Where the preview is known to differ from the game"): a row's last button without a width stretches to a control with a width of its own after the row in its column, until the row is as wide as it less 2, never to one before it (one button under an image 200 wide is 196, two under 400 are 150 and 242: `a_row_s_last_button_stretches_to_a_wider_control_below_it`); at a UI scale that isn't whole each margin, and the gap of two margins between controls, is whole pixels rounded down as a control's size is (ten buttons 30 high are 37 and 43 apart at 111% with margins 2 and 5: `margins_are_whole_pixels_at_a_scale_that_isn_t_whole`); a number in text is the bind's value as a 32-bit float, to as many places as asked (no cap at 16), ties to even (1/3 at 17 places 0.33333334326744080, 2.5 at none 2: `numbers_in_text_are_the_client_s`), where the contributor's reading of 2026-10-08 had ties away from zero (42.125 at 2 places as 42.13): the two readings disagree, and [the deferred list](deferred.md) asks the client for that one again. Pictures before and after of each; the [manual](manual/18-nui-creator.md) says so

Its menu has **Rename**, **Delete** and **Undo Delete**, and **Make a Lighting Scheme**: the lighting as an environment.2da row (no talk-table name; listed by its label) added to the table where the builder chooses (the module's, a copy in it, a hak of the module's, a hak open in the hak editor, a file), open in the 2DA editor to save (`a_kept_lighting_s_row_is_read_back_as_a_scheme`: the row reads back through `Scheme::read` as the fields and colors kept). `mg areas --lighting NAME` (or a file) gives kept lighting from a terminal (`kept_lighting_is_given_to_the_areas`); the UI test `an_area_s_lighting_is_copied_and_kept_for_another` does the rest. * **Verification**: The deferred list's "Walkmesh cutters" (not drawn, not placed: "the trigger kind EE added") asked for what the game hasn't got: a walkmesh cutter is the Neverwinter Nights 2 toolset's, and EE (build of 2025-10-06) has no such kind. No `cutter` in `nwtoolset.exe`, `nwserver-linux` or `nwmain-linux` (their strings, ASCII and UTF-16), nor in the game's `nwscript.nss` (whose object types are creature, item, trigger, door, area of effect, waypoint, placeable, store, encounter and tile) or its talk table; the server's `CNWSTrigger` loads and saves one kind of trigger, its `Type` generic (0), area transition (1) or trap (2), the three Moonglow draws and places. The builders' ask (`research/community_pain_points.md`, §2.6) is for the NWN2 feature; the engine would read nothing Moonglow wrote for it.
* **Fix**: NWSync publishing's "No upload" (the owner approved it on 2026-10-29 and left the way to the sprint): Build › Publish to NWSync's **Upload** sends the repository to the web server by the builder's own command, set in Options › Folders or the project's `[nwsync] upload` (`moonglow.toml`, or `.moonglow/user.toml`, which wins; asked about before it first runs and allowed by its text, as `[commands]` are), never by a way in or a credential of Moonglow's: rsync, scp, WinSCP, `aws s3 sync`, rclone, a share, each with its own login. It runs in the repository's folder once for each part (`{part}`, `{folder}`: `mg_module::nwsync::upload_command`), the data, then the manifests, then `latest`, each after the one before succeeded, since one command over the folder sends `latest` before `manifests/` (rsync goes by name) and nwserver and players' games read the server meanwhile; `latest` naming a missing manifest sends nothing (`upload_parts`). After Remove Unused Data the manifests go before the data (a command with `--delete` takes a manifest away before its data). **Upload after publishing and after removing unused data** chains it; it is a job with its output as a project command's (`project_commands::run_steps`, the runner both share now), Cancel, Show Output. `mg nwsync --upload [COMMAND]` and `mg nwsync-prune --upload COMMAND` for scripts. Tests: `an_upload_s_parts_and_commands`, `the_nwsync_upload_command_is_read_from_either_file` (mg-module), `the_nwsync_repository_is_uploaded_part_by_part`, `a_project_s_nwsync_upload_is_asked_about_first`, `an_nwsync_upload_canceled_sends_nothing_more`, `published_to_nwsync_from_the_build_menu` (`--test app`), `every_command_answers_in_json` and `a_pruned_repository_is_mirrored_by_rsync` (rsync to a folder of the test's), all stand-ins sending nowhere but scratch folders; pictures before and after (`nwsync_publish_and_prune`, `options_window` in `screens.rs`: `nwsync-uploading`, `nwsync-uploaded`). The [manual](manual/09-build-and-test.md#uploading-to-the-web-server) says why so, and how for each tool. Closes the deferred entry.
* **Fix**: Skinned and vertex-animated meshes out of the camera's sight are left out (the deferred list's "never culled"): a skinned mesh by the box its bones carry it to (each bone's copy of its bound box, grown or shrunk by what its weights add up to, as the game's programs weigh them, and the bound box too where a vertex is weighed to no bone: `posed_box` in `renderer.rs`, the weights' sums kept on `GpuSkin`), a mesh whose vertices an animation replaces by theirs; they were drawn wherever they were, an off-screen creature's bones posed for its draw. Tests: `skinned_and_animated_meshes_out_of_sight_are_left_out` (bound in sight and carried out of it left out, bound out of it and carried in drawn) and `a_skinned_mesh_s_posed_box_holds_what_its_bones_carry`. Pictures before and after, the same pixel for pixel in the view: the area view with the Domains of Dread's ash zombie and badgers, one across the view's edge, both shaders (`area_creatures_game_shaders`). What stays: the area's objects are still posed each frame wherever they are (`mg-area`), as the shadows they cast into the view need. A renderer change, through CI on a branch.
* **Fix**: What the GPU refuses of the renderer's drawing no longer ends Moonglow (the deferred list's "A model that fails while it is drawn"): a texture it won't take (larger than it holds: Moonglow reads up to 16384 pixels a side, many cards take 8192; data it refuses) is uploaded under the device's error scopes and drawn as a missing one, and one read in the frame that breaks what reads it is none, as on the threads that read them apart; the application's device notes every other error of its drawing instead of wgpu's panic (`mg_render::guard::note_gpu_errors`, `take_gpu_errors`; tests' devices keep the panic). The log says "The GPU refused part of what is drawn and it is left out", once each ([Troubleshooting](manual/13-troubleshooting.md)). Tests: `a_texture_the_gpu_refuses_is_left_out_and_said` and `the_application_s_device_notes_what_the_gpu_refuses` (mg-render), `a_texture_the_gpu_refuses_is_left_out_and_told` (`--test app`: a ground texture 16384 wide in an area, the area drawn without it), `what_the_gpu_refuses_is_said_once_on_a_line`. Pictures looked at: the three cards (`texture_refused.png`) and the area (`refused.png`); before, there was none: the program ended. A panic in the renderer's own frame code is still a crash, as Moonglow's own mistake. A renderer change, through CI on a branch.
* **Update**: [The deferred list](deferred.md)'s rippling glow (TXI `proceduretype arturo`, the Domains of Dread's ash zombie) rewritten to what was found, waiting on the client: its TXI and texture as they are (scale 0, translation 1, `distort 1`, amplitude 10 of a 256-pixel picture, no `defaultwidth`), Moonglow drawing the texture as it is (a picture of the zombie in the area view), the wiki's word that `distort 1` ignores the channels' scale and translation and that the amplitude is in texels (Moonglow's is a part of `defaultwidth`), that the dimness may be the blending rather than the ripple, the game's 39 arturo TXIs, and what the client batch is to read.
* **Update**: CI made faster again, after the morning's measurement (below). Where its time went: develop had no cache left (GitHub keeps 10 GB a repository; the seven caches there, 9.5 GB, were all renderer-ci's, each of its pushes that changed a manifest saving a new 3.8 GB set), so develop's runs started cold (40872200532's macOS 23.8 minutes, 40798056556's Windows 41); and warm, every Test job was its build (Windows 10m44 of an 11.4-minute Test step in 40645701826, the tests 40 s; Linux 5m00, macOS 5m09), mg-ui the long pole, after fmt and clippy (2 minutes on Windows) and, on Linux, the disk's clean-up (1.8) and a second build of the 26 libraries for the doc tests (1m50). Changed: caches saved by develop alone (ci.yml and release.yml; other branches restore develop's); fmt and clippy in a Lint job per system beside the Test job; Linux's clean-up in the background; the tests built without debug information (`CARGO_PROFILE_TEST_DEBUG=0`: the doc tests' libraries are then the tests' own, as the unit graphs show, and the build at -j4 here took 121 and 117 s against 147 and 136, the machine loaded 7 to 13 by other builds); and each Test job's `--timings` report kept as an artifact. The same checks on the same three systems. [The plan](PLAN.md)'s Testing strategy says how CI runs. Measured on GitHub (run 40971587639 on tmp/ci-speed, cold, as no cache was left): the run took 25.7 minutes against the cold 40798056556's 41.5 (Windows' Test job 25.5 against 41.3, Linux's 14.8 against 26.1, macOS' 17.0 against 16.8; the Lint jobs 3.2 to 13.2 beside them), Free disk space 0 s; its `timings-*` reports show Windows building each unit, compile and link alike, about 1.8 times as long as Linux (mg-ui's tests 401 s against 226), so Windows' `rust-lld`, also tried, was taken out again: from `Compiling mg` to the end of the build, where the large links are, it took 163 s against link.exe's 156 (warm, 40645701826) and 177 (cold). Linux's doc tests were skipped there (a timing test of the projects page failed, its author's to fix); without debug information they rebuilt nothing here.
* **Fix**: A portrait, an item's icon or a loading screen that both the module and one of its haks have shows the hak's, as the game reads it (`mg which` names the hak, the module overridden), where the window showed the module's: the images' loader (`images::Loader`) and the NUI Creator's skin find a picture through the resource manager with the module being edited, unsaved changes too, in its layer's place, below its haks and over `override/` (`ResMan::get_over`, and `ResMan::texture_over` for a texture, DDS over TGA within a class of layers as before). Tests: `the_module_being_edited_is_read_in_its_place` (mg-resman), `a_picture_a_hak_and_the_module_both_have_is_the_hak_s` (mg-ui); pictures before and after (`pictures_a_hak_and_the_module_both_have` in `crates/mg-ui/tests/screens.rs`, our own red and blue TGAs)

Asked by a package of the deferred sprint.

* **Update**: A server and its client in one sandbox, for the readings that need a server (`mg_testkit::joined`, `tools/nwclient/run-server-and-client.sh`): `nwserver` started by `mg_serve::Server::start` with its defaults, the script standing in for its program, the client joining on the sandbox's own loopback. The sandboxed client has no CD key (no Steam): Multiplayer asks for one and `+connect` ends in "Your CD Key is unauthorized", so the readings (`joined_dm_creator_palette`, `joined_nwsync_download` in `client_render.rs`'s `joined.rs`) skip unless `MG_CLIENT_CDKEY` names a `cdkey.ini`, the owner's to give; the server's key check is `if-reachable`, its master server being out of reach.
* **Fix**: The outside-changes watch no longer reads every file again on Windows after a single file changes: Windows' watcher also says the folder a file is written in was modified, which the watch took as a folder made and read all of. A folder's own entry modified (not renamed) is now nothing of its own, its files being said themselves; a folder made, removed or moved is still all (`a_folder_modified_is_said_by_its_files`). Found by CI's Windows job (`the_watch_says_which_files_changed_and_only_then`).
* **Fix**: Add Haks and Talk Table (and `mg attach`) take content downloaded as a `.7z` or a `.rar` (RAR 4 or 5) as they take a `.zip`: its haks and talk table are listed and copied from the archive (`mg_module::attach`'s `archive`: sevenz-rust2 and rars, both pure Rust and Apache-2.0, the owner's choice of 2026-10-29; rars is written from the format's description, not from UnRAR's source, whose license the GPL can't take). An archive is read in one pass for what is copied and one for what is compared (a solid one decoded once, not once a file), its files compared with those there by their bytes; a copy that fails leaves none in place. An archive with a password, or one volume of a set, is refused, said why; two files of one name chosen (in an archive and beside it) are refused too. rar-codec, the other pure-Rust reader under a license the GPL takes, was looked at and left: over 143 archives UnRAR extracts (rars' reference set, 54 RAR 5 downloads on this machine and ours) rars gave UnRAR's bytes for every one but volumes of sets and links, rar-codec failed 35 of those (RAR 4 with a comment, a recovery record or a service block, solid RAR 4, PPMd). `a_7z_s_and_a_rar_s_haks_and_talk_table_go_to_the_user_folder` (fixtures of our own text, made by 7-Zip and rars and tested by UnRAR), `locked_and_split_archives_are_refused_and_said_why`, `a_cut_or_damaged_archive_is_an_error_never_a_panic` (zip, 7z, RAR 4 and 5: cut at every length, every byte changed), `content_downloaded_as_7z_or_rar_is_attached_from_the_archive` (`--test app`), `haks_and_a_talk_table_attach_from_a_rar_or_a_7z` (`mg attach`). The manual's Custom Content and `mg attach`. Closes the deferred entry.
* **Fix**: Inventories, part of what was left: Copy and Paste carry the item as its inventory held it (`item_clip` holds the entry), pasted as a drag carries it, so a placed object's own item pasted into another is itself; a weapon dropped on the Secondary Weapon slot while the Primary is free goes in the primary, as Aurora puts a longsword (one moved there from the primary stays); drags between two objects' windows under test both ways. The [entry](deferred.md) is rewritten to what is left: several items at once (M), the secondary slot for other weapons, a move between two slots that needs a feat and a blueprint made in Familiars (each waiting on Aurora, with what the Aurora batch should read), new strings written to a talk-table range (L), and two of Moonglow's own ways proposed to settle. * **Fix**: A slow picture in a gallery ([the frames note](research/notes_frames.md)): a picture's models are read and uploaded on threads of their own, its textures read apart and the game's programs it is the first to draw with made apart, as the area view's are, and it is drawn in a later frame, once nothing it draws waits (where the window keeps going, as the desktop app runs). The palette Gallery's frames as the wheel turns it: the slowest 16 to 20 ms before, 1.8 to 3.2 ms after, the pictures the same pixel for pixel (`a_gallery_s_pictures_are_read_apart_and_look_the_same`; `frames_at_144` runs the galleries so).
* **Fix**: The model compiler and writers in `mg-mdl`, what was left but `mg pack`'s default (proposed to settle, off): a nasher project's Pack compiles its models kept as text where its project file says `[pack] compile_models = true` (`Module::target_archive_with`; Build › Pack and `mg build`), the sources staying text; the linter's notes (Info) are said with the compiler's wherever models are compiled (Compile Models, `mg pack --compile-models`, Pack, `mg model compile`); a compiled model's part numbers are read once, with it (`Model::part_count`, `PartNumbers::of`). Compared with the game's own compiler here: the spec suite's goldens, the game's compiles, in `the_model_spec_suite_passes`. Found done: Compile Models is a job with its progress and Cancel, one step to undo (`hak_view.rs` `compile_models`), Build › Compile Models compiles the module's own (`lib.rs` `compile_models`), and the viewer's copy is gone (its pin is v2.8.0; `mgv-mdl` keeps only its external compilers and the tests that need them).
* **Fix**: Where mg-mdl differed from nwn-mdl-spec, all but one of the entry's items ([the comparison](research/notes_mdl_spec.md)): the spec's conformance suite runs in Moonglow's tests (`the_model_spec_suite_passes`, its own runner over `mg model compile` and `mg model decompile`, new commands; the suite read from a checkout, not committed), every probe passing (46 of 46 compiles, now 46 of 46 decompiles, 14 of 14 stock models). An animated mesh's frames are decompiled over its compiled vertices, and its animation node repeats the mesh's `verts`, `tverts` and `faces`, without which the game crashes (the spec's E54: Moonglow's decompiles had none; the linter calls it an error). A node with no `parent` line is left out with the nodes under it, as the game leaves it, its part number used all the same. A mesh compiled from text has the game compiler's face records and bounds, from the text's faces and vertices. A body part's skin bound to its skeleton is decompiled with its bones named from the skeleton (`mg model decompile`, `mg cat --text`, the resource browser), where the part's own nodes named them wrongly. Left in the entry: a skin vertex with no valid bone, which waits on the client.
* **Update**: The variable sets' entry is settled by the triage's proposals the owner took (object and location variables left out; Undo Delete within its window and no folders kept as they are), and Advanced Search's entry no longer says `mg search`'s JSON leaves out the creature an encounter spawns (it names it as `spawned` since 2026-10-29).
* **Update**: Settled in [the deferred list](deferred.md) by the owner's verdicts of 2026-10-29 (thirteen entries and three items): the areas' sweeps walked five times; the Command Palette's Undo in a script (the entry's other gap dropped); a retired key's line keeping its text; a talk table's JSON as one table's; the round trip by fields; described variables' object and location values (C2); Exact tile's reach; Tile Height and what stands on a tile; Alt for the camera and the selection; and, Moonglow keeping its own against Aurora, marker models unlit, a module's categories in its own order, Tile Properties' Defaults and Escape during a drag; the items: the outside reads' allocator arena, Advanced Search's Appearance, what a module's tabs don't open again by design; and recorded, closing no entry, Export's Reset Factions unticked and the start location without Shift axis arrows. The sections The turning ring and A project's rules, left empty, are gone. [The sprint's count](deferred-sprint.md): 133 open entries (245 items).
* **Update**: Dropped from [the deferred list](deferred.md) by the owner's verdicts of 2026-10-29: the module tree's rows with pictures and a "more" menu (the entry); and items: choosing a codepage per script, the script's Undo and Redo grayed with nothing in its history, Advanced Search's record of a question asked again, and the menus by the keyboard's letter per row, keys for a submenu in a submenu and the bar focused without a menu open.
* **Fix**: Grass over a field of one tile model, from above: with clumps, every tile of the model grew the same clumps, a grid seen from high up (`grass_over_a_field_of_one_tile_looks`, ignored, a look: a 12 by 12 field of one ttr01 tile, every tile turned alike, its grass and what it changes, before and after). The tiles of one model grow one of four kinds of grass by their column and row (`mg_area::grass::VARIANTS`, `variant`), each made and uploaded once, the same every run. The client's stored pictures show its grass only 15 to 20 m out, too few tiles to tell whether it repeats by model. Test: `tiles_of_one_model_grow_their_grass_by_their_places`.
* **Fix**: Make Placeables Static, what it couldn't see: a placeable whose tag one of the module's scripts names as text (a string literal: `GetObjectByTag("GATE_LEVER")`, a constant of it) stays dynamic, as one a script may destroy, animate or make usable; the log (and `mg areas --static-placeables`'s notes and JSON) names the script and the line (`Kept::Script`, counted apart: "whose tags a script names"). The module's `.nss` are read once a command (`areas::ScriptStrings`, `StaticRules::with_scripts`), tags compared exactly as the game's lookups compare; a tag a script makes up as it runs, or one in a compiled script with no source, is still the project's `keep_tags` to name, as [Areas](manual/04-areas.md#static-placeables-many-at-once), [the project file](manual/03-modules.md) and [the command line](manual/11-command-line.md) say. Test: `a_placeable_a_script_names_by_its_tag_stays_dynamic` (a comment's tag and part of a literal keep none). Closes the deferred entry.
* **Fix**: Where an object stands, read two ways and written in six places (the review of 2026-10-21's entry): `mg_module::placement` reads and writes it once, `Placement::read` and `Placement::fields` by kind (doors and placeables `X`, `Y`, `Z` and `Bearing`; the others `XPosition`… and, those that face a way, the orientation's X and Y), `position_fields`, `point_fields` (an outline's `PointX`… or `X`…), `outline` and `fields_of` (all that say where it stands). The area view's objects, moving and turning them, Resize Area, Update Instances and placing a blueprint (`instance`, `placement_of`), `mg find`'s positions, Add to Palette's fields stripped, the locks and Setup Store's shopkeeper go through it, and the area's sounds; a trigger's or an encounter's orientation, which Update Instances once read as a facing, turns nothing anywhere now. The visual transform is read once (`placement::visual_transform`, what the model is drawn with: `VisTransformList`'s scope 0, else its first, else the older struct; `is_transformed`, whether any scope's or the struct changes the look, which Make Placeables Static and Verify's `static-transform` ask), where the area view and `areas` each read it their own way. Tests: `a_placement_reads_back_as_written_for_every_kind`, `an_outline_is_read_by_its_kind_s_point_fields`, `visual_transforms_are_read_one_way`, and the area, instance and palette tests as they were. Which scope the client draws a placeable by when its list has no scope 0 is the client's to say (a reading for the client batch: a placeable whose only entry is of scope 4). Closes the deferred entry.
* **Fix**: Painting placeables, the entry's six left (builders' reports on 1.31.0): **Erase**, a fourth mode of the area view's Paint, takes the chosen blueprints' placeables out of its red circle as it is dragged over them, each marked with a red cross until let go, then as one step (**Erase Placeables**, through the selection's Delete: `area_view::delete_objects`), locked ones and other blueprints' left; **Lock** locks what the brush puts down (`MG_Locked`, as the selection's Lock), so later clicks pass through it; **Count All** counts every placeable in the circle towards Density; **By Size** keeps Spacing between the placeables' sides, each by its model's box as scaled (`paint_mode::reach_of`: the farthest of its sides), the area's own placeables by their boxes as the view draws them; a Row's end is ringed, and a drag begun within a meter of it goes on from it, the next leg's first piece against the last one's (a path of several legs); a Fill's row lines its pieces up by their middles across it, not their near sides. Random scale on static placeables, the sixth, is the game's rule and is kept (verified: the game draws a static placeable without its visual transform, the `static-transform` check; Scale says so). Tests: `spacing_by_size_and_density_counting_every_placeable`, `a_row_ends_where_the_next_leg_begins`, `a_fill_packs_each_piece_by_its_own_size` (by middles now), `area_viewer_erases_locks_and_goes_on_from_a_row` (`--test app area_view`, with its pictures `erase.png` and `row-legs.png`). [Areas](manual/04-areas.md#painting-placeables) says how. Closes the deferred entry.
* **Fix**: Grass in patches (Bee AI's ask): the area view's tufts gather in clumps with bare ground between them, as the client's do, where they were scattered evenly. Read off the client's own pictures of ttr01's field from the reference camera, with its grass and without (`shadows_ttr01_grass_day_off` and `shadows_ttr01_day_off` among the captures): what the grass changes there lies in clumps a meter or two across with bare ground between (the share of the near ground bare in a neighborhood of 15, 31 and 61 pixels 0.25, 0.15 and 0.08), where the area view's was even (0.09, 0.02, 0.00). A clump to 5 square meters of a material's ground, its tufts (the density's share of that ground: as dense on average) within 1.4 m of its middle on the faces of the material (`mg_area::grass::CLUMP_GROUND`, `CLUMP_REACH`): 0.25, 0.16 and 0.06, the dark clumps the client shows, and the near ground's mean color nearer the client's (49.6, 48.3, 26.3 against its 52.5, 51.0, 28.1; it was 49.0, 47.8, 25.9). Sparse grass has a tuft or two a clump, as the client's white tufts at 0.3 a square meter looked scattered (`reading_grass_tufts`). Pictures looked at, before and after: Moonglow's view beside those captures (`shadows_look` with `MG_CLIENT_CAPTURED=1 MG_SHADOW_GRASS=1`, the client not run) and `grass_covers_the_whole_view`'s (whose close look at the target now takes a square of a few meters, as a clump may leave 40 pixels bare). Test: `grass_grows_in_clumps_as_dense_on_average`. How the engine itself places them is still not read; the [areas](manual/04-areas.md#grass) and [differences](manual/12-differences.md) pages say so. Closes the deferred entry.
* **Fix**: The grass mutations, written twice (the review of 2026-10-21's entry): `mg_module::grass` plans each change to a GIT's `AreaProperties` as steps (`set_plan`, `remove_plan`, `move_plan`, `disabled_plan`), which `mg areas` makes in place (`grass::apply`, behind `set_in`, `remove_in`, `set_disabled_in`) and the area view makes as undoable edits (`mg_area::grass::edits`); the area view's Add Grass to… grows its grass as Area Properties' other changes do (`set_override`: the material's override changed in place, where it was put at the end), and the tileset's grass switched to what it already is makes no step. Test: `the_area_view_s_edits_leave_what_mg_areas_leaves` (each change from four GITs, both ways, the same GIT). Closes the deferred entry.
* **Fix**: Verify's warnings of references only the player's own content satisfies can be left out by their kind: `references:override`, `references:userpatch`, `references:workshop`, or all three as `references:local`, in `mg verify --skip` and Options › Verify's Also leave out (`Skip`'s `locals`; the owner's condition for keeping the warnings on by default). Tests: `verify_leaves_out_groups_checks_haks_and_resources` (mg-module), `userpatch_haks_are_read_when_asked_for` (`--test cli`). Manual: the Options, Build and Test and command-line chapters.
* **Fix**: What `mg haks --usage` (and the window's What the Module Uses) follows, five of the deferred list's "Not followed by `--usage`" (`mg_preview::usage`): a creature's footsteps and combat sounds (footstepsounds.2da, appearancesndset.2da's falls and natural weapons, weaponsounds.2da's sounds of each weapon striking its skin), a weapon's hits (baseitems.2da `WeaponMatType`), an item's **Visual Effect** property's model (`<ItemClass>_fx<suffix>`, iprp_visualfx.2da), an item's inventory icon layers as the window draws them, an armor's `ipm_`/`ipf_` part icons too (`mg_rules::items::icon_names`, moved from the window's images), the 2DAs the game reads by itself (a hak's copy of one the game has is used, and a table a used 2DA's cells name: a class's feat table), and what players choose at character creation (every head of the player races, by gender and phenotype; their portraits; the players' voice sets). On The Domains of Dread's module: tdod_rules 25 to 535 of 597 used (its 2DAs), tdod_wbb1 316 to 320 (a weapon's electric effect). The entry is rewritten to what is left: what scripts reach by a number (L), the body parts players choose (waits on the client batch), and two proposals for the owner. Test: `usage::tests::sounds_icons_effects_tables_and_what_players_choose`.
* **Fix**: What the module uses of its haks, in the window too (the deferred list's entry): Module Properties' **Check for Conflicts…** opens the Hak Pak Conflict Analysis with **What the Module Uses**, which works it out as a job on the module as it is (unsaved changes too) and shows each hak's line and every hak's above the conflicts, as `mg haks --usage` says them (its lines no longer wrap: the window scrolls sideways). The walk moved from `apps/mg/src/usage.rs` into `mg_preview::usage`, behind mg-preview's feature `usage` (it reads modules, with mg-module: mg-module can't have the previews, below it, and the viewer, which pins mg-preview, leaves the feature off), with the game a player has from `Module::player_game_data`; `mg haks --usage`'s output on The Domains of Dread's module is the same, byte for byte, before and after. Tests: `usage::tests` (moved), the app test `check_for_conflicts_says_what_the_module_uses_of_its_haks`; pictures `hak-conflicts-before`, `hak-conflicts` and `hak-conflicts-usage` (`screens.rs` `hak_conflicts_and_what_the_module_uses`, on the TDoD build).
* **Fix**: The module's areas one way (the review of 2026-10-21's "The module's areas, two ways"). **Measured in the engine**: the game loads the areas `Mod_Area_list` names, not every ARE the module holds (`engine_area_list.rs`: an ARE it leaves out isn't reached by `GetFirstArea`/`GetNextArea`, and its tag finds nothing). Every tool now sees every ARE and says which the list leaves out: `mg_module::areas::every` (the list's areas in its order, then the AREs it leaves out) for `mg search --placed`, `mg update-instances`, the window's Update Instances, a conversation's speakers and the areas offered with no area open, where they went by the list alone; `AreaInfo::listed` and `areas::unlisted` for `mg areas` (a warning, `listed` in the JSON), `mg info` (`unlisted_areas`) and the module tree, which marks such an area *(not in the area list)* and offers **Add to the Module's Area List** on its menu (one undoable step). The areas are listed and filtered one way too: the module tree's and Edit Areas Together's `AreaInfo` is mg-module's (`areas::info_of`, with the tag), and Edit Areas Together's Filter is `areas::Filter`, so it finds an area by its tag as `mg areas --match` does. Tests: `areas::tests::areas_the_list_names_and_leaves_out`, `areas.rs` (cli) `areas_its_list_leaves_out_are_said_and_names_shown_as_the_tree_shows_them`, the app test `an_area_its_list_leaves_out_is_marked_and_listed_from_its_menu`, `the_chooser_filters_by_text_tileset_and_kind`; pictures `tree-unlisted-area-before`, `tree-unlisted-area`, `-menu` and `-added` (`screens.rs` `module_tree_unlisted_area`).
* **Fix**: A localized string's text where there is no game, one way (the review of 2026-10-21's entry): `LocString::shown_in(language, codepage)` (its own text, else another language's, English first, else its variant even empty), `GameData::shown_in` (the language edited, else as the game shows it, its talk-table string, else another language's; `GameData::locstring` is built on it, the same as before) and `mg_rules::shown` (either, as there is a game). `mg find --name` matches a name in French alone and a talk-table string's text (`query::find_named`, the game's data read where there is a game; names are no longer read as Windows-1252 and spelled again), `mg areas` names an area as the module tree does (`areas::list_named`), `mg info`'s name and description, a conversation's outline and Find References' line text, `mg merge-gff`'s conflicts, and the module tree's blueprint and area names and the window's lists (`tree::shown`, `text::shown_text`) go through them. Left in English on purpose: the hak editor's description (it edits the English one), the raw fields view's English text, a plugin test's file. Tests: `locstring::tests::shown_without_the_game`, `query::tests::names_as_lists_show_them`.
* **Fix**: The game's data for a module is put together one way (the review of 2026-10-21's "The game data for a module, three times in `mg`"): `Module::game_data` (`mg_module::game_data`: the game, the module's haks, the module, its custom talk table) and `player_game_data` (the game a player has, `GameInstall::as_player`, which Check for Conflicts' report uses too), with what it lacks said in the same words everywhere (`ForModule::warnings`: "hak X not found", "the module's haks: …", "custom talk table X not found …"). `mg search`, `mg find`'s and `mg areas`' rows named (and the areas' surface materials, which read the game's surfacemat.2da without the haks), `mg update-instances` (which read no custom talk table before), `mg gff --names` with `--module` (the game alone before), `mg nui render` and the plugins' game data in `mg` and `mg plugin test` go through it (a plugin's game data is none, said, where a hak of the module can't be opened, as every command stops there; it was the haks before that one) (`game_data_in`, `game_data_if_any` in `apps/mg/src/main.rs`), and a game that can't be read is said where `mg search` said nothing. The custom talk table is found one way too (`talk::of_module`, returning `Custom`: none, found, not found, unreadable): Verify's count, NWSync's manifest, the window's custom talk table and a plugin test's fixture; `talk::find_any` is gone. Tests: `game_data::tests`, `update_instances.rs` `a_custom_talk_table_not_found_is_said_alike`.
* **Update**: The deferred list's "Lists across several blueprints" says what each list page needs to be edited across several blueprints, and why the Edit Together guard (`blueprint::partial`) drops it now; sized L, from M.
* **Fix**: Module Properties and the journal are built on `blueprint::Form`, as the blueprints and Area Properties are (the deferred list's "Module Properties and the journal redo `Form` by hand"): the module's tag takes 32 characters as typed (it took any number), a journal category's too (it was cut after), and every tag field is `Form::tag` (`mg_module::tags::MAX_TAG`); the XP scale is `Form::slider`, and a drag that ends where it began adds no empty undo step; their text fields keep what is typed as a blueprint's (`hold_text`: set when the page goes with the field holding the keyboard, and among what Cancel sets back); the "…" for every language is `widgets::languages_button`, in three places of seven. [Modules](manual/03-modules.md). Test: `module_properties_and_the_journal_fields_are_a_form_s`; pictures before and after: `screens::module_properties_and_journal_fields` (the pages as they were, the fields a little narrower, a journal entry's text headed with its language as a description is).
* **Fix**: OK and Cancel where there were none (the deferred list's "OK and Cancel where there is none yet", a builder's ask): the hak editor, the tileset editor, the talk table and a script have them as the 2DA editor has (`side_doc::ok_cancel`, the 2DA editor's now too): OK saves (a new hak asks where; a script's Save puts its text into the module) and closes; Cancel closes as last saved, asking Save, Don't Save or Cancel first (`Moonglow::may_cancel`; the talk table's and a script's × still keep what is unsaved for the module's Save, and their Don't Save drops it); a talk table found in a hak has Close. The raw fields view has a Properties window's. In the windows that had them: text typed in a field whose page went other than by a press of the pointer was set at the frame's end (`Moonglow::held_text`) and not among what Cancel set back; it is now (`blueprint::HeldText` knows its window). A field in a struct of an object's is set back alone (`field_changed`: an area's music, not its sounds with it, the GIT's `AreaProperties`). A faction's removal cancelled numbers the objects it renumbered back as they are now (`faction_view::numbered_back`), instead of setting each area and creature back whole with what was changed in them meanwhile; when the factions file is as none of the window's steps left it, they are set back whole as before. [Blueprints](manual/05-blueprints.md), [Differences](manual/12-differences.md). Tests: `the_file_editors_ok_saves_and_cancel_drops`, `the_raw_fields_view_s_cancel_sets_back_its_change`, `cancel_sets_back_text_set_as_its_page_went`, `area_properties_cancel_sets_back_its_sound_alone`, `a_faction_s_removal_cancelled_numbers_its_objects_back`, `removals_are_numbered_back_last_first`, `a_window_s_change_is_noted_by_its_object_s_field`; pictures before and after: `screens::editors_ok_and_cancel`.
* **Fix**: Dialog windows are built one way (the deferred list's "Dialog windows built by hand"): `widgets::modal` (answered by its own buttons, no close button) and `widgets::dialog` (closed by its × and Escape too), both on `widgets::centered`, which opens a window in the middle of the main one by its middle and lets it be moved by its title. The anchored `widgets::window` is gone, and the 24 windows made by hand (the wizards, Add Faction and Change Faction Name, Levelup, New Script's name, Add Haks and Talk Table, Changed Outside Moonglow, Delete from Module, the Renames, Find Text, About, Publish to NWSync, the update notice, the Plugins and a plugin's Settings, Command Output, Hak Pak Conflict Analysis, Palette Categories, Adjust Location, Select Token, Conversation Test, Variables, Files Already in…) go through them. Select Portrait, Setup Area Transition, Select Texture, the missing feats' question (Equip), a conversation's Input Text and the export's question were an `egui::Modal`, whose Escape the Properties window under it took too: Escape in Select Portrait closed the blueprint's window as its Cancel (seen in a picture). They are `modal`s now, and Escape closes the picker alone; an `egui::Modal` is kept for what stops the work until it is answered (a job's progress, a plugin's questions, the Command Palette). [The main window](manual/02-main-window.md). Tests: `portraits_are_chosen_from_their_pictures` and `equipping_without_the_feat_asks_to_add_it` (Escape); pictures before and after: `screens::dialog_windows`.
* **Update**: The [deferred sprint](deferred-sprint.md)'s count after its thirteen small-fix packages: 161 open entries (275 items), from 440 at its start; 173 fixed, 41 verified and 63 settled in the sprint (fixed and verified counted by the log's lines). The medium-fix wave follows.
* **Fix**: Edit Areas Together chooses areas by what they hold too (**Holding**: music by day, night or battle, a variable of a value, a field's value of the area or its sounds and music; `mg_module::areas::Has`, read as the module is now through `areas::HoldsCache`, an area read again only once its bytes change), and keeps the areas ticked as a set for the module (**Save Set**, **Tick a Set…**, **Delete Set**; `area_sets`, JSON files in the data folder's `area-sets`). Taking Advanced Search's areas into it was there (its **Choose N Area(s)…**, `advanced_search_changes_what_it_found_and_takes_conditions_together`). `mg areas` takes `--where FIELD=VALUE`, `--where-var NAME=VALUE` and `--music ROW` as filters. The chooser's list is 250 points high (320), so the window with its two rows more keeps its height. Tested by `areas_are_chosen_by_what_they_hold` (mg-module and `mg`), `a_set_is_kept_for_its_module_and_read_back` and `areas_are_chosen_by_what_they_hold_and_kept_as_a_set`; pictures in `screens.rs`' `edit-areas-together*.png`, and the [manual](manual/04-areas.md).
* **Fix**: Find References for a talk-table line looks where it didn't: the module's haks' 2DAs, in the columns known to hold a line, each hak's own copy (`rename::hak_strrefs`, listed as **In the haks' 2DAs**); fields that hold a line as a plain number (`rename::STRREF_FIELDS`: a palette's category's `STRREF`, the table the raw fields view can name them by too; a few shipped palettes' `CatName` dword isn't known to be one, and is left out); a soundset's entries; and a script's call of one of `nwscript.nss`'s nine functions that take a line with the number written as that argument (`rename::STRREF_FUNCTIONS`: `GetStringByStrRef`, `SendMessageToPCByStrRef`'s second…; a variable or a constant isn't followed), at its line. Tested by `the_haks_2das_name_their_lines`, `a_script_s_talk_table_lines_are_its_calls_numbers`, `the_index_answers_as_the_scan_and_reads_only_what_changed` and `find_references_finds_a_line_in_scripts_and_the_haks_2das`; the [manual](manual/03-modules.md).
* **Fix**: Find and Replace Text looks in the fields builders and scripts read too, each where ticked (the players' text by default): Tags, ResRefs (not a resource's own name, which Rename changes), Variables (names and string values) and Other fields (`mg_module::text::TextKind`, `TextKind::PLAYERS`); a resref the new text can't be, or a tag longer than 32, is left and said. It and Advanced Search take regular expressions (`regex-lite`): Find and Replace's **Regular expression** (`text::Finder`, its groups named in the replacement, `$1`), Advanced Search's **Regular expressions** for its names and texts (`search::Query::regex`, `query::like`); `mg replace --regex --only tags,resrefs,variables,fields,all` and `mg search --regex`. Tested by `plain_fields_are_found_and_replaced_where_asked`, `a_regular_expression_finds_and_replaces_with_its_groups`, `names_and_texts_by_a_regular_expression`, `find_and_replace_in_tags_by_a_regular_expression`, `replace_takes_plain_fields_and_regular_expressions` and `search_a_module_without_the_game`; pictures in `screens.rs`' `find-and-replace.png` and `advanced-search-*.png`, and the [manual](manual/03-modules.md#find-and-replace-text).
* **Fix**: Duplicate tags are said whatever gives the tag, not only a paste and an area's Copy…: each command applied is weighed (`mg_edit::tags::made_by`, from `Moonglow::apply`): a tag typed in Properties, an object placed from the palette (its blueprint's tag only where the project warns of any), Create Set's waypoints, Find and Replace's Tags, Advanced Search's Set a Field, Edit Together, a plugin's; not an Undo, nor a tag set to what it was. A blueprint copied with a tag another blueprint has is said in the Copy window and the log, as a paste is weighed (`tag_clash::blueprint_copy_warning`, `UsageIndex::blueprints_tagged`). `mg set`, `mg apply`, `mg areas --set Tag=…` and `mg replace --only tags` warn of them. Tested by `tags_typed_placed_or_named_by_create_set_are_weighed_as_a_paste_s`, `a_blueprint_copied_with_another_s_tag_is_said`, `a_tag_set_twice_is_warned_of`, `a_command_s_tags_are_those_it_changes_or_places` and `find_and_replace_in_tags_by_a_regular_expression`; the [manual](manual/04-areas.md#duplicate-tags).
* **Fix**: The Duplicate Tags window's Retag follows what was made with the objects it retags: a pasted or copied door's or trigger's `LinkedTo` that names a tag it changes names the new one, where one object made in that area had it (`tag_clash::retag_edits`; the copy's transitions lead within the copy, the original's within the original), and **Retag All** retags an area copy's tags only counted too (`TagClash::more`, where the project warns of any tag). Scripts that spell a tag listed or counted are named in the window and in the log after a Retag, and left as they are (a script is the original's and the copy's alike). Retag keeps a number's width (`Patrol_01` to `Patrol_02`, as Create Set numbers; `tags::retag`). Tested by `retag_all_follows_the_copy_s_transitions_and_names_the_scripts` and `a_retag_is_the_tag_with_the_first_free_number`; the window's picture in `screens.rs`' `duplicate-tags.png`, and the [manual](manual/04-areas.md#duplicate-tags).
* **Fix**: Described variables (C2) are described further (The Domains of Dread's builder proposal): a variable's `requires` (the variables it needs beside it on the same object: a rental proprietor's `tdod_rental` and `tdod_rental_door`), `[[rules]]` between variables as a table's description has them (`variable`, `is`, `require`: needed where another has a value), and `default` (the value the scripts take where it isn't set, held to the type), read in `mg_module::var_desc` (`Variables::missing`), what is wrong with them said by line. Verify's `var-requires` (a warning, the Objects group) says a variable set without what it needs on the same object; the Variables window says it under the list until it is added, and a row named as a variable with a default takes it as its value; `mg.vars.describe` gives plugins `requires` and `default`. A variable described by the script that reads it: Verify's `var-script` (a warning) finds the module's scripts' reads and sets of a variable by its name written out (`GetLocalInt(o, "tdod_lit")`, `var_desc::script_reads`), and says a variable kept in a file as another type than every one of them takes, with the script and line, where no description gives it a type (one that does is held to it by `var-type`). The Domains of Dread's Verify finds none of either (its types were checked by hand on 22 October), the rest of its report the same. [Modules](manual/03-modules.md), [Plugin API](manual/17-plugin-api.md). Tests: `what_a_variable_needs_beside_it_and_its_default_are_read`, `the_scripts_reads_of_a_variable_by_its_name_are_found`, `a_variable_without_what_it_needs_beside_it_is_said`, `the_variables_window_says_what_a_variable_needs_and_gives_defaults`, `a_plugin_s_tests_run_moonglow_s_own_checks`. The deferred entry is rewritten to what is left: an object's or a location's value, which nothing in a file describes, proposed to the owner to settle.
* **Fix**: Files changed outside Moonglow, six of the entry's seven items (The Domains of Dread's builder proposal, C12). The watches (a module folder's or a nasher project's files, and the haks, `override`, `development` and portrait folders and the custom talk table) are told by the system which files changed (the `notify` crate: inotify, FSEvents, ReadDirectoryChangesW; a file by its folder, so one written whole and moved into place is heard), and say so once a burst is quiet for 300 ms, asking the window for a frame: it looks then, not at its next 3 s, so a change is in a moment after it settles, not up to 7 s. The look takes the watch's list (`Module::outside_scan_of`): those files alone are snapshotted and read, whatever their time (a change within the clock's grain is found), no folder walked, and only their areas' GITs taken for their locks; a folder made, moved or removed, or the system losing count, is a look at all. Where the system can't watch, the walk every 3 s stays; told, the files are still walked every 30 s, for a change the system didn't tell (a network share's). A `.mod` changed outside is read again (`mg_module::archive_file`: its time, size and resources noted as read or written, the file read again on the look's thread when they change and compared by resource), taken or asked about as a folder's file, and Save doesn't write over changes found and not yet answered. Scripts read again compile for 4 ms a frame (`compile_outside_step`), the window taking input, however many a checkout changed (more than 24 went to a job that held it). Measured (`frame_perf` `reload_frames_at_144`, the persistent world as a nasher project, 21,000 files; load 5 to 7 on 32 threads): a pull of 970 files all in 2.1 s from its start (4.6 s before), the longest frame 3.1 ms (3.5), none over the budget; one of 2,870 in 4.5 to 5.8 s (6.2), the longest 6.7 to 9.2 ms over three runs (6.5 before), the look's start 1.6 ms and its take 4.5 ms of the window's thread, both of the files changed, not of the project's. [Modules](manual/03-modules.md), [Options](manual/10-options.md), [Building and testing](manual/09-build-and-test.md). Tests: `the_watch_says_which_files_changed_and_only_then`, `the_content_watch_hears_its_files_and_folders`, `a_path_said_under_a_real_place_is_the_watched_one`, `a_look_at_the_files_named_looks_at_them_alone`, `a_folder_s_look_at_the_files_named_looks_at_them_alone`, `a_mod_changed_outside_is_found_by_resource`, `a_mod_saved_by_another_program_is_read_again`, `many_scripts_saved_outside_are_compiled_a_few_a_frame`. The deferred entry is rewritten to what is left: the reads' allocator arena, proposed to the owner to settle, and the entry's other notes as they were.
* **Fix**: A Verify of a module with many haks reads each hak's models once: what it finds of each model of an archive (its text's findings, `mdl-text`; a tile model's faces, `tile-faces` and `mdl-read`) is kept in the cache folder beside the shader programs that compiled (`verify/`, a file an archive named by its label, time and size, of this release: `mg_module::doctor::Cache`), and a hak that is as it was is not read again; a hak changed is, and its earlier file goes. Folders (`override`, `development`, the module's) are always read. Measured on The Domains of Dread kept as a nasher project with its 145 haks (28 GB; `--user-dir` a scratch folder linking `~/Projects/tdod-module/build/hak`), `mg --json verify --timings` under a 16 GB cap, the machine shared (load 18 to 37 on 32 threads): before, 1.7 to 2.9 s wall and 27 to 30 s of the workers' time (lint 13 s for 1.9 GB of text models, the text tiles' face bound 5.4 s for 1.2 GB, the compiled tiles' 1.3 s for 2.6 GB); now 2.3 s and 23.5 s the first time (the cache written in 0.24 s), then 0.87 to 1.25 s and 8.1 to 8.5 s over seven rounds (with `var-script`, below, 21 ms of it), at most 0.9 GB held rather than 1.3 to 1.6; the JSON byte for byte the same in every round. What a first Verify still does as before: lint every text model and read every tile model (a text tile model read twice, by the linter and for its faces), and on every run the 2DAs (671, 3 to 5 s of the workers' time, `appearance.2da` 0.43 s: the longest part of a Verify whose models are known). [Building and testing](manual/09-build-and-test.md). Tests: `an_archive_s_models_are_read_once_while_it_is_as_it_was`, `facts_read_back_as_written`. Closes the deferred entry.
* **Fix**: Verify leaves out more than whole groups (Taro49's report): one check (`set-hole`, as `mg checks` lists them, or a plugin's, `example.tag-conventions/tag-case`), the missing references of one category (`references:creatures`: Build Module's Missing Resources' categories, which Aurora's dialog keeps as they are), a hak (`tdod_tiles01.hak` or `hak:tdod_tiles01`), the `override` or `development` folder, and one resource (a tileset, `ttr01.set`, with its tiles' models; a 2DA; a model), by `mg verify --skip` and Options › Verify (each group's checks and categories under it as checkboxes, the enabled plugins' checks under Plugins' checks, and an Also Leave Out field for haks, folders and resources; a word that is none of these is said under it and not kept). What is left out is not checked where that can be told beforehand (`mg_module::doctor::Skip`: a hak's or a resource's own checks aren't run, a tile's model isn't read with `tile-faces` and `mdl-read` left out, nor a model's text with `mdl-text`, nor are shader programs compiled with `shader-compile`, nor a plugin's check run), and the summary line and the JSON's `skipped` name each word. [Building and testing](manual/09-build-and-test.md), [Options](manual/10-options.md), [Command-line tools](manual/11-command-line.md). Tests: `verify_leaves_out_groups_checks_haks_and_resources`, `a_group_left_out_is_not_checked`, `verify_s_checkboxes_and_field_apply`, `a_plugin_s_checks_run_with_verify`, `verify_fails_on_errors_and_names_them_in_json`. Closes the deferred entry.
* **Fix**: An area filling in as it opens (the deferred list's entry), taken apart in TDoD's woods with the builder's haks (`game_shaders_frames_at_144`, `FRAME_AREA=tdod`). Reload Resources (and a hak attached or let go) reads an open area's models and textures again on a thread, as its first reading, where it held the window (`reload_resources_reads_an_area_again_on_a_thread`; [Building and testing](manual/09-build-and-test.md)). The rest was measured, not fixed: `frame_perf`'s harness stepped its clock a quarter of a second a frame, so every frame of the opening was an animation step's, drawn whole; at a 144th of a second a step, as the window's frames go (the harness's since), one frame in six is drawn as the textures arrive, the animations' steps as an open view's are, each costing what the view costs drawn whole (15 to 21 ms off, 17 to 26 on), the others under 1.5 ms. The frame it is first shown in is 11 ms, of which its materials (298 meshes' slots, their MTRs looked up and read) are 0.6 to 1.1 ms, not 19 to 30. An edit, read in the frame, costs a millisecond besides its picture (14.2 ms off, 17.8 on, against 13 and 18 drawn alone), and tests read in the frame on purpose. What is left is the picture's cost, in "The largest areas in full view". [The frames note](research/notes_frames.md).
* **Fix**: The minimap in Moonglow (the deferred list's entry): the area view's toolbar has **🗾 Minimap**, the area's map in the view's top right corner as the game's map draws it (`mg_module::minimap`, kept while its tiles stay as they are, unsaved ones included: `crate::minimap`), with the camera's target and the way it faces and the objects selected; a click or a drag on it moves the camera's target there, the selection left be, and the switch is kept for the views opened after (`Settings::area_minimap`). **Setup Area Transition…** is a window, as Aurora's `TdlgAreaTransition`, where it was a list of every area's doors and waypoints: the Target Area, its map with its tagged doors (squares) and waypoints (dots) to click, the one under the pointer named, and its lists beside; OK sets Destination Tag and its type, Cancel nothing. No renderer change: the map is the game's tile pictures. [Areas](manual/04-areas.md), [Blueprints](manual/05-blueprints.md), [the parity checklists](parity/checklists.md). Tests: `the_minimap_shows_the_area_and_a_click_on_it_looks_there`, `setup_area_transition_picks_on_the_target_area_s_map`, `the_map_s_points_are_the_area_s_north_up`, `a_map_is_drawn_again_when_its_tiles_change`. What Aurora's window has besides, its Connection Type (the target linked back), is what the entry is rewritten to: it waits on a capture of what Aurora writes.
* **Fix**: A read-only area no longer takes the pointer (the deferred list's "A read-only area still takes the pointer", the builders' proposal C6): an area another builder holds, in a project that keeps those read-only, is looked at and selected in, and its tools are off (`AreaView::read_only`, from `claims::read_only` each frame, the table read again included). A drag selects by a box or moves the camera, no object; the rings, arrows, scale handle and spawn points' handles aren't there; Delete, Cut, Paste, Q, E and G do nothing; Paint and Select Tiles are grayed, the tile brushes and the palette's blueprints don't place (one dropped on it is named in the log, not placed) and the menu has Preview and Properties. Its Properties and its objects' are gray, with Close for OK and Cancel, as the game's blueprints opened by View are (`blueprint::look_only`, now shared by both: what is typed is dropped, and the Variables… button is off, `claims::variables_button`). Where each change was taken and then refused with a line in the log, nothing is refused now. The start location, the module's, still moves there. [Areas](manual/04-areas.md), [Modules](manual/03-modules.md). Test: `a_read_only_area_is_looked_at_and_its_tools_are_off` (pictures before and after: the chest selected with Shift held, its rings, arrows and handle gone).
* **Fix**: The scale handle follows the pointer while it is dragged (the deferred list's "The scale handle", from builders' reports after 1.10.1): its square is drawn where the pointer is, its stalk from the object's feet to it, and the other selected objects' handles move out or in along their stalks as far (`AreaView::scale_handle_drawn`), where the square stood where it was taken. The entry's other half, a scale for each axis, is left as it is because the game draws it so: it scales a model by `ScaleX` alone, the same every way (in the client, `visual_transforms_look`; [Findings](findings.md), What a visual transform's rotations turn), so a handle for each axis would set what the game doesn't draw; a file's own `ScaleY` and `ScaleZ` are kept and Adjust Location says so (`adjust_location_says_an_uneven_scale_is_kept`). [Areas](manual/04-areas.md). Test: `a_scale_handle_scales_a_placeable_s_model_while_shift_is_held` (the handle's square at the pointer in the picture, seen to fail without the change).

Update Instances and Setup Store read the items held and the store's blueprint as edited, where they read them as last flushed. A command with `input = true` in `moonglow.toml` reads what is typed in a line under its output (Enter or Send, as a terminal; Hide for a password, kept out of the output; End Input closes it); the others keep their input closed, so a question they ask is answered empty at once as before, a team's script that works today not left waiting. And found in doing it: a command canceled left the module's haks closed and its project files unwatched until the module was opened again (a canceled job hands nothing over, and the reopening was in its hand-over); they are taken back when it ends so too (`a_project_s_command_canceled_reads_the_project_s_files_again`). What compiled is kept in the cache folder the views keep theirs in (`ProgramCache::check`; `mg verify` and the window's Verify, `doctor::shader_cache_dir`), so a second Verify compiles only what failed: The Domains of Dread with its haks, six settings, 0.26 s the first run and 0.05 s the next (one setting before: 0.26 s and 0.22 s; a loaded machine), no program failing at any. * **Verification**: The Moonglow Viewer session's other findings against [the model notes](research/notes_models.md) (its PLAN §10.1), confirmed here where the corpus can and written in: part numbers follow file order and a compiled tree does not keep it (5,079 of the 24,298 game models without a supermodel or a skin numbered in another order than their tree's; `decompiled_models_compile_back` now counts them, over the whole install), a mesh's box holds its origin and its average counts a place once (boxes of 99.6% of the game's meshes, radii and averages 94%: `every_binary_model_writes_back_the_same`), and ten `c_wingdrg2_*` wing skins bound in another pose than they rest in (80 of 4,594 bones). What the client and the game's compiler showed (function pointers ignored, the 0x3B0 skin's bones as `i16[64]` with 52 bones, shininess 1 where a text gives none, the game's normals and tangents as `mg-mdl`'s to four decimals) is written in as the viewer's measurement, which this wave could not run again (no client). The one key found missing, Aurora's Alt+X for File › Exit, is Exit's now (`keys::Cmd::Exit`; [the main window](manual/02-main-window.md)'s keys; `closing_the_window_asks_first`, seen to fail without it); Verify Area (none: Verify Module) is marked ✗ there. The 20 tests named in brackets that are not in `crates/mg-ui/tests/app/` (in `mg-ui/src/options.rs`, `mg-render`'s tests and `scene.rs`, and eleven corpus tests) have their file after the name, and the Module Properties, Faction and Journal rows and the blueprints' paragraph that said Moonglow has no OK or Cancel say it has both, as Aurora's (`the_editors_cancel_sets_back_what_they_changed`). Closes the deferred entry.

Closes the deferred entry.

* **Fix**: Loading screen pictures are put together as the `pnl_loadscreen` model the game loads lays the texture out (`images::screen_parts`: each mesh drawn with `gui_replace01`, its texture coordinates' rectangle, left to right by where it stands), so that a hak's own panel, or a texture laid out otherwise (wide, in three parts), shows as the game shows it; the game's own model gives the two halves as before (`STOCK_SCREEN`, checked against the install). A screen whose texture isn't found shows Aurora's small preview (`NAMEs`) where there is one; the full texture, sharper, is used where it is. * **Fix**: Text typed and its field gone before the keyboard left it, what was left (a builder's report, October 2026): a plugin's command and a project's command by their keys wait for a field typed in to set its text, as the window's own commands do (`commands::AfterText`; a plugin's begun with the frame's actions, since egui says a field lost the keyboard in two frames and one drawn after the plugin's edits set what was typed again over them); and a look at the module's files changed outside is taken after such a field has set its text (`Moonglow::take_looked`), so that what was typed is a change of Moonglow's, kept, and the file asked about in Changed Outside Moonglow.
