Tests: `a_plugin_s_command_by_its_key_waits_for_a_field_typed_in`, `a_field_typed_in_sets_its_text_before_files_changed_outside_are_read` (fails without the fix: the description typed went).
* **Fix**: Written more than once, in the editors and wizards (the review of 2026-10-21's entry): a table's rows as a list's choices, none without a game, is one function (`widgets::table_choices`, the game data's kept list and not a copy), which `Form::choices`, Area Properties, the creature, the Creature Wizard, the Levelup Wizard, Select Portrait's filters, the waypoint wizard and the grass page ask; the Variables (N)… button is one (`widgets::variables_button`, with its tip, for a blueprint, an area and the module); an item's and a creature's body parts are numbered and set by the same two (`item::model_numbers`, so the creature's list keeps the game's three digits too, and `item::set_part`); recovery copies and a conversation's backups default to their minutes in one place each (`settings::AUTOSAVE_MINUTES`, `DIALOG_BACKUP_MINUTES`, `Settings::autosave_every`); and the New Module, Area Wizard, Add Faction and Add Popup Text windows' field names are field labels.
* **Fix**: Undo's name for a side editor's step (the deferred list's entry): Edit › Undo and Redo name a hak's and a 2DA's step ("Undo Add files", "Undo Empty 1 cell"; `SideDoc::step_name`, `Moonglow::local_step_name`), a tileset's and the talk table's, which have no names, say plain Undo, and the toolbar's tips name the step as the menu does. In doing it, a gap the entry took for done: the pointer gone from a hak's or a tileset's editor to the Edit menu or the toolbar, their Undo was the module's again, and took the module's step; it is now the editor's the pointer left (`undo_before_bars`, kept while the pointer is on the bars or a menu is open, the frame a menu's entry is chosen included). The tileset's editor has a test of its own for its keys and its menu (`undo_over_a_tileset_editor_is_the_tileset_s`); a text field in one of these editors clicked into and not typed in leaves Undo to the editor's step and takes its own typing back once typed in, the rule of GitHub issue 18 (held in `undo_over_a_hak_editor_is_the_hak_s`, which also takes the hak's step from the Edit menu and the toolbar; `a_rebound_undo_key_reaches_a_table_s_grid` the 2DA's from the menu). A 2DA's "Empty 1 cells" step is "Empty 1 cell". Pictures: `edit-menu-over-a-hak`, before ("Undo A table", the module's) and after. [The main window](manual/02-main-window.md).
* **Fix**: Options › Keyboard › Export… and Import… driven through their dialogs (the deferred list's "Sharing key sets"): the keys chosen go to the file the save dialog names and come back from the one the open dialog names, OK keeps them, and a canceled dialog does nothing (`key_sets_are_exported_and_imported_through_their_dialogs`). The dialogs are their own now (`FileKind::Keys`: titled Export Keys and Import Keys, `.json` files offered), where they were a bare Save As and Open of any file. [Options](manual/10-options.md).
* **Fix**: Finding the game (the deferred list's "Finding the game", builders' reports after 1.14.1): on Windows, Steam installed in a folder of the user's choosing is found through the registry (`reg query` of the user's `SteamPath`, then the machine's `InstallPath`, no console window shown; `reg_value`), not only as `X:\Steam` or `X:\SteamLibrary`; and the game Beamdog's client installed is looked for in the libraries its `settings.json` lists (`"folders"`, as neverwinter.nim's `findNwnRoot` reads it), else in `~/Beamdog Library`, the stable release (`00785`) before the development one (`00829`) (`beamdog_roots`). Held by `beamdog_s_libraries_are_looked_in`, `the_registry_s_steam_folder_is_read` and, by hand, `steam_folder_from_this_machine_s_registry`, run under Wine with a `SteamPath` set (mg-resman's tests pass there; it builds and is clippy-clean for Windows and macOS). The entry is down to a real Windows machine and a Beamdog install. [Getting started](manual/01-getting-started.md) says so.
* **Fix**: userpatch.ini's haks, what was left of them (the deferred list's "userpatch.ini's haks", El Grillo's question). **Measured in the engine**, not only from the wiki: `nwserver` reads the user folder's `userpatch.ini` and names its haks `PATCH:NAME` in `ResManGetAliasFor`, under the module and over `override/`, the higher number over the lower, music (`bmu`) and ambient (`wav`) in them as any resource, as Moonglow's stack has them (`resolution_matches_engine`, with two patch haks; the client shares the engine's resource manager). **`mg --userpatch` and `mg --workshop`** read them and the Workshop, as Options › General's switches have the window do (`userpatch_haks_are_read_when_asked_for`). **Verify warns** of a reference only the player's own content satisfies, the user's `override/` and a Workshop item as well as userpatch.ini's haks (`verify::Local`, `only_local`; `Missing::only_local` in place of `only_userpatch`, and `mg verify --json` has `only_in` beside `only_userpatch`), and **of a custom talk table found first there**: the engine reads a module's table from a userpatch hak or `override/` too (`where_the_game_reads_custom_talk_tables`' new cases), so a player without it can't load the module (`a_custom_talk_table_only_the_player_has`). The user's `development/` is left out of the warning: it is where `mg watch` puts the module's own work. [Options](manual/10-options.md), [the command line](manual/11-command-line.md), [differences](manual/12-differences.md).
* **Fix**: The recovery copy is written at a panic outside a frame too (the deferred list's "The recovery copy written at a panic", Sacha's report): one in a thread of its own (a job, an autosave, a plugin's run, an area's read; the panic hook counts it, `recovery::note_panic`) has the next frame write the copy in the background and say so in the log, and one in eframe's own code has the application write it as the unwinding drops it (`Moonglow::keep_while_unwinding`, from the `moonglow` binary's `Drop for App`; not twice after a frame's). An autosave whose writing panicked no longer stays "being written" for good (no copy made again, a crash waiting five seconds for it). Held by `a_panic_elsewhere_has_the_next_frame_keep_the_work`, `a_panic_unwinding_through_the_application_keeps_the_work` and `an_autosave_that_panics_is_done`. The entry is down to a real crash on Windows and macOS. [Troubleshooting](manual/13-troubleshooting.md) says so.
* **Fix**: Build › Publish to NWSync… publishes as a job (`jobs.rs`), where it had a thread of its own (the deferred list's "NWSync publishing on a thread of its own"): the job's window says how far it is and has **Cancel**, which stops before the next file and writes no manifest, `latest` left as it was (`mg_module::nwsync::write_until`, `CANCELED`), and a panic in the work is said in the log, not a crash. Held by `canceled_and_pruned` and `published_to_nwsync_from_the_build_menu` (now through the job). Pictures: `nwsync-publishing`, `nwsync-published` (`screens.rs` `nwsync_publish_and_prune`). [Build, verify and test](manual/09-build-and-test.md) says so.
* **Fix**: An NWSync repository's unused data is pruned (the deferred list's "No pruning"), as neverwinter.nim's `nwn_nwsync_prune` does (measured: what it removes, at what age, what it only says): **Remove Unused Data** in the Publish window and `mg nwsync-prune REPOSITORY [--min-age S] [--dry-run]` take away the data files no manifest names once older than two weeks (`PRUNE_MIN_AGE`), the folders they leave empty and the `.json` of a manifest taken away, and say what they can't mend (`latest` naming no manifest, a manifest without its `.json`, missing data); a manifest that can't be read stops it before anything goes (`mg_module::nwsync::prune`, `Pruned`). Held by `pruned_as_nwn_nwsync_prune_prunes` (the same files left as the oracle leaves, old and young), `canceled_and_pruned` and the UI test; picture `nwsync-pruned`. The entry is down to the upload, which waits on the owner's word. [Build, verify and test](manual/09-build-and-test.md), [the command line](manual/11-command-line.md).
* **Fix**: A tile's animations are bound to its model's nodes once, as the model is loaded (`mg_render::anim::Binding`, by part number or by name as before, in the area scene's `Loaded`, with the tile's animations and source-light nodes found by name then too), and posed with their keys gathered once for its pose, lights and meshes (`anim::Playing`), not looked up by name, every node's name lower-cased, three times a tile's model a frame. The scene's making, Tyrants of the Moonsea (`render_area`'s new `MG_TIME`, the median of 400; the machine at a load of 26 to 31): area092 0.30 to 0.43 ms, now 0.12 to 0.13; area075 1.65 to 1.72, now 0.41 to 0.60; Elmwood 2.8 to 5.4, now 1.4 to 1.9; the five areas' pictures the same, every pixel. The functions Moonglow Viewer calls (`locals`, `mesh_state`, `pose_worn`, `lights`…) are as they were. Test: `animations_bound_once_pose_and_light_as_bound_each_time`. [The frames note](research/notes_frames.md).
* **Fix**: The first Find References in a module reads its references on a thread of its own (`References::index`, read where they are asked when at most 64 resources changed, `UsageIndex::refresh_at_most`; the persistent world's first read was 0.2 s of a frozen window): the References tab says "Reading the module's references…" meanwhile, and the Rename window counts what names a resource once they are read (its Rename waits for the count, to offer the strings). Tests on a window run without background work read where they ask, as before. Test: `the_first_find_references_reads_the_module_on_a_thread_of_its_own` (200 scripts: the frame that starts the read says so; one changed after is read at once), `the_index_reads_at_most_what_it_is_given`. [Modules](manual/03-modules.md#where-things-are-used-and-renaming). `mg refs` reading the module each time it runs is as a command run once must (nothing is kept between runs): not a defect, closed with the entry.
* **Fix**: The module tree's Delete… says what names what it deletes, left as it is, before you answer: the places that name it (from the module's references, what goes with it and the area list's entry left out), the strings in scripts that spell its name, and for an area the doors and triggers of other areas that lead to its objects by a tag no other area's object has (`UsageIndex::links_into`), a dozen of each and how many more, with **Find References** to the whole list. Those chosen in the tree are deleted together: **Delete…** in the menu of several, and the Delete key on one of them (`Action::DeleteChosenDialog`, `DeleteResources`; one step, "Delete N resources"), the start area and a protected area left out and said. Moonglow keeping the start area (Aurora deletes it and then refuses to save) is kept as the better of the two: closed with the entry. [The main window](manual/02-main-window.md). Pictures: `delete-named-script.png`, `delete-named-area.png`, `delete-several.png` of `delete_says_what_names_it` in `tests/screens.rs`, before (the window said nothing of them, and the menu of several had no Delete…) and after. Tests: `deleting_says_what_names_it_and_takes_those_chosen_together`, `the_transitions_into_an_area_are_those_from_outside_to_its_own_tags`.
* **Fix**: `Workspace::flush` writes back only the documents edited since the last flush (`Workspace::unflushed` counts them), not every document a session has read: with 300 GITs of 200 creatures read, 5.0 ms of writing them out each time became 0.5 µs (19 µs with one edited). A document only read stays as the module has it, byte for byte. Test: `a_flush_writes_every_edit_and_only_what_was_edited` (a field set, an amendment, an item taken out and put in, an undo, a redo, a field taken out from among others: each written by the next flush). [The frames note](research/notes_frames.md).
* **Fix**: Code never used, or never used otherwise, taken out (the code review's entry): `situated::beside_model`'s `model_first` (always the fields first), `Form::palette_color`'s and `palette_pick`'s optional game (always given), `dialog_view::script_with_params`' label, list and salt (one choice: a link's condition or a line's action), `render_minimaps`' size (`tileset_view::MINIMAP_SIZE`, 32), `GameInstall::talk_table`'s `feminine` (always `dialog.tlk`), `widgets::Picker.types`, `LoadedTexture.mtr`, `commands::Id::defaults` and the table behind it (the general key commands' ids are commands' ids: `the_keys_that_work_anywhere_are_all_commands`), `DebugView` (its `MaterialDiffuse` never set: the renderer's `debug` field and the shader's branch with it), `Renderer::color_format`, `Reader::data` and `Reader::pos`, `SetFile::is_empty`; none is used by Moonglow Viewer (v2.8.0, searched). `Txi::alpha_mean` stays: the renderer reads it now (`renderer.rs`, a texture's cut-out). The second half of `ModelView::frame` is left for after the packages that change `model_view.rs`.
* **Fix**: Rows by name on the command line, as the window's fields view has them: `mg gff --names` puts the name of the row a field's number stands for beside it (`"row": "Human"` in the field's object, `"__row"` on each item of a list whose items are rows, a creature's skills), which `mg gff` reads back as without; `mg find --where` and `mg areas --set` take a row's label or name in place of its number (`GameData::row_of`: the label first, then the name, two rows of one name said), `--where` for each type the field is a row of (`query::Query::typed_fields`: `Appearance` is a placeables.2da row for a placeable, doortypes.2da's for a door); `mg areas --names` shows the rows changed by name (`5 (Cave) -> 4 (…)`, `from_name`/`to_name` in the JSON). Numbers and patterns are taken as before. [Command-line tools](manual/11-command-line.md), [The main window](manual/02-main-window.md). Closes the deferred entry.
* **Fix**: `mg tlk-import` makes a table in the language asked for (`--language CODE`), else the JSON file's own `language` (`nwn_tlk`'s), else English, its text in that language's codepage (Polish's Windows-1250: a table it made was English, and a Polish line was refused); a table that is there keeps its language (`--language` another is refused). `mg tlk-import` and `mg tlk-export` take `--module MODULE`: the text written and read by the module's `encoding.2da` (in its haks, through the game install), as the window's talk table editor does (`Table::game`). The JSON being one table's, as `nwn_tlk`'s, is by design, moved to Settled (the feminine table exported on its own; the CSV carries both). Tests: `a_table_made_by_an_import_is_in_its_language`, `a_module_s_encoding_table_is_the_text_of_its_commands` (`--test cli`: a Turkish table's `ğ` through `--module`, `ð` without). [Command-line tools](manual/11-command-line.md). Closes the deferred entry.
* **Fix**: **Build › Round-Trip Check** runs `mg roundtrip`'s check in the window, as a job (cancelled with it): the module as it is there, unsaved changes too, saved to a temporary copy and read back, and each GFF and 2DA written anew; the log names each resource that came back otherwise and each that can't be read as its type says, then how much was compared (`mg_module::roundtrip::check`, which `mg roundtrip` now calls; "1 GFF", not "1 GFFs"). The rest of the entry (GFFs by their fields, no game or Aurora asked) is by design, moved to Settled. [Build, verify and test](manual/09-build-and-test.md#round-trip-check). Closes the deferred entry.
* **Fix**: A premium campaign's talk table (the game's `data/tlk`) or a Workshop item's opens read-only in the talk table editor, as a hak's does: it opened editable, and its Save would have written into the install. Only a table in the user folder (its `tlk`), the module or a nasher project is edited. The editor names where a read-only table is. `premium_campaigns_find_their_talk_tables_in_the_install` opens Tyrants of the Moonsea's in the editor. Found with the entry of a module's table.
* **Fix**: A talk table kept in the module (a `.tlk` among its resources, which the game reads after the haks' and before the `tlk` folder's) is edited in the talk table editor and saved with the module: found as `talk::Source::Module` (not a layer read-only), its Save hands it to the module as a step (`Table::save_with_module`, as a 2DA of the module's is), which the module's Save writes; String Edit's Move to Talk Table takes it too. A hak's table stays read-only, built where the hak is (the editor says so). Tests: `a_talk_table_kept_in_the_module_is_edited_and_saved_with_it` (`--test app`: typed into, the module saved, the game data reads the line, nothing in the `tlk` folder), `a_module_s_table_is_saved_into_it`, `haks_then_the_module_then_the_folder`. [Modules](manual/03-modules.md#the-talk-table-editor). Closes the deferred entry.
* **Fix**: `mg tlk-alloc` gives new keys their lines in the order the names file has them, not of their names: a TOML names file is read with its order kept (`tlk_alloc::toml_in_order`, over `toml_edit`; the `toml` crate's tables are sorted), as a JSON one already was. A key's `feminine` (`{ text = "…", feminine = "…" }`) is its line in the feminine table, and the line is the key's text there otherwise (the feminine table's lines had stayed as they were, a new key's empty); a key with one makes `TABLEf.tlk` beside the table where there is none. A retired key's line keeping its text is by design, moved to Settled (a saved number still shows what it said). Tests: `names_get_lines_that_stay` (`--test cli`: file order, the feminine table made, its line back to the text), `toml_keeps_the_file_s_order`. [Command-line tools](manual/11-command-line.md#strings-by-name). Closes the deferred entry.
* **Fix**: A conversation's CSV export carries every language: a column for each other language (and feminine text) its lines have text in, `text_fr`, `text_de`, `text_pl`, `text_fr_f` (`dialog_io::csv_text_column`), and one for the language edited (Options › General) in the window or each `--language CODE` of `mg dialog-export`, though no line has text in it yet, to be translated into; Import Lines… and `mg dialog-import` read them back (a cell left empty takes that language's text off), each in its own codepage or the module's `encoding.2da` table (`to_csv_in`, `update_from_csv_in`, `Format::write_in`). Twine and Ink stay English, a passage holding one text: said in the manual and by `--language` on them. [Conversations](manual/06-conversations.md#writing-conversations-elsewhere), [Command-line tools](manual/11-command-line.md). Closes the deferred entry.
* **Fix**: The conversation editor's Search pane keeps its Match Case and Match Whole Word as the script editor's Find Text does (`DialogSearch::options`, a `script_tools::SearchOptions`), which Find and Replace All take as they are, not rebuilt from two fields of its own at each. `conversation_search_bookmarks_and_test` ticks Match Case in the pane and finds by it. Closes the deferred entry of the October review.
* **Fix**: A builder code that holds claims in the project's claims table, chosen by a builder it wasn't the code of, is said: in the Builder Code window as it is typed and in the log once set, and by `mg builder-code` (`Claims::code_taken`: "cd holds claims in builders/claims.2da (kz_square): if that is another builder's code, choose your own…"). [Modules](manual/03-modules.md#your-builder-code). Pictures: `claims-builder-code-free.png` and `claims-builder-code-taken.png` of `claims_and_project_commands` in `tests/screens.rs`. Tests: `a_code_that_holds_claims_is_said`, `a_builder_code_that_holds_claims_is_said` (`--test cli`), `a_builder_code_that_holds_claims_is_said_in_its_window`.
* **Fix**: Edit › Claims Table… shows the claims table whole (every area of it and of the module, whose each is and since when, merged or protected, not in the module: `ProjectFile::claims_listing`, which `mg claims` now lists by too), with Claim, Release, Hand To… (another builder's code typed; `Change::Hand`) and Take Over… (asked first; the claim forced, as `mg claims claim --force`). `mg claims PROJECT hand AREA… --to CODE` hands claims on from the command line. [Modules](manual/03-modules.md#claimed-areas), [Command-line tools](manual/11-command-line.md), [The main window](manual/02-main-window.md). Pictures: `claims-table.png`, `claims-table-take-over.png`, `claims-table-hand-to.png` of `claims_and_project_commands`. Tests: `a_claim_is_handed_on_by_its_holder`, `claims_are_listed_claimed_released_and_merged` (`--test cli`), `the_claims_table_window_shows_every_area_and_hands_claims_on`.
* **Fix**: The Areas Not Yours window follows the claims table read again while it waits (a pull, read by the folder's watch with reloading on): its list is made again from the table, the project's `[claims]` and the builder's code as they are now (`claims::SaveAsk.by`), and with no area left the save goes on, said in the log. [Modules](manual/03-modules.md#claimed-areas). Test: `an_area_another_builder_claimed_is_read_only_or_asked_about_before_a_save`.
* **Fix**: A protected area's "anyway" lets through the one change it was asked about, while what it went on with is under way (`claims::Pass`, of the area and the change: Tag, rename or delete): a Rename or Delete window opened by **Rename It Anyway** or **Delete It Anyway** and closed without the change takes the pass back (`claims::end_pass`), so the area's next rename, deletion or change of its Tag is asked about again; a Tag changed while the Rename window is open is asked about too (the pass was the area's, of any change). [Modules](manual/03-modules.md#protected-areas). Test: `a_merged_area_s_tag_and_resref_are_asked_about_before_they_change`.
* **Fix**: A paste's and Setup Store's look at the module's tags parse an area not open only once for each copy of its bytes the module holds (`mg_module::tags::TagCache`, kept by the window: an area's ARE or GIT is read again once it is set again, `Module::shared`; one an editor holds is read from its document, unsaved edits too), not every area of the module at every paste. Test: `the_tag_cache_parses_only_what_changed`.
* **Fix**: A tag the project wants unique in each area (`[tags] unique_in_area`) pasted into another area that has it is said where `paste_warning` is `"any"` ("KZ_STORE_1 is now on two objects"), as any other tag on two objects; with `"custom"` and `"unique"` it is as meant, as before. Setup Store's `{n}` of such a tag is the first number free in the area, not in the module (`mg_module::tags::taken_in`). [Areas](manual/04-areas.md), [Modules](manual/03-modules.md). Tests: `a_tag_unique_in_each_area_is_counted_in_its_own`, `a_store_s_number_is_free_in_its_area_where_the_project_counts_it_there`, `setup_store_follows_the_project_file` (another area's `_STORE_2` left free here).
* **Fix**: Setup Store's list of stores opens scrolled to the store chosen at first (the project's `[store] blueprint`), however far down the list it is, once each time the list is shown (Standard or Custom). Pictures: `setup-store-project-blueprint.png` of `setup_store_project_blueprint_far_down` in `tests/screens.rs`, before (the list's top, the store out of sight) and after (the last store of the game's, chosen, shown). Test: `setup_store_s_list_shows_the_project_s_blueprint_far_down`.
* **Fix**: Add Haks and Talk Table (and `mg attach`) take content as it was downloaded, a `.zip`: its haks and talk table, wherever in it they are, are listed and copied into the user folder's `hak` and `tlk` from the archive (`mg_module::attach`, `Placement::entry`), compared with those there by their bytes as files are; what else it holds stays in it, and a name in it that climbs out of its folder is left out. `a_zip_s_haks_and_talk_table_go_to_the_user_folder` (a scratch user folder). The file chooser offers zips; the window says which archive each comes from. The manual's Custom Content and `mg attach`. The deferred entry is rewritten to 7z and rar, each a new dependency, for the owner.
* **Fix**: The hak editor's View shows a picture as a picture (a TGA or DDS; a PLT in each layer's first color, by the game's palettes), fitted under the list; a sound with Play (and Stop) on the preview channel; a 2DA as its rows and columns, the resource browser's view of a table (`browser::two_da` over `Layered::single`); and a model drawn in the model viewer where the game reads this hak's copy (one of the module's haks, saved as it is). The rest is shown as before (fields, text, a model as its text, the first bytes). `the_hak_editor_shows_pictures_sounds_and_tables`, and `hak_built_from_a_folder_attached_edited_and_reloaded` (a table; the hak's model drawn, then its text once changed and not saved); pictures of a hak's TGA and 2DA viewed (`screens.rs` `hak_editor_and_attach`, `hak-editor-view-*`). The manual's hak editor. The deferred entry is rewritten to what is left: a model of a hak the game doesn't read, drawn (M).
* **Fix**: The hak editor's Update from Folder is one step to undo (`Hak::update_from_folder`): the folder's files replace the hak's of their names, the others are added, and what the hak has and the folder has no file of is taken out and named in the log (a file added by hand, or from another folder), which one Undo puts back; it was a removal of everything and an adding, two steps. Taking out what the folder lacks is what updating from it means, so it stays, said. `update_from_folder_is_one_step_and_names_what_goes`; the hak editor's test checks the one step. The manual's hak editor. Closes the deferred entry.
* **Fix**: Four leftovers of the October code review. The remembered-files cache is named by the module's path's SHA-1 (`Remembered::file`), which no compiler changes, not by Rust's own hasher (a module was built afresh after an upgrade). The `moonglow-hak-<pid>` folders of renamed bytes that a Moonglow that crashed left in the temporary folder are taken away, once a process, where the system says the process is gone (`/proc` on Linux; elsewhere a folder a week untouched: `hak_edit::sweep_stashes`). A write into a hak (`hak_edit::write_into`: a plugin's files, NUI images) keeps the old hak as `.bak` linked, as the module's Save does (`keep_previous`, both), not copied (a hak of gigabytes). A file half written when Moonglow stopped (`name.ext.moonglow-tmp`) beside a module or in its folder, or among a nasher project's sources where its includes take it (`src/**/*`), goes when the module is opened once it is over ten minutes old, rather than lie in the project for version control to offer (`left_writing`). `stashes_a_crashed_moonglow_left_are_swept`, `write_into_keeps_the_hak_it_replaces`, `a_crash_s_half_written_files_go_when_the_module_is_opened`, the remembered file's name pinned in `build.rs`. Closes the deferred entry.
* **Fix**: Export Files writes what whoever mends a model needs (builders' reports after 1.19.1): a blueprint's own file (a placed object's, the blueprint it was placed from) and its row of the table its look is chosen by, as a 2DA of that one row numbered as it is there (`mg_preview::files::look_row`: placeables.2da, genericdoors.2da or doortypes.2da, appearance.2da, baseitems.2da; written as `placeables.2da-row-N.txt`, which no hak takes for the table); a tile's walkmesh (`.wok`) with its model, from the tile's view; and an area's files, from **Export Model Files…** on an area in the module tree (`model_view::export_area_files`, `Gatherer::area`: its `.are` and `.git`, tileset, tiles' models, walkmeshes and minimap pictures, skybox, loading screen, and the models of every creature, door, placeable and item placed in it; `mg haks --usage` gathers an area's tiles through it now). What a model names and the load order has nowhere is said, in the log and in `mg model-files`' note and `missing` (`Gatherer::missing`: the game's own armoire, `plc_a01`, names a cloud texture the game lacks). `mg model-files` takes an area (`chess.are`) and writes the blueprint and its row too. Held by `a_hak_s_model_brings_its_files`, `a_placeable_s_row_is_written_as_a_2da_of_it`, `an_area_brings_its_tileset_and_tiles` (unit), `a_placeable_s_model_files_are_exported`, `an_area_s_model_files_are_exported` (`--test app`) and `every_command_answers_in_json` (`--test cli`). The manual's Export Files and command line. Closes the deferred entry.
* **Fix**: Pictures of long and tall things fill their frame (the Moonglow Viewer session's proposal of 2026-10-07): a gallery's, a hovered row's and a tile's pictures are framed on the model's vertices where it stands in its animation, a skin's where its bones put them, as the picture sees them (`Composed::points_in`, `mg_preview::compose::framed_on`, which the viewer's `OrbitCamera::frame_tight` is), not by a ball around its box; a dragon filled a corner of its picture. The model viewer frames a model as it stands in the animation it plays, not at rest. Pictures before and after (`screens.rs` `gallery_framed`: dragons, banners, statues, rugs, longswords; and a tile's variants, `exact_tiles_and_tile_height`); `a_long_thing_is_framed_tightly`. The form widgets the viewer keeps copies of are exported: `mg_ui::widgets::field_label`, `section_heading`, `table_heading`, `install_fonts`, `SECTION_GAP`, and the PLT color chooser as `palette_color` (with `tones` and `swatch`), which the blueprint editors' chooser now is (`the_form_widgets_and_the_color_chooser_are_exported`, from outside the crate; `colours_are_chosen_from_palette_swatches`). Taking them up is the viewer's, at its next pin. The manual's Gallery. Closes the deferred entry.
* **Verification**: The palette's Gallery keeps its pictures over an edit: a picture is drawn again only where its blueprint now looks otherwise (`model_view.rs` `make_thumbnail`: the look a picture was drawn from, kept beside it, compared with the blueprint's after the edit), and every picture in sight is kept, with 512 more out of sight (`THUMBNAILS_KEPT`), not 192. `a_gallery_s_pictures_are_made_once_and_kept_while_in_sight` (`--test app`, passing): none drawn anew after an edit, those in sight kept however many. Closes the deferred entry.
* **Fix**: The model viewer's wheel is the area view's (`area_view::Wheel`, which both read): Shift or Ctrl with the wheel zooms finer, a pinch on a touchpad zooms (its zoom events; in the area view too, where it had wanted Ctrl held: `a_sideways_scroll_pans_the_area_view` pinches; Ctrl and the wheel is read only while Ctrl is held, as before, so that the zoom egui spreads over the frames after it is let go stays the area view's to ignore), and a sideways scroll moves the model left and right; it had the wheel alone, and with Shift or Ctrl held it did nothing. Its camera is `area_view::Orbit` (`ModelView::orbit`), not four fields of its own. `the_model_viewer_s_wheel_is_the_area_view_s`, seen to fail on the wheel as it was; the manual's model viewer and the area view's keys. Closes the deferred entry.
* **Fix**: An item stood up in its viewer shows its broad side (GitHub issue 7): of the quarter turns about its length, the one that shows the most of it to the viewer, by the area its triangles show (`Composed::seen_area`, every part's: the vertices' normals that a picture's side is chosen by counted a blade's edges as much as its flats), the first unless another shows a tenth more; drawn without a GPU (no model built) it is stood as before, so the GPU test holds the choice, not `helmets_and_shields_preview_as_worn`. A longsword and a dagger show their flats, a battleaxe both blades, a longbow is as it was (`an_item_stood_up_shows_its_broad_side`; pictures before and after, `screens.rs` `item_previews`, which now takes four weapons). The manual's model viewer. Closes the deferred entry.
* **Update**: Two rendering entries read against the client (`client_render.rs`'s readings): Forest - Facelift's cliffs (`reading_facelift_cliffs`, ttf02's tiles 0 to 3, Moonglow's view with a magenta background where nothing is drawn, `MG_MAGENTA=1`): the client shows the sky through them in places where Moonglow draws black meshes, which puts the index lists' entry in doubt (rewritten: Moonglow's picture with the face lists alone is what settles it); a rural shore (`reading_shore`): no blue-white patches in the client or the area view (the see-through entry says so; the reporter's area isn't here).
* **Verification**: `areaGlobalWind` read back from the client (`program_choice_in_the_client`'s band with the new `MG_WIND`): (0, 0, 0) at `WindPower` 0, (0.707, 0.707, 0) at 1, (1.414, 1.414, 0) at 2, as Moonglow's `wind` (`mg-render`'s `scene.rs`) gives (TDoD's comments were right). Closes the M2 entry's wind item. The same probe, with `MG_LYING_ENVD` (new), read a texture's `envmaptexture default` on a placeable whose `Reflection` is `default`: the client reflects the area's map, not `chrome1` (its entry rewritten to the fix), and the cards as a static placeable against a dynamic one (`MG_STATIC=1`): three differ, by bucket (the M4 item rewritten to what was seen; tiles not read).
* **Update**: The DM client's view of a custom palette's names (the palettes entry) waits on a harness that starts `nwserver` and the client in one sandbox, as the NWSync download test does: the sandbox's own loopback works (`bwrap --unshare-net` brings `lo` up), so the harness can be built (M, the download entry). The feminine talk table's entry waits on that test.
* **Fix**: The area view lights an area by the sun's and the moon's directions its GIT gives (`AreaProperties` `SunDirectionX/Y/Z`, `MoonDirectionX/Y/Z`), normalized, the game's own where it gives none (`mg_area::Lighting::read_with`, `the_sun_and_moon_take_the_git_s_directions`), and casts its shadows by them: the client does, read back (`reading_git_sun_uniform` in `client_render.rs`'s readings: its `lightAreaDiffuseDirection` through the debug `inc_standard`, turned back to the world, is the GIT's vector normalized, (−0.970, 0, 0.243) for (−1, 0, 0.25), and the default (0.433, 0.487, 0.758) without) and seen (`reading_git_sun_direction`: a city street at noon, a north sun's long shadows falling south alike in the client and the area view). A low sun's long shadows differ (the client's fade), added to the Shadows entry's fade item. Closes the entry.
* **Update**: A grass tuft's shape in the client (`reading_grass_tufts` in `client_render.rs`'s readings: white opaque tufts of an area override, 0.3 a square meter, beside Moonglow's area view, and the stock grass from close, low and from above): one upright quad turned at random, as tall as wide, as Moonglow's. The client's sway in the wind (`reading_grass_sway`: the tufts' tops moved between two pictures at `WindPower` 2, their feet still) and Moonglow's don't: the entry is rewritten to the sway (M: the game's `vsgrass` reads no wind, so the engine moves the vertices). The pictures also show the client's grass half see-through and ending some 15 to 20 m out, added to the M5 grass item.
* **Fix**: A cloak of a base item of a module's own lies in an area as that base item's `DefaultModel` (then the bag), the game's model of a cloak dropped (`gi_cloak01`) being the game's cloaks' (base item 80) alone (`mg_preview::item_placed`, `a_cloak_of_a_base_item_of_its_own_lies_as_its_model`): seen in the client (`reading_custom_cloak_base_item` in `client_render.rs`'s readings: two copies of the cloak's baseitems.2da row, one naming a torch and one the bag, lay as a torch and a bag, the game's cloak folded beside them). Moonglow drew all three folded. And the dropped cloak's shine, compared with the client (`reading_dropped_cloak_shine`: colors 0, 56 to 59 and 61): the same colors and solid shine with both of Moonglow's shaders, the client's highlights a little sharper. Closes the entry.
* **Verification**: A placeable whose model lacks its state's animation, in the client (`reading_placeable_without_its_state_s_animation` in `client_render.rs`'s readings, beside Moonglow's area view; CEP's `plc_orc2` stood in for by the goblin's model, renamed and made a placeable appearance, on its creature supermodel as CEP's corpses are): drawn in its rest pose in both; and a goblin whose idle `AnimationReplace` renames to one its model lacks stands in its rest pose in both, beside one in its idle. CEP's own file was not looked at (its hak isn't on this machine). Closes the entry.
* **Verification**: Tiles far below and above the ground in the client (`reading_tile_heights` in `client_render.rs`'s readings, each beside Moonglow's area view from the same camera): a rural area with tiles at heights −2 and 20 (−10 m and 100 m) and an armoire on each; the player entering on the high one sees it and its armoire alone in the sky, on the low one its ground, as the area view draws them; from the ground both are gaps, as in the area view. (A player entering at the corner of a high tile is stood on it by the server.) The entry's statements are how Tile Height works: moved to Settled.
* **Fix**: An armor's inventory icon draws each part's piece (pelvis, chest, belt, shoulders, robe) in that part's own colors where it has them, else the armor's (`an_armor_s_icon_takes_its_parts_own_colors`): the game's inventory does, seen in the client (`reading_inventory_icons` in `client_render.rs`'s readings: two performer's outfits of colors 0 in the player's bag, the one whose torso has colors of its own (44) drawn with its chest in them, the rest alike). Moonglow drew the whole icon in the armor's colors. The manual's Part Colors paragraph says so. Closes the entry.
* **Verification**: Grass on a placeable's walkmesh, in the client (`reading_grass_on_placeables` in `client_render.rs`'s readings, beside Moonglow's area view): two 4 m slabs 2 m high on a rural field with Grass Detail High, their walkmesh's tops grass (material 3), one static and one not: the client grows no grass on either, only on the tiles around (and the server stood a player entering over the static one on the ground inside it: a placeable's walkmesh is no ground). Moonglow grows it on the tiles' walkmeshes alone, as the client does. Closes the entry.
* **Verification**: An exact tile's seams in the client (`reading_exact_tile_seams`): ttr01's all-water tile put among grass draws as each tile's model, the gap between them open to the sky, as Moonglow's area view draws it. The entry's other statements are how Exact tile works by design (its tip and the manual): moved to Settled.
* **Fix**: A tile's replacement texture (`Tile_ReplaceTex`) is drawn in the area view as the client draws it, read with the one model that has a `replace_tex` texture (`tms01_c08_01`, made every tile of an area, its texture and `replacetexture.2da`'s rows given colors of their own: `reading_tile_replacement_texture` in `client_render.rs`'s readings): the value is the row after the one drawn (1 draws row 0, 2 row 1), a tile without the field draws row 0, and 0 or a value past the table (3, 255) crashes the client as it loads the area, in `CNWCTile::SetReplaceTexture` (its crash report). Moonglow drew row 0 for 0 and nothing for a tile without the field (`mg_area::replacement_row`, `tiles_take_their_replacement_texture`). The entry is rewritten to what is left: the field back in Tile Properties by that rule.
* **Fix**: A creature's own Inventory page shows its armor with the icons of its Gender (a woman's `ipf_` parts), whatever Options › General's Show armor and cloaks on a woman says (`a_creature_s_inventory_shows_the_icons_of_its_gender`): the game does so, seen in the client (`reading_inventory_icons` in `client_render.rs`'s readings: the same armor in the player's inventory drawn with `ipm_` icons, the player made a man by `SetGender`, and with the woman's, made a woman). The manual's Options chapter says so. Closes the entry.
* **Verification**: A custom talk table kept in the module alone (inside the .mod, named by `Mod_CustomTlk`, none in the user directory's `tlk`) is the client's too, in single player (`reading_custom_talk_table_in_the_module` in `client_render.rs`'s readings): a StrRef floated over the player (`FloatingTextStrRefOnCreature`, which the client looks up itself) showed the table's text, as did the server's lookup sent as text. A player joining a server never has the module, so there the table goes in the `tlk` folder, a hak or NWSync, as the game asks. Closes the entry.
* **Verification**: Test Module's Choose Character, run in the client (`reading_choose_character` in `client_render.rs`'s readings, `target/test-output/client_reading_choose_character/`): `+LoadNewModule MgScene`, as `test_module::command` builds it, opens the module's character selection (the local vault's and the pregenerated characters, New Character, Play), and Play enters the module's area (its entry script ran). Closes the entry.
* **Verification**: A placeable's Illuminated and its flame, in the client (`reading_illuminated_brazier` in `client_render.rs`'s readings, its picture in `target/test-output/client_reading_illuminated/`): two braziers (placeables.2da row 57) in a dark room, one Illuminated and one not, both burn and smoke alike: the flame is the model's animation state, not the light, as Moonglow draws it (the light alone left out). Aurora neither shows nor writes the field: its executable's data names `AnimationState`, `Static`, `Useable` and `BodyBag` but no `LightState`, and an area it saved (the Aurora batch's `wave1/area-saved.mod` against `wave1/before.mod`) has none (a field it doesn't know it drops, nwn-issues 528). Closes the entry.
* **Update**: The client's `worldtimerTimeOfDay`, read back in the first shot of 21 modules (`time_uniforms_in_the_client`, with `MG_TIME_CASES` and dawn hours): a count taken within the module's day (24 × its minutes an hour × 60,000 ms), whatever its start hour, day or dawn, which the test now holds it to; the count itself was about 17,697,400 ms as the player entered the area in 16 runs until after 12:09 (424,400 seven seconds in at 1 to 4 and 6 minutes an hour, 3,304,400 at 5 and 10, 6,184,400 at 8, the count from 15 up), and two hours more by 13:11. A rule that took the count for fixed was made and taken back the same day (never merged); the M1 item is rewritten to what moves it. And the test takes eight shots two seconds apart and needs three distinct frames, so that a frame the window shows again no longer fails it (the M4 entry's flaky-test item closed) ([the shader notes](research/notes_shaders.md) §B.4).
* **Fix**: The frame test keeps the budget (`crates/mg-ui/tests/frame_perf.rs`, run by hand): each of its five tests fails where a view's median frame is over the 6.94 ms budget by more than a quarter (`MARGIN`, for the times' movement from run to run), and names, without failing, the views the deferred list holds as over it (`KNOWN_OVER`: the store's blueprint picker searched over 8,000 items, TDoD's woods seen whole); the reload test's longest frame is held to it the same way. Seen to pass (`FRAME_VIEW="Area Properties"`, 0.68 ms) and to fail with the budget set to 0.5 ms for the run. A run of the whole on a quiet machine is the owner's: this one had other builds running.
* **Update**: Two entries of [the deferred list](deferred.md) said to wait: Moonglow Viewer's shader cache (the viewer, on v2.8.0, names it by `mg-shader`'s own version, and `mg_core::release` first exists in v2.9.0, so the viewer's one line goes in with its next toolset tag, in its repository) and the client tests' scripted cameras (the numbers taken from them are measured again only in a session that runs the client).
* **Update**: The game's models are decompiled once for the corpus tests: `decompiled_models_compile_back` compiles each text in the decompiler's sweep (`mdl_decompile::swept`, with `mdl_lint`'s), which keeps only what it finds (`mdl_compile::CompiledBack`), no texts. Over the whole install (`MOONGLOW_FULL_CORPUS=1 --test models`, 25,227 models compiled back, every one as it was) the group's processor time went from 5 min 14 s and 5 min 27 s to 4 min 41 s and 4 min 45 s (user, alternated on a loaded machine). What is left of the entry, the five area sweeps that share the module's set-up and not the walk, is by design and waits on the owner's word.
* **Fix**: The command line's tests start `mg` with what they add from the group's shared helpers (`tests/groups/cli.rs`): `mg_on(root, user)` (`--root` and `--user-dir`, or `--no-user-dir`), `mg_alone()` (`--no-user-dir`), `mg_json` and `mg_output`, in place of a wrapper in each file and the arguments written out at some sixty calls (`--test cli`, all passing); the plugin tests' data folder of their own is built over `mg_alone`, and the language server's test (`lsp.rs`, a user folder and no root, piped) stays as it is. The GPU tests' "hold a place, open a device, else say it is skipped" is `mg_testkit::gpu::open` (`Gpu::headless` given), which the corpus tests' `gpu()` (`game_shaders.rs`, and `hlsl.rs`'s three) and mg-render's tests' (`crates/mg-render/tests/render.rs`, a test file alone) call instead of each its own.
* **Fix**: The journal editor's lower half (a quest's or an entry's fields) is as tall as it is dragged to, up to seven tenths of the editor: as tall as what it showed, it couldn't be dragged taller than the fields chosen, its default height was not kept, and choosing Root took it back to one line (seen in pictures, `screens.rs` `journal_lower_half_kept`, before and after; the conversation editor's had been fixed so before). The limits on the script, conversation and journal editors' lower halves are under test (`the_editors_lower_halves_are_held_to_a_share_of_the_editor`: each dragged up as far as it goes, three fifths of the script editor, seven tenths of the others), as the talk table's were; the manual's journal section says so.
* **Update**: A file of `crates/mg-ui/tests/app/` that its `main.rs` doesn't name as a module fails `every_test_file_is_a_module` (`mg_testkit::unlisted_modules`, with a test of its own): such a file is not compiled, and its tests passed by not running. The set-ups the same entry lists stay open, now sized M: they are in 17 of the folder's files, which the sprint's other packages are changing.
* **Fix**: The corpus tests put a module over the game as mg and the window do, with `Module::stack_onto` (its haks, the user's or the install's, then the module), not by hand: `modules`, `doctor`, `nwsync`, `engine_verify`, `engine_transfer`, the area and script sweeps' set-ups, and the `verify_summary` and `render_area` examples. `engine_resman`'s comparison with the server's `ResManGetAliasFor` now stacks its probe module that way too (the module's hak list read from its `module.ifo`), so the engine checks the order mg itself builds. `client_render` adds a builder's haks with no module of theirs and stays as it is.
* **Verification**: Pictures that differ from run to run, looked for (`render_area` twice in two processes over every area of three campaigns): with Moonglow's own shader none differs (Tyrants of the Moonsea's 140 areas, Prelude's and Chapter 1's 118); with the game's shaders 8 of Tyrants' 140 do, by a few scattered pixels, while the same scene drawn again in one process, the scene's lights and every pipeline's modules are the same. The deferred entry is rewritten to that: the cause is in the game shaders' draw path, set once a process.
* **Fix**: The start location in the area view takes what an object does. The palette has **🏁 Start Location** among its types (Aurora's Paint Start Location): chosen, its marker follows the pointer over a view, and a click puts the module's start location there, in that area (which is how it goes to another area; a drag of the marker keeps to its own, as an object's does), facing the way the view looks; Shift + click keeps it chosen, a right click or Escape puts it down (`the_palette_s_start_location_is_placed_with_a_click`, its picture). Selected, Q and E turn it, a right click on it selects it, and its menu's Adjust Location sets its X, Y, Z and bearing (`area_tools::adjust_start`, `the_selected_start_location_takes_the_keys_and_adjust_location`); the pointer is a hand over its ring, arrow and tip, closed while it is held. The Shift axis arrows stay the objects'; the marker lies on the ground, where the game puts a player, and its drag and Adjust Location place it exactly. The manual's toolbar section. Closes the deferred entry.
* **Fix**: Area Properties › Grass's **Add Grass to…** lists the materials the area's tiles have first, the most of the ground first (each one's square meters of the tiles' walkmesh on hover: `mg_area::grass::materials_of`, read once a revision), then the others, dimmed; grass on a material no tile has says that nothing grows there; the tileset's grass texture and each override's have their picture beside the name, on a light ground (`grass_is_set_in_area_properties_and_grown_in_the_view`: ttr01's field has grass, stone and nonwalk, not dirt or lava; its pictures `add-grass-to.png`, `own-grass.png`). Any texture is still taken as four tufts, two by two, as the game takes it. The manual's Grass page. Closes the deferred entry.
* **Fix**: Grass's edge where the area view draws fewer tufts: dense grass (more than 10 tufts a square meter) lost two thirds of them at once at 45 m, and the tilesets' half at 180 m, at a tile's edge, a band seen as the camera moved (a picture of what the grass changes in the view, before and after: `grass_thinned_with_distance_looks`, ignored, a look). Grass now thins over a stretch from each level's distance on, a quarter of it, in eight steps (`mg_area::grass::FADE`, `level`, `share`; each tile model's grass uploaded once and drawn at 25 levels sharing its buffers), as dense as before nearer than the level (`grass_covers_the_whole_view` held to it), `grass_thins_over_a_stretch_from_each_level`. Drawing grass wherever the view shows it, past the client's 30 m, is the area view's on purpose. The manual's toolbar and Differences say so (the latter still said within 30 m of where the view looks). Closes the deferred entry.
* **Fix**: The tiles' emitters given particles are the 128 nearest the camera, as the objects' 256 are (`scene.rs` `nearest`, `the_emitters_nearest_the_camera_get_particles`): they were the first 128 tiles listed, the south-west corner's, wherever the camera was. A tile's emitters new to the view were run ahead as an object's are already (`scene.rs`, "Run ahead as an object's"). The deferred entry is rewritten to what waits on the client: the fog's density and Magic Sparks from above.
* **Fix**: A click on a creature in an area is tried against its model as it stands, where its box is (builders' reports after 1.19.1: a dragon at rest lies stretched out, wings spread, far out of the box around its pause): the area's scene keeps each model's stance in its pause, at its start (`Composed::stance`), and boxes it and meets a click on it alike (`Composed::ray_hit_in`; `ray_hit` at rest stays for the views that frame a model at rest). `a_dragon_s_box_is_around_its_pause_not_its_rest` now casts a grid of rays down over the red dragon: every hit of it as it stands is in its box, and at rest more than twenty lie outside it. The box and the click stay with the pause's start while the animation plays, as the selection's box is steady. Closes the deferred entry.
* **Fix**: Placing onto a placeable as it is placed (builders' reports on 1.31.0): the area view's toolbar has **Onto Objects** (beside Together, kept in the settings, off by default so that a drop in a forest stays out of the canopies): on, the see-through copy of the palette's blueprint lies on the top of the placeable or door the pointer is on, by its model, and a click or a drop places it there (`AreaView::put_down_at`); a top more than 2 m above the ground under it is passed for the ground, as Drop onto Object's reach. Closes the deferred entry.
* **Fix**: What follows the pointer over the area view stops while the pointer is over a window in front of it: the coordinates in the view's corner and the outline of tiles about to be pasted showed the ground under the window (seen in a picture first: `what_follows_the_pointer_stops_under_a_window`, its pictures before and after). "Over the view, not under a window" is one test now (`AreaView::is_over`, `pointer_over`), taken by the brush's see-through copy, Paint, the paste outlines and the coordinates. Closes the deferred entry.
* **Fix**: Escape or a right click to put down what is in hand in the area view is one test (`area_view::cancelled`, Escape alone `escaped`), taken by Paint, the tile paste, the object paste, an outline redrawn and the brush; Escape during a Ctrl + wheel scale and Escape that clears the selection take it too, so Escape over a window in front of the view, or in a field elsewhere, is not the view's (the wheel's took it from anywhere). Closes the deferred entry.

Moonglow Viewer builds on the toolset's `v2.8.0` (`moonglow-viewer/Cargo.toml`), past every field M1 to M5 added: the four "at its next bump" items are done. Moonglow writes `CurrentHitPoints` as the base and the feats' hit points (`CreatureStats::current_hit_points`): the Creature Wizard, and every change to a creature that has the feats (with the maximum, `refresh_hit_points`; a creature below its base is left wounded, and one without the feats as it is: a stock creature, `tm_cr_dragblksml`, has more than its base without them). * **Verification**: Which resource names the game takes, in the server (`engine_resource_names.rs`): any of up to 16 bytes, of either case (found by either, listed in lower case), with `-`, `.`, a space or a byte past ASCII, each found by `ResManGetAliasFor` and run by `ExecuteScript`. The deferred entry of the three rules keeps the one rule to choose.
* **Fix**: Test Module and Test From Here know a game Moonglow did not start (GitHub issue 6: one started otherwise still had the test module written under it). Before a test writes its module (Test Module before its save, which writes it, a nasher project's before its packed module is copied, Test From Here before its copy), the game clients running are looked for by their program's name (`test_module::running`: `/proc` on Linux, `ps` on macOS, `tasklist` on Windows; read only, a game is never stopped), less those Moonglow started for a test (known by process id now, not a count). One started on the module the test writes (`+TestNewModule`/`+LoadNewModule` and its name in its command line) stops the test with a warning to close it, as one Moonglow started does (now also before the save); any other is named in a warning and the test goes on (on Windows, where another program's command line isn't listed, every game running is a warning). [Build and test](manual/09-build-and-test.md). Tests: `a_running_program_is_found_by_its_name`, `a_line_of_ps_is_read`, `a_game_moonglow_did_not_start_is_known_before_a_test` (stand-ins named as the client; the game client itself is not run).
* **Verification**: [The deferred list](deferred.md)'s Indentation by file entry's other sentences are the rule as built, now in [Scripts](manual/07-scripts.md): the guess is made when a script's text is read (`ScriptBuffer::read` and `follow`, `script_view.rs:157` and `:186`), not as it is typed and not kept between sessions; Enter copies the line's own leading spaces and tabs (`script_tools::new_line_indent`, `a_new_line_in_a_script_is_as_far_in_as_the_last`); the Edit menu's conversion is the script in front's, else the one last typed in (`commands::script_in_front`). The entry is closed.

Convert Indentation to Tabs and to Spaces convert only the lines a selection touches when text is selected (`script_tools::convert_indentation_in`; from the Edit menu, only where the editor had the keyboard as the menu opened, so a selection left from before and not shown is not taken), and a right click in the script editor keeps the text selected for its menu, where the field had moved the cursor to the click (pictures: `script-selection-menu-before.png` and `-after.png` of `script_selection_menu` in `tests/screens.rs`). The entry is closed.
* **Fix**: To Scratch and Copy to Scratch Folder remove from the scratch folder every compiled script and debug file of the scripts copied that the copy did not write, whatever its spelling ([Scripts](manual/07-scripts.md); El Grillo's request): a `HELLO.NCS` another program wrote, on a file system that tells cases apart, went on standing beside the copy, and the debug file of an older compile stayed beside a script compiled now without debug information. To Scratch copies a debug file only when its compile made one (not the module's from an older compile). The same file under another spelling is told apart by the file system's identity (Linux, macOS) or the name, case aside (Windows). Tests: `a_script_goes_to_the_scratch_folder_compiled`, `a_stale_compiled_script_is_compiled_before_it_goes_out`.
* **Fix**: The lines Moonglow shows of a module's scripts are read in the scripts' codepage, not as UTF-8 (GitHub issue 27: a Polish letter showed as "�"): Find References' and Used By's script strings in Options › Language's ([Scripts](manual/07-scripts.md)), read again when the language changes; `mg refs`, `mg rename`'s "still spells" warnings and `mg rename` of a NUI window in the game client's, as `mg lsp` reads scripts (the user folder's `settings.tml`: Windows-1250 for a Polish game), or the module's own `encoding.2da` table ([Command-line tools](manual/11-command-line.md)). `mg_module::rename::mentions` takes the codepage; the scripts' bytes are never changed. [The deferred list](deferred.md)'s entry is left down to a per-script codepage (nobody asked; the owner's word on dropping it) and the Polish client check. Tests: `a_mention_s_line_is_read_in_the_scripts_codepage`, `mg_refs_reads_a_script_s_line_in_the_game_s_codepage`, `references_show_a_script_s_line_in_the_scripts_codepage`.
* **Fix**: Compile and the check as you type read a script's includes alike, as the module has them ([Scripts](manual/07-scripts.md)): the check read an include's open editor first, so an include with unsaved changes passed one and failed the other. One reader, `script_view::sources` (the script compiled as its editor has it, the others the module's, else the game's), for Compile, the check and the wizards, as the build reads them already; not the editors' text, since the compiled script goes into the module beside the sources it is compiled again from (the build, an export, the test loop) and Aurora reads an include saved. Compile says when it left an open include's unsaved changes out (`uses_n.nss: inc_n read as saved, not with the changes in its editor (save to compile with them)`). Test: `compile_and_the_live_check_read_an_include_as_saved`.

A new script (New Script, Save As) in a project or module folder has no file until File › Save writes it, so it opens in Moonglow's editor with a copy in the external one, as a script with unsaved text does (`open_only_external` compares the file with the module's bytes).
