Its Add to Palette, captured the same day, gave `w1_held002`, `w1_arrow002` and `mgp_utc002` for templates without a number, where Moonglow's `new_resref` (and the earlier capture's `mgp_utp001`) give 001: filed in the entry.
* **Fix**: A sound's random range is drawn as Aurora draws it (deferred list): a rectangle its `RandomRangeX` wide and `RandomRangeY` deep about the sound (half each way; Aurora's of 6 and 2 measured 6.2 by 2.1 m), where Moonglow's was twice that (`a_sound_s_random_place_has_a_reach`).
* **Update**: [The deferred sprint](deferred-sprint.md), wave 1's Aurora checks: 28 entries of [the deferred list](deferred.md) checked in Aurora on the oracle (two sessions, the probes and steps of `tools/aurora/capture_wave1.py`, captures in `captures/wave1/`), 16 closed (4 verified, 9 fixed, 3 settled) and 12 rewritten to what was found, none added; `tools/aurora/xdrive.py` drags, holds the button, turns the wheel and raises a window.
* **Update**: `mg watch PROJECT --serve` takes `--player-password`, `--dm-password` and `--admin-password`, as `mg serve` does (the TDoD module team: a builder couldn't join the watched server as a DM). The three are one set of options of both commands (`serve::Passwords`, flattened into each); one not given is a random one, and the player and DM passwords are printed at the start (a `passwords` event with `--json`), with `mg serve`'s own events. The server, started again when a 2DA, an area or `module.ifo` changes, keeps them. `mg watch`'s server options (`--dir`, `--port`, `--env`, `--preload`, the arguments after `--` and the three passwords) are now refused without `--serve`; before, they were ignored without it. Tests in `--test cli watch`: `the_server_s_options_want_serve`, and `a_project_is_served_and_started_again_when_it_is_built_again` now gives a DM password and checks the passwords printed, the server's own arguments (`/proc/PID/cmdline`) and that a restart keeps them. Manual: the command-line chapter.
* **Fix**: a module's haks are layered into the game data again (a builder's report on 2.13.1, Windows: every module opened said its haks "could not be added to the game data (it is in use)", closed and opened again or not). The 3D views kept the game data their textures are read apart from (`Viewport3d::textures_from`, since 2.10.0) for good, in the viewport's cache, the renderer's texture reader and its threads; since 2.13.1 the lit check filled that cache at startup, so the first module failed, and before it every module opened after an area had been drawn did. Whatever needs the game data to itself had silently done nothing meanwhile: Close left the old module's haks layered, edits weren't layered into the game data (`refresh_module_layer`, `update_module_layer`), nor Module Properties' hak list, the custom talk table, Reload Resources and its watch, a tileset's minimap pictures. Now each of them first has the views let go of it (`Moonglow::release_game_data`: the viewport's cache and the renderer's readers, taken again when an area next draws; and, ten seconds at most, what still reads it on a thread, such as an area of a module just closed; never a job) before `exclusive`; the content watch asks whether anything else holds it (`game_data_held`) and has the views let go only when something changed. The lit check reads the game's 92 programs (`.shd`) out of the game data first (0.3 ms) and holds none of it while it runs, so a module opened in the first frame (`moonglow path/to/module.mod`) layers its haks too. Tests in `--test app resources`: `a_module_s_haks_are_layered_with_the_lit_check`, `…_after_an_area_was_drawn` (Reload Resources with textures still being read, which are asked for again and drawn, then Close) and `…_after_one_closed_while_its_area_was_read`, failing before (the first with the builder's message; the other two also without the wait).
* **Update**: The lit check's notice says what it found: where the game's shaders draw its red test mesh in the wrong colors (Heed's Vulkan on a Radeon RX 580, 2.13.1: [212, 133, 133], washed-out pink), it says so, that Moonglow's own draw it right and that Moonglow draws with its own for the rest of the run, not that they "draw nothing right" (the area was drawn); where they draw nothing of it, it says that; Moonglow's own shader's notice likewise. The pink is exactly the stock `fslit` with `fNdotV` zero (the surface lit as if seen edge on: the environment specularity's Fresnel term at a grazing angle, 0.29 for 0.04, the diffuse unchanged); no uniform, texture, buffer, vertex input or uninitialized value of Moonglow's differs by driver (each written or zeroed, checked by filling them with other values and by forcing the program's terms here). What does change the program is the load order the check read it through: `override/`, `development/`, Workshop items and the module's haks, where a builder's `inc_lighting` draws so. The check now draws the game's own copies (`Assets::game_shader`, the keys'; `shader_origin` says where the load order's copy is), its debug-log line names any copy the load order puts over them and the color each shader drew, and `probe::Lit` keeps the two pictures. Tests: `a_shader_s_game_copy_and_where_the_load_order_s_comes_from` (mg-render), the lit-check render test's colors, `a_builder_s_include_leaves_the_lit_check_to_the_game_s_own` (corpus: the pink drawn by an overriding include, red by the check), `the_lit_check_turns_the_game_s_shaders_off_where_they_draw_wrong` (mg-ui: Heed's colors, the notice's words). [Findings](findings.md) (The lit check's pink on Heed's Vulkan), [Troubleshooting](manual/13-troubleshooting.md), [the deferred list](deferred.md): Heed's interiors' lighting entry says what the next release's log line will tell.
* **Update**: A model whose textures are read apart (the area view where jobs run in the background, as in the desktop app) asks for every mesh's textures in its first frame: the renderer stopped at the first mesh still waiting, so the next mesh's were asked for only once that one's were read, a round of reads a mesh, and a tile of several textured meshes stayed out of the view (black) that many rounds. The rural tileset's tile with a rock took six (`ttr01_stone06` … `ttr01_dirt04`, about 110 ms here), and the lit check's picture (`renderer_check::the_lit_check_turns_the_game_s_shaders_off_where_they_draw_nothing`) was taken in the middle of them: one tile black. Nothing of the game's shaders: the tile was left out before the switch to Moonglow's own as after it (118 meshes drawn on both sides, 142 once read), and the picture was black from the test's first commit (3680a48f), drawn or not as the lit check and the reads raced. A new render test, `a_model_s_meshes_ask_for_their_textures_together`, holds a three-mesh model to the frame after one round; the lit check's test waits for the textures and the game's programs, then asserts every mesh still drawn after the switch and each of the 16 tiles lit at its middle in the picture.
* **Fix**: A list's click to select several is one rule (the deferred sprint, [the deferred list](deferred.md)'s "written more than once"): the hak editor's list and Module Properties' haks call `widgets::select_in_list`, as Open Script and the module tree do. The module's haks chose the common rule: Ctrl+Shift + click adds the rows from the one clicked last (it was a Ctrl + click alone there). Tests: `widgets::select_tests::a_click_with_ctrl_and_shift_selects_as_lists_do`, and `the_hak_list_is_reordered_by_keys_buttons_and_a_drag` (`--test app resources`) with a Ctrl+Shift + click.
* **Fix**: Every list's search is one field (the deferred sprint, "Filters: one matcher and one search field, what is left"): the Creature Wizard's appearances, the Script Wizard's feats, the Store Wizard's stores, the Area chooser, Open 2DA, Open Script, the script editor's side lists, the Command Palette, a long dropdown's Filter and the ResRef picker are `widgets::SearchField`s now (a hint that says what it looks in, ×, Escape; Find's keys with the pointer over the window, except the script editor's side lists, whose Ctrl+F is the script's). A dialog stays open while Escape clears what was typed in its search field and closes at the next (`escape_on`; Open Script closed at once before), as the Command Palette and a dropdown's list do; a Properties window keeps its rule (clear, leave, then Cancel). The field and its × fill the room together (the button measured). What each list looks in and where Down leaves the field, the choices the entry listed, are in [the main window](manual/02-main-window.md)'s chapter, with the Escape rule. Tests in `--test app`: `open_script_s_escape_clears_the_name_then_closes`, `a_dropdown_s_filter_field_can_be_typed_in` (Escape twice), `the_command_palette_finds_a_command_and_runs_it` (Escape clears, then closes), `an_open_dropdown_keeps_its_width` (with the ×).
* **Fix**: Arrows after the last numbers that have them in Aurora (the deferred sprint, "Arrows after numbers, what is left"): the Creature and Levelup Wizards' class levels, the Script Wizard's conditions (ability scores, class level, the random chance), the Area Wizard's and Resize Area's size, Adjust Location's position, bearing, scale, rotations and translations (a tenth of a meter or of the scale, a degree: Moonglow's steps), and Options' spawn point marker size and its recovery copy and conversation backup intervals, through `widgets::spin_draft` (`spin_with` on a dialog's own copy). Options has no undo levels field to give them. The repeat's pause and rate stay Moonglow's (0.4 s, then 20 a second). Test: `adjust_location_and_resize_area_have_arrows` (`--test app area_view`). [Blueprints](manual/05-blueprints.md) lists them.
* **Fix**: Arrow keys on the last lists that took none (the deferred sprint, "Arrow keys on lists of choices", GitHub issue 7): the lists of commands (Module Properties' Add hak…, a trigger's Setup Area Transition…) and the 2DA editor's list of flags go through `Dropdown::show_menu`: once the box is clicked or reached with Tab, Down opens the list, Up and Down (Home, End) move a cursor along its rows, and Enter or Space does what the row's click does (a command closes the list, a tick leaves it open). A described variable's ▾ of its values is a dropdown of choices like the others. A list whose options are read only while it is used (Module Properties' movies and talk tables, the NUI editor's targets and binds) no longer reads them every frame it has the keys: only when an arrow key is pressed (`Dropdown::show_with`). Tests: `add_hak_takes_the_arrow_keys` (`--test app resources`), `a_flags_list_is_ticked_by_the_keys` (`--test app tables`). [Blueprints](manual/05-blueprints.md) says so.
* **Verification**: Undo in a text field (the deferred sprint, "Undo in a text field", GitHub issue 18) is the field's in every kind of editor, as the one rule in `commands::keys_pressed` says (every Undo and Redo, the module's and a side document's, goes through `Id::Undo`): tried one by one in tests, the script editor (`the_keys_the_menu_s_and_the_toolbar_s_undo_in_a_script_are_the_script_s`, already there), a conversation line's text (`undo_in_a_conversation_line_s_text_is_the_text_s`: the delete before it stays, and once the field is left Ctrl+Z is the module's), a 2DA cell being typed (`undo_in_a_table_cell_being_typed_is_the_cell_s`: the table's own step stays) and a talk table line (`talk_table_made_edited_saved_and_used_by_strings`: the table's step stays), with the module tree's Filter and a Properties field before them.
* **Fix**: Toolbar buttons: Icons only, what was left (the deferred sprint, "Toolbar buttons: Icons only", ShadowM's request): the editors' toolbars take it too, the script editor's (Save 💾 … Used By ⤴), the conversation editor's (Add ➕ … Scripts 📃, its Export menu and highlight toggles), a model's view (Play/Pause, Frame, Lights and Shaders' ticks, Pop Out, Export Files…) and the NUI Creator's (Export…, Build & compile, How to open it…), their words alone with Icons and text as before (`Tools::word`, `toggle_word`, `checkbox_word`, glyphs in `icons::editor`). The area view's All ☑, None ☐, Together 🔗, To Scratch 📤 and Save as Prefab… 🗐 have icons, and with Icons only its dropdowns show 🏠 Fade Geometry, 📐 Snap and ↻ Turn before their choice, the name first in their tips. One icon is one command's: Area Properties is 🔧 (ℹ is Module Properties), a palette's Gallery 🖼 (⊞ is the Grid); 🏃 stays both the main toolbar's Start and the area's Go to Start Location, the same command. With Icons only a button is named by its name for a screen reader, and a toggle says it is on or off (`Tools::named`). The palette's tile tools are rows of its list, named as Aurora's palette tree names them (the parity inventory's `tvTerrain`), and keep their names. Tests: `icons::tests::a_glyph_is_one_command_s` and `the_fonts_have_every_toolbar_glyph` (every new glyph drawn), `the_editors_toolbars_show_icons_only_when_asked`, `the_area_toolbar_shows_icons_only_when_asked` and `the_toolbar_shows_icons_only_when_asked` (named by names); `alt_and_a_drag_off_the_selection_moves_the_camera` drags past egui's 6-point drag threshold each step (four meters were about 6 points a step, and passed or failed as the toolbar wrapped). [Options](manual/10-options.md), [the main window](manual/02-main-window.md), [areas](manual/04-areas.md).
* **Fix**: The look of the two side panes, three of its four (the deferred sprint): the palette's search text and the categories left open are remembered between runs (`Settings::palette_search`, `palette_open`: a category by its blueprint type, Standard or Custom and its place, open at the next start until opened or closed; one a search opened is not kept); Expand All and Collapse All have keys, Ctrl+Shift+Right and Ctrl+Shift+Left by default (Options › Keyboard, a group of their own, Module Tree and Palettes: `Cmd::ExpandAll`, `Cmd::CollapseAll`), in the module tree and the palettes while they have the keys, named in the buttons' tips; Collapse All closes every area opened out to its objects and the kinds under it too (Expand All leaves them closed: an area's objects can be hundreds of rows); holding the walkmesh button is held by a test. Tests: `the_palette_shown_last_is_remembered` (a category and the search, kept and opened again), `the_module_tree_s_groups_open_and_close_together` (the keys, and an area opened out closed by Collapse All), `minimap_exported_and_object_walkmeshes_shown` (held: the two open, the release no click). Left in [the deferred list](deferred.md), rewritten: the module tree's rows' pictures and "more" menu, waiting on the owner's verdict (the triage proposed dropping it). [The main window](manual/02-main-window.md), [blueprints](manual/05-blueprints.md), [options](manual/10-options.md).
* **Fix**: The module tree and the palettes by the keyboard, its fix-S items (the deferred sprint, GitHub issue 12): F6 and Shift+F6 give each pane's keys to what its tab in front has (`Moonglow::step_pane`): the middle's, the tab used last outside the palettes' pane (a window's too), is a talk table's lines, a 2DA's cells (at its first cell where none is selected) or the resource browser's list, else an area's; the palettes' pane's the palette or the resource browser docked there; a palette given the keys has its cursor at its first row at once (`PaletteView::to_first`). In the palette's Gallery Up and Down go a row of pictures at a time, to the nearest across, and Left and Right a picture (`Listed::step_pictures`). Open Script's Escape clears the name first (the filters' fix, above). Tests: `next_pane_gives_the_keys_to_a_table_in_the_middle` (`--test app tables`), `next_pane_puts_the_palette_s_cursor_at_its_first_row` (`--test app palettes`), `palette_view::tests::the_arrow_keys_go_a_row_of_pictures_at_a_time_in_the_gallery`. Left in [the deferred list](deferred.md), rewritten: F2 at a placed object and the blueprint kept in hand on a category (both waiting on the Aurora oracle), the log outside F6's panes, and keys for the hak editor's, the tileset editor's, the Appearance Gallery's, the tileset palette's and the Prefabs lists (M); the keys repeating as the system repeats them is the platform's, not a gap. [The main window](manual/02-main-window.md), [blueprints](manual/05-blueprints.md).
* **Fix**: The module's codepage is worked out one way (`mg_rules::text_codepage`, and `GameData::text_codepage` on it): the table of an `encoding.2da` outside the module file, its missing rows the letters of the game's language, else Windows-1252. `mg` (`module_codepage`, Verify's fixes) filled a table's missing rows with Windows-1252's letters whatever the install's language, where the toolset and plugins went by the language's; settled in the server first (`engine_encoding.rs` `a_polish_game_keeps_its_own_letters_where_a_table_has_no_row`: with `game.language.override = "pl"` a partial table's missing rows are Windows-1250's, and the game writes `ð`, which Windows-1250 lacks, as its `đ` where Moonglow writes "?"), and the plugin runtime, `mg plugin run` and the window's text all call the one rule (`a_hak_s_encoding_table_is_the_game_s_codepage`). A tileset's names stay read by the language's own codepage, as `mg_area::tileset` reads them, in the plugin API too. Closes the deferred entry "The module's codepage, four ways".
* **Fix**: Names in order and found, accents aside, fold polytonic Greek and Cyrillic's `ё` too (Sacha's report, October 2026; `mg_core::text`): Greek Extended's breathings, accents, iota subscript and length marks (`Ἀθῆναι` beside `Αθηναι`, a Filter's `ομηρ` finds `Ὅμηρος`), and `ё` as `е` with Bulgarian's and Macedonian's graves (`ѐ`, `ѝ`), while `й`, `ї`, `ў`, `ѓ` and `ќ`, letters of their own, stay (`polytonic_greek_and_cyrillic_sort_and_are_found_beside_their_letters`). The manual's main window chapter says so. Closes the deferred entry "Names in order, accents aside".
* **Fix**: A box's eight corners are worked out in one place, `mg_render::box_corners` (bit 0 of a corner's number its x, bit 1 its y, bit 2 its z, as the area view's twelve edges take them: `a_box_s_corners_in_their_order`), where eight did it by hand: the shadows' reach, the screen's boxes, the area view's outlines, the composed preview's bounds, the model viewer's two framings, the renderer's test framing and its snapshot example. The same numbers as before, in the same order. **lightcolor.2da's toolset colors**, the entry's other half, were already read once (`tile_select::light_colors`, `tile_select.rs:888`, which the area view calls at `area_view.rs:6109`; `TOOLSETRED` is read nowhere else). Closes the deferred entry "The eight corners of a box five times".
* **Fix**: A GFF value as a whole number or as text is read one way, `Value::as_integer` and `Value::number_text` in mg-gff (`whole_numbers_of_every_width_and_numbers_as_text`; `Struct::integer` on it): the custom palette's build (`new.rs`), which left the 64-bit types out, now reads a skeleton's `TYPE` and `STRREF` in any width (`a_skeleton_s_types_are_read_in_any_width`: a DWORD64 placeholder was kept in the palette), and Advanced Search, the raw fields view, the lighting page, `mg areas`, `mg find`'s fields and the resource browser's dump call them where each matched the eight types by hand. A 2DA row's name is one rule (`GameData::named_row`, `cell_string`): a field's row and a table's list of rows (`choice_list`) name a row alike, a blank talk-table string, a negative StrRef and a `****` label none (`a_row_s_name_and_its_choice_are_one`), and the script wizard's constants, the spell warnings' class names and the item editor's base item name and description read a cell's string through it in place of `game.string(StrRef(..))` by hand. A StrRef held as a number (a palette category's, a plugin's) is looked up as it is. Closes the deferred entry "A value as a whole number or text, three times".
* **Fix**: In mg-mdl, `compile::tangents` is gone (no caller in the workspace or in Moonglow Viewer, which reads a mesh's `tangents` field alone; the compiler makes them as the game's does, `engine_tangents`); `sub`, `dot` and `cross` are written once (`lib.rs`, under `face_normal`, which wrote its own), not in the compiler and the binary writer apiece; and `PartNumbers::read` walks a compiled model's nodes with the reader's own walk (`binary::part_numbers`: the same pre-order, its loop and size guards and its copy budget), not a second walk beside it (the models group's compile and decompile sweeps, which number every node by it, pass). A PLT is colored one way, `Plt::colorize_by` (the ten palettes by their names, each as the caller finds it: the renderer from its resources, the window's pictures from their cache; `colours_come_from_the_palette_row_counted_from_the_top`), the same picture as before. Closes the deferred entry "In mg-mdl: `compile.rs:93` `tangents` has no caller".
* **Fix**: A plugin's PNG (`mg.image`) and a NUI window's are read by one reader, `mg_image::png::read_picture` (the NUI skin's, moved down): 16-bit samples rounded to the nearest 8-bit one where the plugin's cut them (0x00FF was 0, now 1: `a_png_of_any_kind_is_read_as_rgba`), each caller's limit by pixels checked before the picture is unpacked and the decoder held to what that many take (`a_png_too_large_or_cut_short_is_an_error`), the window's 4,096 a side kept as its own rule for every kind of picture. The plugin API's reference says so. Closes the deferred entry "The plugin's PNG reading differs from the NUI skin's".
* **Fix**: The PC faction where it is written (Jasperre's report): `mg set` sets an object's faction of 0, or takes its faction field away, and says so (`warning: guard.utc /FactionID: puts it in the PC faction (0)…`, `set_warns_of_the_pc_faction`), and the raw fields view marks a blueprint's or a placed object's faction of 0 beside it (`raw_fields_warn_of_the_pc_faction`; pictures before and after: `raw-fields-pc-faction.png` of `screens.rs` `raw_fields_of_the_pc_faction`), each by Verify's rule (`mg_rules::objects::pc_faction`, `the_pc_faction_is_0_or_no_faction_at_all`), triggers left out. An object with no faction field is of faction 0 in the server, settled there first (`engine_factions.rs` `objects_of_the_pc_faction_in_the_engine`: a placed creature, placeable, door or trigger without the field, and a creature or placeable made from a blueprint without it, as one of 0; a creature put in Hostile), and Verify's `pc-faction` warns of such a creature, placeable or door now ("has no faction, which the game reads as the PC faction (0)"; an encounter's reading not measured, so not one without). The manual's command line, main window, blueprints and Verify chapters say so. The deferred entry "The PC faction, what's left" is rewritten to what waits on Aurora and the client.
* **Fix**: A module's `encoding.2da` where `mg` read Windows-1252 whatever the table: `mg gff --module PATH` reads and writes a lone file's text by that module's table (and gives the same bytes back), `mg lsp --module PATH` reads scripts in it (`--codepage` first, as `mg refs` reads a module's scripts otherwise), and `mg nwsync` publishes the module's name and description in its letters (`query::info_in`, which `mg info` and the window's Publish to NWSync read by too): `a_module_s_encoding_table_is_the_text_of_its_commands`, `the_language_server_reads_scripts_by_a_module_s_table`. **Verification**, in the server (`engine_encoding.rs`): it reads a table from the user folder's `override` too, as Moonglow does (`the_game_reads_an_encoding_table_from_override`); a Polish game keeps its own letters where the table has no row (above); and an English game told `game.language.codepage = "cp1250"` in `settings.tml` reads its text so, as `mg lsp` takes it (`the_game_reads_its_text_in_the_codepage_its_settings_name`). The install has no East Asian language to try. The manual's modules and command line chapters say so. The deferred entry "A module's `encoding.2da`" is rewritten to what is kept by choice (a prefab's bytes, a hak's description, nasher's `--nwn-encoding`, Options › Language over `game.language.codepage`) and one text field's edge.
* **Fix**: A waypoint's flag is judged by the appearance rule (`mg_rules::appearance`: its `Appearance`, a waypoint.2da row whose `RESREF` is the flag; dansen9191's report): the preview, which checked it on its own, goes by the rule; Verify's `object-row` warns of a waypoint blueprint or placed waypoint whose row is not there or whose flag model is missing (a warning wherever it is: the game draws no waypoint; `a_waypoint_s_flag_is_a_row_with_a_model`); and the waypoint's Properties say why under Appearance Type, as the other kinds' do (pictures before and after: `waypoint-no-flag.png` of `screens.rs` `waypoint_with_no_flag`). A waypoint without the field is not judged: BioWare's campaign has 190 waypoint blueprints without one (which the shipped modules' Verify test caught), and its placed waypoints are all row 1 (Chapter 1's 493), as Aurora places them; the waypoint is judged where its file has a row, as the preview shows it. The manual's blueprints and Verify chapters say so. The deferred entry "An appearance with no model, what the warning leaves out" is rewritten to the rest (an item's own models, a body of parts, an unreadable model, the lists' marks), each S, about a day together.
* **Fix**: The placed objects' nine kinds are no longer spelled out in the window (the deferred sprint, [the deferred list](deferred.md)'s "written more than once"): Advanced Search's Look at, the module tree's groups (a `const` built from `ObjectKind::ALL`, whose `label`, `name`, `index` and `restypes` are `const fn` now) and their New… commands, the palettes' New… (`commands::Id::new_blueprint`, the label the tree gives it; every kind has a wizard, so the palettes' check for one went), the tabs' kinds and `icons::blueprint` (gone: `icons::object`) take them from `mg_rules::ObjectKind`. Look at lists the kinds in the toolset's order (Creatures, Doors, Encounters…), as the tree and palettes do. Each blueprint type's windows now keep a size and place of their own (encounters', sounds', triggers' and waypoints' were one kind, `blueprint`): one kept under `blueprint` before is used until the type has its own (`Tab::kind_before`). [The main window](manual/02-main-window.md). Tests: `tree::tests::the_keys_steps`, `search::tests::a_search_is_kept_to_each_kind_of_object`, `tabs::tests::each_blueprint_type_is_a_kind_of_window`.
* **Fix**: A tileset is read one way in the window (the deferred sprint, [the deferred list](deferred.md)'s "written more than once"): `Moonglow::tileset` gives the one an open area's view read, else reads it from the game, and `Moonglow::open_tileset` the view's tools (its tiles' index too). The tile's view, the tiles palette's group pictures, the grass's tileset look and Add Grass to…, Resize Area and Area Properties' tileset name go through it: the last four read the tileset from the game every time, even with its area open. Test: `the_tile_gallery_searches_opens_a_tile_and_drops_one_in_the_area` (`--test app tiles`: the open area's tileset is the view's own, another is read, an unknown one is none).
* **Fix**: A module's haks are read one way (the deferred sprint, [the deferred list](deferred.md)'s "written more than once"): `mg_module::haks_of` (an IFO root's `Mod_HakList`, else the older single `Mod_Hak` where there is no list; names trimmed) is what `Module::haks` reads the saved module by and the window its IFO with unsaved edits (`mg_ui::listed_haks`, for the haks layered, the external compiler and the NUI Creator's pictures, which had no `Mod_Hak` fallback). Module Properties' hak list reads its rows itself (it edits them in place), and where a module has only `Mod_Hak` it now says so, where it said the module had no haks ([Modules](manual/03-modules.md)). The user's hak folder is `GameInstall::user_hak_dir` (the hak editor, the NUI Creator, a plugin's haks in the window and in `mg plugin`), and a hak's file `GameInstall::find_hak` (the resman's layering, `mg haks`' report, NWSync's publishing, `mg serve`'s copy, the external compiler and the resource browser's haks), five copies of one search. Tests: `legacy_single_hak_field`, `a_hak_is_found_in_a_workshop_item` (the layers' priorities too), `an_older_module_s_single_hak_is_layered_and_said` (`--test app resources`).
* **Fix**: Four things the window wrote twice or more, once each (the deferred sprint, [the deferred list](deferred.md)'s "written more than once"): Compile Models' report (`model_view::CompiledModels`: the module's and a hak's; the module's now also logs three of the compiler's notes on each model, as the hak's did, [Build and test](manual/09-build-and-test.md)); the "Files Already in…" window (`widgets::files_already`, the hak editor's adding and a model's Export Files); "A, B and N more" (`text::some_of`, never "and 1 more": `transfer::listed`, Update Instances, a prefab's tags, a blueprint's names in the palettes' tips, the model view's nodes, and the Duplicate Tags window's lines counted the same way); and the 2DA and talk-table file dialogs, which asked for any file under "Open" and "Save As": `FileKind::Table` (Open 2DA, Save 2DA As, `.2da`), `FileKind::TalkTable` (Open Talk Table, New Talk Table, `.tlk`) and `FileKind::TalkLines` (Import Lines, Export Lines, `.csv` or `.json`). Tests: `text::tests::a_few_names_and_how_many_more`, `tables_and_talk_tables_ask_for_their_own_files` (`--test app tables`), and the existing `the_module_s_text_models_are_compiled`, `the_hak_editor_compiles_the_models_kept_as_text` and the two windows' Skip Those tests.
* **Fix**: What the window still made every frame, kept (the deferred sprint): a blueprint's Category list (`palette_view::categories`, made again when the module changes, the game data is read again or Options › General's order changes; it cloned the module's own palette skeleton or read and parsed the game's each frame), Module Properties' listing of the hak folders (read again two seconds on, so a hak copied in still shows), the script editor's symbols (sorted by name once as they are made, the script's own before `nwscript.nss`'s of one name; the side panel's width from the longest names worked out once for them) and its templates' folders (read again two seconds on, or when another folder is chosen), the Find Text window's copies of Find in Files' results (the window copied the whole search twice a frame, results and all), the thumbnails' lists (`Thumbnails::made` looks a picture up: the Appearance Gallery, the Creature Wizard, the palettes' and the tiles palette's Galleries each made a map of every picture made, each frame) and the module tree's rows for the keys (each group's resources borrowed from the lists made for the module's revision, not copied). Tests: `a_blueprint_s_categories_are_made_once` (`--test app palettes`), `module_props::tests::the_hak_folders_are_read_again_after_a_while`, `script_tools::tests::a_script_s_symbols_are_kept_by_name`, `…::the_templates_are_read_again_after_a_while`; the galleries' and the tree's keys' tests as they were.
* **Fix**: The project file (`moonglow.toml`, and the builder's own beside it) is read again when it changes with Options › General's reloading off too (every few seconds as the window draws, a few small files' stamps; with reloading on, the folder's watch does it as before), and Setup Store, open then, follows the rules as they are now rather than those it opened with ([Modules](manual/03-modules.md)). One `Moonglow::reread_project_file` for the four places that read it again. Test: `the_project_file_is_read_again_with_reloading_off` (`--test app projects`; Setup Store's test's module made by a helper both share).
* **Fix**: A window's Maximize has a button and a key, and shows ([The main window](manual/02-main-window.md)): a button at the right of the window's bar, before its ✕ (🗖 Maximize, 🗗 Restore, named so for a screen reader; drawn over the bar beside the tabs, which egui_dock leaves empty, as its double click and menu were), and **Maximize Window** (Ctrl+Shift+M, Options › Keyboard; a command of the Command Palette's too) for the window of the tab in front. A maximized window is put over Moonglow's window again as that is resized, larger or smaller (it stayed at the old size). That a size dragged by hand at the window's corner is the next window's of its kind is under test now. Tests: `a_window_is_maximized_by_its_key_and_button_and_stays_so` (`--test app windows`); pictures: `window_bar_maximize_and_an_older_hak` in `tests/screens.rs` (before, from the code before the package: no button; after: 🗖, and 🗗 maximized).
* **Fix**: A module's kept tabs keep more ([the deferred list](deferred.md)'s entry, rewritten to what is left): a script's bookmarks and numbered bookmarks (those its text still has lines for), the talk table's line chosen (chosen again and brought into view), and a Tile Properties still waiting for its area's view when the module is closed again (its area's tab not shown since the module opened: it was dropped from the record). A conversation's marks are checked by a fingerprint fixed whatever the toolchain (`siphasher`'s SipHash-1-3 with zero keys, the value Rust's `DefaultHasher` gives today, so no kept record loses its marks by the change). [The main window](manual/02-main-window.md). Tests: `views_open_again_as_they_were` (the bookmarks), `the_talk_table_opens_again_on_its_line`, `tile_properties_waiting_for_its_area_is_kept_again` (`--test app windows`), `dialog_view::tests::a_conversation_s_fingerprint_is_fixed`.
* **Fix**: Alt and a menu's letter leaves the key to a command given it in Options › Keyboard (it took the key from it, and opened the menu); AltGr and a letter (Ctrl+Alt, as Windows gives a character of another layout) opens no menu, and Alt alone, pressed and let go, is under test now by its modifier events, beside F10 ([The main window](manual/02-main-window.md)). `Keymap::bound` says whether any command (a plugin's and a project's too) has a key. [The deferred list](deferred.md)'s entry is rewritten to what is left: Alt alone on Windows (waits on a Windows build) and three proposals to drop. Test: `menus_by_the_keyboard` (`--test app options_and_keys`).
* **Fix**: The palettes' pane's covered arrow (egui_dock's, which folds a pane to its bar) is no longer reached by Tab or Shift+Tab, where Enter then folded the pane: the keyboard goes on past it the way it was going (`Moonglow::pass_covered_arrow`, before the dock draws, as egui hands the focus on), and the cover's own click area takes no focus. [The deferred list](deferred.md)'s Panes folded away entry is rewritten to what is left, each for the reporter of GitHub issue 10 (still open) to say, as their rule shapes it. Test: `the_palettes_covered_arrow_is_passed_by_the_keyboard` (`--test app windows`: round the window both ways, never on the arrow).

## 2026-10-28
* **Update**: Direct3D 12 compiles shaders with DXC (the owner's decision on the FXC entry): the Windows installer and zip put Microsoft's DirectX Shader Compiler `dxcompiler.dll` (x64, release v1.9.2609 pinned by its SHA-256, downloaded as the package is built: `packaging/windows/dxc.ps1`) beside `moonglow.exe` and `mg.exe`, and every wgpu instance Moonglow makes (the window's, the headless device's, the tests') names that file (`mg_render::dx12`: by its whole path, loaded first; FXC where it is missing or won't load; `WGPU_DX12_COMPILER` still decides). The debug log's `graphics:` line ends with the compiler taken. `dxil.dll` isn't needed (`dxcompiler.dll` alone signs what it compiles: checked) and isn't shipped; DXC is NCSA- and MIT-licensed, and its texts and notices are in `THIRD-PARTY-LICENSES.txt`. Checked under Wine (`tools/dx12/dxc.sh`, the arguments wgpu gives DXC): the install's 38 distinct stages of the game's programs all compile at shader models 6.0, 6.6 and 6.8, `fslit_nm` and `fslit_sm_nm` among them, which FXC still can't (X3511). A new render test, `a_texture_read_in_a_loop_of_a_varying_count_compiles_and_draws`, draws an authored shader with the same construct (a texture read with derivatives in a loop whose count varies by pixel; FXC compiles one whose count is a uniform alone) and says which compiler; CI's Windows job puts the pinned DLL beside the test binaries and requires DXC there (`MOONGLOW_REQUIRE_DXC=1`). With DXC, wgpu offers no Direct3D 12 adapter whose driver lacks shader model 6. [Troubleshooting](manual/13-troubleshooting.md) (Direct3D 12's shader compiler), [the deferred list](deferred.md): **FXC can't compile the normal-mapped lit programs** closed; what DXC on Heed's own driver shows added to his waiting entry.
* **Update**: The area view's Alt + middle click puts the camera's focus on the ground clicked (El Grillo's ask, Blender's Alt + middle click): Alt + middle click alone (a plain middle click does nothing, so a short middle drag never jumps the view). The focus goes at once to the point on the walkmesh, else on the floor of the tile there at its height; the distance, turn and tilt stay; the sky and the ground past the area's edge do nothing, and Ctrl or Shift with it leave it to the middle drags. Mouse gestures stay fixed (not in Options › Keyboard, as settled). The model viewer has no point to pick on (no ground, no ray against the model), so it has none. [Areas › The camera](manual/04-areas.md)
* **Update**: A tile's textures (Leoendethas' request): Tile Properties, a tile's view and the tileset editor's tile list the textures the tile's model draws, each with its picture, its file (DDS, TGA or PLT, size, format, bytes), where in the load order it comes from (the game, a hak by name, override, module), what draws it (the mesh nodes, the slot, the material it is drawn through, the environment map a TXI names, emitters and flares) and the missing ones marked; one listing (`mg_preview::replaceable::uses` and `texture_file`) that the Visuals page's texture replacements now come from too. [Areas](manual/04-areas.md), "A tile's textures".
* **Update**: Return types declared one way and defined another compile as in Aurora (El Grillo's report: scripts that compile in Aurora fail in Moonglow; the base game's `x2_inc_cutscene` fails every includer). The built-in compiler (neverwinter.nim 0972fc1f, as nwn_script_comp 2.3.1) refuses a function declared with one return type and defined with another; Aurora (8193.37) compiles it, the type met first kept at the calls. Watched in Aurora with a probe module (`tools/aurora/capture_scripts.py`, captures `script-compile`): `x2_inc_cutscene`'s includers, the mismatch both ways, include files, comments of every form. A marked patch of the vendored compiler (its [README](../crates/mg-script/nwscript/README.md)) gives Aurora's bytes, all 19 of the probe's; 338 scripts of the base game and the shipped modules compile now (all four mismatches met there are definitions that return nothing). The compile warns instead, naming the function and both lines, with what the game does (`engine_return_types`: the declared type's empty value; a value where `void` was declared overwrites the caller's last variable; another type stops the script where it is used): in the Compiler messages and the log, once per warning in Compile All, `mg compile` and `mg watch` (`"warnings"` in their JSON), and in `mg lsp`. Also: an `#include` commented out includes nothing (Verify reported one in a block comment missing; the includers compiled with an include, and Rename, read it), `#include` is read one way (`mg_script::lex::includes`), and a script whose `main` follows broken code (a "nested" block comment) is reported, as Aurora reports it, not passed over as an include. [Scripts](manual/07-scripts.md), [the deferred list](deferred.md) (`#include` read three ways, closed; the include files' entry seen again).
* **Update**: Heed's armourer (`hss_armourer`, the area he sent as still lit unlike the game): drawn in the client and in the area view from four cameras, it matches within 0.1 to 1.6 of 255 over the picture and 3.1 in any region, the Shaders switch's two pictures the same (`heed_armourer_look`, a new client comparison that builds his area of stock content: `tni01`'s smithy in its black filler, his tiles' lights, 60/170, fog of 5, the innkeeper; `a_builders_area_in_the_view_draws_as_the_comparison` holds the window's picture of his own file to it). **A tile's emitters run ahead** (fixed: a forge's fire and a chimney's smoke were dark for their first seconds after the area opened or changed, an object's were not; `a_tile_s_emitters_burn_from_the_first_frame`). [Findings](findings.md): a tile's main lights take the row's color over the node's own, black or not, and a tile without light nodes lights none of its settings; Lighting Scheme is Aurora's preset only. [Deferred](deferred.md): "Heed's tile lights" and "Heed's interior washed out with the game's shaders" made one entry, waiting on Heed's description, a picture and his haks (2 closed, 1 added).
* **Update**: Heed's Radeon RX 580 still drew nothing of an area through Direct3D 12 with 2.13.0, and neither check said so. What could get past them, found in the code and closed: **the watch** (`mg_render::watch`) now judges frames that stay not ready with nothing of what they wait for finished for 20 s (`PATIENCE`: a texture never read left every tile out, and no look counted), expects tiles whose models were read and failed (`AreaScene::failed_tiles`), gives up a look the GPU doesn't give back, reads the picture's color as well as its depth, and writes what it sees to the debug log (`graphics watch:` lines). The game's pipelines catch internal and out-of-memory errors as well as validation ones (Direct3D 12's FXC failing is internal: it reached the device's default handler, a panic and a crash report), and their failures go to the log, not stderr. **The lit check** (`probe::probe_lit`): once the game's data is read, a lit, BC1-textured mesh drawn by Moonglow's own shader and by the game's `vslit`/`fslit`, apart from the window's thread (25 and 40 ms here); where only the game's draw nothing, Moonglow's own draws for the session. **HLSL as Direct3D 12 writes it** (`mg_testkit::hlsl`, `tools/dx12/`): Moonglow's own shaders (29 stages, on every platform's tests) and the game's programs as the worker gives them to wgpu (the install's 38 distinct stages), compiled by Microsoft's FXC under Wine: all of Moonglow's compile; of the game's, `fslit_nm` and `fslit_sm_nm` don't (X3511, their height-map loop), so on Direct3D 12 those meshes fall back to Moonglow's own shader. The watch's warm-up test made deterministic. [Troubleshooting](manual/13-troubleshooting.md) (the lit check, what to send), [the deferred list](deferred.md): Heed's entry rewritten (what is closed, what waits on him), El Grillo's report on Direct3D 12 noted, the FXC failure filed, **Renderer changes not yet on Direct3D 12 and Metal** closed (2.13.0's CI drew them on both).
* **Update**: Unused scripts (Jasperre's report). **A script is one line**, with the files the module has of it (`Unused: x.nss (source and compiled)`, `compiled only (no source)`, `source only (not compiled)`), in Build Module and `mg verify --unused` (whose JSON `unused` is now objects: `resource`, `names`, `files`). **What counts as used**, beyond the fields and `#include`s it read before: a string in a script that spells a name (`ExecuteScript("x")`, `CreateObject`, what an `ExecuteScriptChunk` quotes; not a script's own name, nor comments); the scripts the game runs from its 2DAs as the module sees them (`spells.2da` ImpactScript, `vfx_persistent.2da` ONENTER/ONEXIT/HEARTBEAT, `statescripts.2da` SCRIPTNAME, `traps.2da` TrapScript, `disease.2da` End_Incu_Script/24_Hour_Script, `poison.2da` Script_1/Script_2; each checked against nwn.wiki and the game's tables, `areaeffects.2da` left out as the engine doesn't read it), a new column kind `script` (`mg.twoda.kinds`, the 2DA editor's tips) that also goes into `mg haks --usage`; what the module reaches in its haks and the game names (Kingmaker's 1,220 unused scripts are 205: its conversations and blueprints are in its hak); and a module table's column described `kind = "script"`. **Marks**: the project file's `[used] scripts`, `conversations`, `blueprints` (names and globs; beside a `.mod`, `NAME.moonglow.toml`, made where there is none), written by Build Module's **Mark as Used** (a button and the line's menu) and **Counted as Used** (listed, Remove, Add), and by `mg used`. Find References and `mg refs` say which table runs a script and which mark counts it. [Build, verify and test](manual/09-build-and-test.md#what-counts-as-used), [Modules](manual/03-modules.md#the-project-file-moonglowtoml), [the command line](manual/11-command-line.md), [the plugin API](manual/17-plugin-api.md). No entry of [the deferred list](deferred.md) added or closed.
* **Update**: The renderer checks what it draws. Heed's Radeon RX 580 drew nothing of an area through Direct3D 12 (Automatic's choice for AMD on Windows since 2.11.0) and drew right through Vulkan; El Grillo's Radeon PRO lost its tiles through Vulkan: no fixed rule serves both. **The startup check** (`mg_render::probe`): before the window opens, a small scene drawn offscreen through Moonglow's own pipelines (solid, see-through, see-through over solid, skinned) and compared; where it draws nothing on Automatic, the same GPU's other backend is taken (once), the failure recorded in the settings by GPU, driver and backend, and passed over by Automatic until the driver changes (88 ms on the development machine's Vulkan). **The area view's watch** (`mg_render::watch`): once a second the depth drawn at a 64 by 36 grid against the ground of the tiles the camera sees, judged only with the pipelines ready and warm, above a floor and for five seconds; a notice names the adapter and backend and offers **Restart with …** through the close flow, with the module. Automatic's order is kept. Neither builder's case was reproduced; both are imitated with `guard::blank_draws`. [Options](manual/10-options.md), [Troubleshooting](manual/13-troubleshooting.md), [the deferred list](deferred.md)'s graphics backend entry (two confirmations waiting on the builders, in place of one).

* **Update**: [The deferred sprint](deferred-sprint.md), the package of nasher projects and module folders: 8 entries of [the deferred list](deferred.md) closed (all fixed; three sentences settled, one part already fixed and one dropped), none added. **A module folder with a resource under two spellings:** measured with nasher 1.1.3, its pack renames the spelling not in lower case over the other, whichever is newer, and of two folders packs one in the file system's order; a project now reads the spelling nasher packs, says so, and its next save leaves that one file in lower case ([Modules](manual/03-modules.md)). **A nasher project's file that isn't UTF-8 throughout:** a file broken some other way (a merge's markers) is left out and said, not written over, and read as added once mended. **Compiled scripts without source:** nasher packs a `.ncs` from the sources as it is, so Save As nasher Project and `mg init` keep such scripts, their `nasher.cfg` taking `.ncs` in; one whose source comes is build output again. **Export's stamp:** a module string variable `moonglow_base` (a commit, written by a team's download script) is a `.mod`'s base outside a checkout, stamped and compared by Import; only a hash is handed to git; Import onto a branch settled. **A module folder worked on in place:** scripts compiled after a reload are written at once where the module had no unsaved changes (`Module::write_made`); `mg`'s open, change and save was found already guarded, now under test; includers compiled by Compile, Save and the module's Save under test; Save As into another's folder confirmed already fixed; compiling on the window's thread and needing the game's data settled. **A `.mod` with its folder beside it:** found in any case (and written as that `.mod`), Aurora checked on the oracle (it asks only where the folder holds a `module.ifo`, as Moonglow), every `mg` command opens through one helper that notes the folder, the welcome page ends a folder's name in `/` with its folder after; Measured in the client first ([the research note](research/notes_shaders.md) §G.1): its Creature Shadow Detail governs every dynamic object (an ogre and the dynamic armoires cast at Best, none at Fast or Off; tiles and static placeables cast with the environment's); a creature casts one shadow from its nearest light of `shadow 1` and radius 10 m or more (a lamp post's; the tiles' flames, 7 m, none), from 3 m above it (`verticaldisplacement`), only where the area has no sun's or moon's shadows at the time, as dark as the opacity times the plane's fade. Moonglow: skinned meshes cast into the sun's map as their bones pose them; per creature a 512² perspective map from its light. Within 0.03 of the client's darkening for the ogre's sun shadow and the lamp's at 30 and 100. Cost at 3440×1440 with 4× MSAA: creatures under 0.1 ms; 16 creatures casting the lights' shadows 0.6 ms. Measured in the client first (`shadows_look`, [the research note](research/notes_shaders.md) §G.1): a flat multiply (0.30 at opacity 30), opacity 60 and 100 alike under the fade, dynamic placeables receive none, the same direction by night. ## 2026-10-27
* **Update**: [Blueprints](manual/05-blueprints.md) (Variables…) and [Troubleshooting](manual/13-troubleshooting.md#where-moonglow-keeps-its-files): variable sets are renamed and deleted (Add Set › Manage Sets…, the Variable Sets window: Rename… refuses a name another set has in any case, or one that can't name a file; Delete with Undo Delete, as the prefab palette's), saved to a file of one's choosing (Save to File…) and added from one (Add from File…, merged as Add Set merges), and their folder opened (Open Sets Folder); the manual says where the files are on each system and that they can be edited by hand (Troubletcat's and ShadowM's requests, October 2026). Rename, delete and put back are `saved::Shelf`'s, which prefabs and variable sets share. Add Set keeps its name: it merges. [The deferred list](deferred.md): what is left.
* **Update**: Options › General › **Graphics backend** ([Options](manual/10-options.md), [Troubleshooting](manual/13-troubleshooting.md#an-area-draws-nothing-or-loses-its-tiles-on-an-amd-gpu-under-windows)), from El Grillo's report (October 2026): on an AMD Radeon PRO under Windows (AMD's Vulkan driver 25.10.37.03) an area's tiles were drawn when it opened and then lost one by one, and `WGPU_BACKEND=dx12` drew them right. Automatic, the default, is wgpu's choice except that an AMD GPU on Windows is taken through Direct3D 12 rather than Vulkan; Vulkan and Direct3D 12 can be chosen on Windows, Vulkan on Linux, Metal on macOS (not OpenGL, which Moonglow draws wrong: wgpu falls back to it only where nothing else draws), from the next start (read from eframe's settings file before the window opens); a backend with no adapter falls back to Automatic and the log says so; `WGPU_BACKEND` still decides over it. The choice is one function, `mg_render::backend::pick_adapter`, over the adapters' backend, vendor, device and kind (wgpu's order by power preference kept), used by the window and by the headless renderers (`mg`, the previews), which now follow `WGPU_BACKEND` too; the debug log's `graphics:` line says why the adapter was taken. What is left is in [the deferred list](deferred.md) (The install, starting and crashes).
* **Update**: [The framebuffer proposal](framebuffer-proposal.md): its spike named by its commit, `039c999b`, the branch `framebuffer-assessment` deleted with the others merged or done.
* **Update**: The review's list worked through ([Scripts](manual/07-scripts.md) and [Options](manual/10-options.md): the external compiler's limits). **Hostile files:** what GFF, TLK and binary-model readers copy from offsets that point at one stretch is capped (64 times the input and 64 MiB), a 2DA's short rows are padded to a bound (8 cells a byte and a million), a TGA's run-length path makes room by the data there, a TLK in JSON with an entry far past the rest is refused (where `nwn_tlk` builds it); truncation tests for PLT, ASCII models and walkmeshes, 2DA and SET. **Writers** refuse a model past 4 GiB, a render order or bone past its field, and a GFF value past 4 GiB before writing it. **External programs** stop after a time (the compiler two minutes, git 30 s), the compiler is given the scripts asked for and what they include (not every script), and a hak path with a comma is said where `{haks}` can't carry it. **Writes:** locks, the builder's project file and `moonglow.toml` through a temporary file; the locks file named by SHA-1 (an old one taken over), and one that can't be read is said and kept as `.unreadable`; a folder save reads each file once; a `.mod` is written straight to disk and its `.bak` linked, not copied. The hak editor finds resources by name and keeps only each step's change (20,000 resources); script stamps walk each include once (the first Compile after this recompiles every script); undo and redo keep a command that fails; a missing 2DA is looked for once. **Frames** (`frame_perf`'s new views, before and after): the log of 10,000 lines 12.5 to 0.40 ms, the Preview window of a 1,000-item store 9.36 to 0.34, the Export window of 25,000 resources 3.56 to 0.35, an area with everything selected and its walkmesh 5.4 to 4.4, typing in `nwscript.nss` 28.8 to 13.2, 12,480 particles 1.07 to 0.87; Find References and Speaker Tag's list made once. **Defects:** a placed sound took over the one deleted before it, the brush's ghost kept a blueprint's old look, a conversation whose lines own one another twice grew as 2^depth, plugin trust looked up the project folder on disk every frame. One ray-triangle test, one lightcolor.2da conversion, one `map_file`. [The deferred list](deferred.md): what is left.
* **Update**: A review of the code for defects and cost ([Modules](manual/03-modules.md): Save As into a folder) (six readers over the workspace, clippy's pedantic lints as a finder, `frame_perf` as the measure: every view within 144 fps). **Fixed, each with a test:** `mg unpack`, Export and a folder or nasher save wrote a resource named `../x` (as a crafted hak may name one) outside the folder chosen (`ResRef::is_file_name`, `ResKey::path_in`); a plugin's code could be loaded as Luau bytecode, which runs unverified (now text only); Save As into a folder of other resources deleted them (now refused, nothing deleted); a hak's compressed resource made room for the size its header claimed and read a frame without end (now bounded by the header and the frame); a model whose keys have more than 15 values was compiled to a byte that read back as a Bézier key of none (now an error); Undo of a removed field put it back at the struct's end and dropped any other of its label, and of an item that made its list left the list (now exact, so an undone file saves as it was); closing a module kept its 2DA editors, whose Save then wrote the table into the next module; a recovery copy being written as the module was saved was offered at the next start; the script editor's completion accepted after the cursor left its word panicked; the script editor's functions and error markers followed an include edited in another tab, and another install's `nwscript.nss`, only after a restart; the claims table rewritten turned a Windows-1252 name to U+FFFD and was written in place; nasher.cfg's line added turned a CRLF file to LF; a Delete of tiles that was refused never showed which cells; a spawn point held while Undo took an object before it away edited another (or panicked); a store's script named a tag of `é` wrongly; the Standard palette's tags searched another module's; the external compiler waited on Moonglow's input. **Faster:** the includers of a changed script found in one pass (a scan of every script for each found), uncompressed hak resources read without a second copy, 2DA column names looked up without an allocation per cell, blueprint and area pages share the game data's choice lists instead of copying them every frame, drop-onto-objects looks up the selection in a set. `frame_perf`'s reload test waits for every file of the pull, not the last of each kind (it failed now and then; "all in" is 3.6 s, not the 1.5 s it printed). [The deferred list](deferred.md): what the review found and left.
* **Update**: The tests faster, and logic written more than once made one. **Speed** (measured per test with libtest's `--report-time`, whose times include the wait for a lock): the UI tests were one queue behind the GPU lock, 188 tests of about a fifth of a second each one after another (42 s of the `app` binary's 42); the lock is four places now (`mg_testkit::gpu`, `MG_TEST_GPUS` for another count, 1 as before; eight was tried, three runs stable and 3 s faster). The command line's five tests that start the server by another program take turns three at once (`engine::timed_turn`, where one at a time made 15 s), and the corpus test that waits for a tileset with LF line ends not to load waits three times the CRLF run, not a fixed minute (the `engine` group 66 s to 25 s). The everyday suite 69 s to 27 s; the three semaphores of `mg-testkit` are one `Places`. **A defect found by it:** the window took the failures of models that broke as they were built (`mg_render::guard`, kept for the whole process) in every frame, and a test's window with no GPU took another test's; a window takes them only when it draws (in the program there is one window), and a test that makes a model fail on purpose takes the GPU alone (`gpu::alone`). The UI tests ask for a GPU adapter once, not with a second device in each test that draws. **One of each:** the day-to-date arithmetic (five copies, and ERF build dates by a loop) is `mg_core::time`; the struct a `GffPath` leads to, to change, is `GffPath::get_mut` (two hand copies gone); a ray against a box is `mg_preview::compose::ray_box` (the area view's picking and the previews'); a plugin's log level and a script's text hash once each in mg-ui; the Aurora comparisons' seven `fn lines` one `aurora_common::lines`; the command line's tests read `mg --json` with `json_out` and `quiet_json_out`; and eight tests that another nearly covered or that shared its set-up and changed nothing are part of it (each assertion kept). **Scratch folders named twice** (eight tests' pictures in one `render` folder, two script tests' modules in one, a test emptying its own `before.png`): each call of `scratch_dir` empties the folder, under any test using it beside; pictures go to `output_dir`, and `every_scratch_dir_is_named_once` holds each name to one call. [The plan](PLAN.md) §7, [the deferred list](deferred.md) (the fixtures left, the tests that could be one).
* **Update**: [Areas](manual/04-areas.md): the Tiles palette's search finds a group or feature by the models of its tiles as well as by its name (GitHub issue 35, vickydotbat's report: Medieval City's Inn 1 (2x3) by `tcm02_s01_05`, a tile no terrain or crosser makes), in the list, the count and the Gallery, where it stays one picture and is no heading of its tiles. Each model is searched alone, not with the group's name.
* **Update**: Heed's interior with dark gray area lights (Heed's report, October 2026), settled in the game client: an area's ambient and diffuse are light added to the tile lights, each channel (byte/255)^2.2 of white (a gray of 60 is 4% of white ambient, 170 is 41% from the sun's side), and a white ambient drowns the tile lights in the client too. A stock `tin01` interior set as his smithy (Always Bright, Interior Bright, black fog of 5) draws the same with the Shaders switch on and off, within 0.7 to 1.5 of 255 of the client's (`heed_interior_look`, its client pictures kept). With his custom pair on the tiles, Moonglow's own shader lit them as `fslit` where his `fhss_skintile` lights as `fslit_nm` (0.2 to 0.8 of 255 apart, brighter in the dark corners): **a custom pair is lit as its fragment program defines** now, 0.00 apart. [Findings](findings.md) (An area's ambient and diffuse beside its tile lights), [the deferred list](deferred.md) (Heed's tile lights and interior, needing his module; a custom fragment program's other defines).
* **Update**: The application's UI tests, `crates/mg-ui/tests/app.rs` (32,414 lines), are `crates/mg-ui/tests/app/`: twenty subject modules of the one `app` binary and `common.rs` with what they share, among it `harness`, `game_app` and `gpu` for the set-ups that were written out each time (the GPU lock taken by the helper that draws; the one test that rendered without it takes it). The tests' names and what they assert are unchanged. [The plan](PLAN.md), [the checklists](parity/checklists.md), [findings](findings.md) and [the shaders proposal](shaders-proposal.md) name the new files; [the deferred list](deferred.md): the set-ups left by hand.
* **Update**: [Areas](manual/04-areas.md#duplicate-tags), [Options](manual/10-options.md) and [Modules](manual/03-modules.md): the Duplicate Tags window after a paste is Options › General › Show duplicate tags warning after a paste or a copy, off by default as Aurora has none, with Don't warn again in the window; a project's `[tags] paste_warning`, where set, wins over it, and with it off only the tags the project wants unique are said. Every kind is weighed alike (a tag typed in, not the blueprint's): a store pasted with its blueprint's tag no longer asks (its blueprint is named by `ResRef`). (Troubletcat's report, October 2026)
* **Update**: [Blueprints](manual/05-blueprints.md) and [Modules](manual/03-modules.md): a blueprint copied into an open module folder or nasher project (or changed or deleted there) shows in its custom palette as soon as the files are read again, its entry written at the next save, and the palette changed is unsaved work; the palette's More › Refresh Palette makes that type's custom palette anew, as Aurora's does (Troubletcat's report, October 2026). [The deferred list](deferred.md): entries left stale while the module was closed, a blueprint in a category the palette lacks.
* **Update**: Verify of the Domains of Dread's 145 haks from 4 s to 1.4 s, its findings the same byte for byte: the model linter no longer looks through the whole text for each node named twice, a tile model's faces are told from its `faces` counts, compiled models by their first bytes, and the shader programs compile in parallel; [the deferred list](deferred.md): what is left of the time.
* **Update**: The build: a release rebuilds the two programs alone: the libraries keep `version = "0.0.0"` and the release is the programs' (the root `Cargo.toml`'s one line, as before), which they set as they start (`mg_core::release`) for what the libraries write of it (an export's stamp, an NWSync manifest, the About box, the update check, the debug log, the compiled shaders' cache); a bump recompiled 27 crates for `mg` and now 2 for both programs. [Packaging](../packaging/README.md) says what a release changes. [The deferred list](deferred.md): the entry done; the viewer's shader cache now named by its `FORMAT` alone until it sets the release. And `mg` without the GPU stack: of what it reached that renders, only `mg nui render` draws (wgpu) and `mg nui layout` lays out with the GUI's library; `mg model-files` and the usage search read mg-preview's models and files, which need no renderer (minimaps and palettes are mg-module's, drawn on the CPU). Those two commands are the feature `nui-preview` of `mg`, on by default (the packages and CI build it as before); mg-preview's renderer half (`compose`) is its default feature `render`, and the two helpers its files read moved below the renderer (`mg_image::plt::split_colors`, `mg_mdl::animations`, still `mg_render`'s too). `cargo build --release -p mg --no-default-features`: 140 crates in place of 229, 87 s in place of 162 cold, 22.7 MB in place of 37.8. CI checks that build with clippy. [The plan](PLAN.md) §4, [the deferred list](deferred.md).
* **Update**: [The framebuffer proposal](framebuffer-proposal.md), step 4 built: soft particles. The client's `particleSoftness`, read through a patched `fsparticle`, is 10 over the emitter's larger size (none for `Billboard_to_Local_Z` and `Billboard_to_World_Z`), the same at Performance and High Quality; Moonglow's particles fade through the framebuffer's depth copy as the client's (a floor particle's foot 3.0 of 255 from the client's without the fade, 0.8 with it; 0.1 to 0.35 ms of the GPU where particles alone make the copy). [Areas](manual/04-areas.md) › the toolbar's Shaders; [the deferred list](deferred.md).
* **Update**: [The framebuffer proposal](framebuffer-proposal.md), steps 1 to 3 built: the framebuffer's copies made with the area view's targets and filled only in a frame that reads them, bound to the game's programs with `gl_FragCoord.y` counted from the bottom in IR, and **shiny water drawn by the game's `fswater` by default** with the Shaders switch on, closer to the client than Moonglow's own (a rural area's far water 10.9 of 255 against 12.6, TDoD's fountain 10.6 against 11.7; 0.3 to 1.3 ms more of the GPU where water fills the view); particles that don't spin drawn upright, as the client draws them. [Areas](manual/04-areas.md) › the toolbar's Shaders; [the deferred list](deferred.md).
* **Update**: [Scripts](manual/07-scripts.md) and [the main window](manual/02-main-window.md): Edit › Undo and Redo and the toolbar's are a script's own while its editor has the keyboard, as its Ctrl+Z is (a builder's report), say plain "Undo" and "Redo", and give the keys back to the editor; why the module's history was not given the script's typing. [The deferred list](deferred.md): not grayed with nothing to undo, a menu closed with nothing chosen, keys given in Options, the side editors' toolbar Undo.
* **Update**: [The main window](manual/02-main-window.md): letters typed in the module tree go to an area's kinds and placed objects as their rows show them; Down from its Filter goes to the first row it finds; Delete at a placed object the keys are at deletes it, as its menu does. Every Filter and Find field finds a row with every word typed, in any order and in any of its fields (`sword iron` finds Iron Sword: the palettes alone did), `mg areas --match` too ([Command-line tools](manual/11-command-line.md)); one search field, its Ctrl+F the Find of Options › Keyboard. [The deferred list](deferred.md): what differs still.
* **Creation**: [A framebuffer for the game's water and particles: a proposal](framebuffer-proposal.md) (draft): what reads `texFBColor`/`texFBDepth` (`fswater` and `fsparticle`; none of TDoD's 37 programs, its two `sample_framebuffer` materials only drawn late), where a copy goes in the frame, a spike on the `framebuffer-assessment` branch (the copy 0.0-0.2 ms of the GPU, 4 ms when made in a full video memory; `fswater` with it 1.4-2.0 ms more than Moonglow's water, and see-through), the alternatives, portability, and a recommendation (M, gated on a comparison with the client); [the deferred list](deferred.md) links to it.

[Frames](research/notes_frames.md), [persistent-world scale](research/notes_scale.md), [the deferred list](deferred.md): the rows out of sight take a guessed height, a new question reads the module again, the events list reads what was found.

[The deferred list](deferred.md): what is left of both.

## 2026-10-25
* **Update**: [Conversations](manual/06-conversations.md) and [the deferred list](deferred.md): a conversation opened (from the module tree, or its tab brought to the front) has the keys at once, as Aurora gives them: Delete before a click no longer asks to delete the whole conversation (Sacha's report; the owner's choice).
* **Update**: Sacha's reports on the conversation editor. **A crash:** Delete on a line of a conversation opened from the module tree went to the module tree, which still had the keys, and asked to delete the whole conversation; Delete there removed the conversation's tab from its window with egui_dock's `retain_tabs`, which left the window an empty pane that egui_dock panicked drawing. Tabs now close one at a time, so a window goes with its last tab (deleting a resource, a placed object or a file gone outside Moonglow while its window was open crashed the same way), and a click in the conversation editor gives its tree the keys. **The arrow keys:** [Conversations](manual/06-conversations.md) › The keyboard: Up and Down from line to line, Right and Left open and fold a branch or go into and out of it, Home, End, Page Up and Page Down, as Windows' tree control (Aurora's) has them, the line scrolled into sight; not while a field is typed in. **A panic keeps the unsaved work:** the recovery copy is written as the frame stops, not only every few minutes ([Troubleshooting](manual/13-troubleshooting.md) › After a crash). [The deferred list](deferred.md) (the tree's keys left out, bookmarks after a delete, the copy at a panic, egui_dock upstream).
* **Update**: Sacha's report on 2.5.4. **Dropable and Pickpocketable** were there but hidden until an item was clicked; [Blueprints](manual/05-blueprints.md) › Creatures: a creature's Inventory has Aurora's **Options** under the palette (Selected Item, Dropable, Pickpocketable), always shown and grayed until an item is selected; Ctrl+click and Shift+click select several items, which the boxes set in one step (a dash where they differ); the same on a placed creature. Placing a creature now keeps its equipped items' flags in the GIT, and Add to Palette in the blueprint (both were lost). Settled in the engine: the fields' names, 1 sets them, 0 or left out does not ([findings](findings.md): A creature's items). **Ctrl+Insert, Shift+Delete and Shift+Insert** copy, cut and paste wherever Ctrl+C, X and V do, text fields included, and Shift+Delete never also deletes ([The main window](manual/02-main-window.md) › Keyboard shortcuts). [The checklists](parity/checklists.md), [the deferred list](deferred.md) (Aurora's backpack and Add to Palette not captured; Shift+Insert not pressed in the running program; a placed creature's slot names out of sight in its window as it opens).
* **Update**: Heed's interior washed out with Shaders on, settled in the game client: his walls are placeables whose custom vertex program picks a texture coordinate set by an MTR int, and Moonglow fed `tverts2` and `tverts3` as zeros, so such a wall drew one flat, grayish color. [Areas](manual/04-areas.md) › the toolbar's Shaders: **a custom shader reads every texture coordinate set of its model** (`vTcIn1`…`vTcIn3`; zeros for a set the model doesn't have, as in the client), the MTR's parameters as its uniforms, and **tangents only with the model's own or a render hint** (without, a custom pair's normal map draws black, as in the game). Authored cards in a `tni01` interior, drawn by Moonglow's own test pair and by Heed's, match the client (those of one texture stripe within 0.6 of 255, those of the whole texture within 3.4, as the stock pair's); the maps an MTR doesn't name are unbound in both ([findings](findings.md): What a custom shader gets of its mesh). [The shader notes](research/notes_shaders.md) B.1, [the proposal](shaders-proposal.md), [the deferred list](deferred.md) (Heed's interior to confirm in his module, Moonglow's own shader and custom vertex programs, uniforms a script sets, the extra sets in every vertex).
* **Update**: xtul's report that visual transforms aren't saved in some cases, settled in the game. Moonglow saves them as Aurora does and the engine reads every one (a private server reads back each transform Adjust Location writes, on Useable and other placeables, static ones, a creature, a door and an item, through a `.mod`, a folder and a nasher project: `engine_visual_transforms`). What was wrong was Moonglow's drawing: **X Rotation turns a model about its upright axis, Y Rotation tips it about its X and Z Rotation leans it about its Y**, made in that order outside in, and the game scales by `ScaleX` alone; Moonglow tipped the model about X for X Rotation, laying xtul's mirror flat on its desk where the game stands it upright (`visual_transforms_look`, in the client). The area view now draws transforms as the client does and its tilt rings set Y and Z Rotation. The client draws a static placeable without its transform: [Areas](manual/04-areas.md)' Adjust Location says so for static placeables, and [Verify](manual/09-build-and-test.md) warns of one with a transform (`static-transform`). [Findings](findings.md): What a visual transform's rotations turn; Visual transforms, as the game reads and draws them. [Differences](manual/12-differences.md), [the deferred list](deferred.md).
* **Update**: Heed's reports on 2.5.1 and 2.5.2, settled in the game client. [Blueprints](manual/05-blueprints.md): **a placeable's `Reflection` `default` is the game's `chrome1`** (silver), wherever it stands, as in the client: not the area's map, which Moonglow drew there (in a `tin01` interior a copper tint: Heed's armor stand); a texture's `envmaptexture default` and a creature's `ENVMAP` `default` stay the area's, as the client has them (reflective spheres painted with the map bound to them, `area_env_map_look`). The interior "darker, grayer and colder with Shaders on" was not reproduced with stock content: in seven stock interiors the game's shaders and Moonglow's own draw the same picture, near the client's, at the tests' settings, the owner's and the client's defaults; a shader include in a hak or the override changes the Shaders switch's side alone, as it changes the game ([findings](findings.md): A placeable's `Reflection` `default` is `chrome1`; The game's shaders and Moonglow's own in stock interiors). [The shader notes](research/notes_shaders.md) A.4, [the deferred list](deferred.md) (Heed's interior, the rooms the player can't see, a texture's `default` on a reflecting placeable).

* **Update**: The owner's report that Next Tile Appearance in the Tiles palette, clicked with a tile selected in Select Tiles, dropped the selection and changed nothing: [Areas](manual/04-areas.md) › Painting terrain and Tiles: **with tiles selected, the palette's Next and Previous Tile Appearance and Rotate Tile step the selected tiles at once,** each on its own, as the tile menu's items do (one step to undo; Shift + click on Rotate Tile, clockwise); the tool isn't chosen, Select Tiles and the selection stay, so another click steps them again. Refusals say why on the status line and flash red, as the menu's. With no tile selected the tools are chosen as before. Held by `palette_tile_tools_step_the_selected_tiles`, which fails without it. [The deferred list](deferred.md): Refine Tile and the Eraser are still chosen as brushes over a selection. Pictures looked at: one tile and two stepped, the selection kept; a barn's tile refused.
* **Update**: GitHub issue #28, "Built-in updates", its small part: **Help › Check for Updates…** (and a button in Help › About) asks GitHub for the latest release and says that this copy is the newest or that a newer one is out, with its release page ([The main window](manual/02-main-window.md) › Menus); a **daily check** in the background, on by default, tells of a newer release in a line under the toolbar with Don't remind me about this version, and says nothing offline; **Options › Updates** switches it off ([Options](manual/10-options.md#updates), which says what is sent: one request to api.github.com a day at most, nothing about the user). Flatpak, Scoop and Nix installs don't check by themselves and say how they are updated. Downloading and installing (checksums, signatures, Velopack, cargo-dist, self_update) and the Flatpak's network are in [the deferred list](deferred.md).

A folded pane leaves a thin strip with the same button in the same place, fully in the window (the palettes' had been mostly off-screen). The tree's «, the palettes' » among their type icons, the log's ⏷ and **⏶ Log**, and egui_dock's arrow in the palettes' tab bar, which folded the pane to its bar, are gone; View and its keys are as they were. Palettes split off the middle's left have their button on their right edge and their strip on the left. Held by `each_pane_folds_by_one_button_on_its_inner_edge` (one button a pane, on its inner edge, centered, the same folded and shown, in the window; a click at the tree's resizing edge folds it; the tab bar's arrow folds nothing) and `panels_fold_away_and_come_back`. Pictures looked at: the main window with each pane shown and folded, wide, narrow and at twice the scale, the palettes split off the left (`panes_folded` in `screens.rs`). [The deferred list](deferred.md): what is left.
* **Update**: The owner's report that the area view drew grass only around the camera's target: [Areas](manual/04-areas.md) › the toolbar's Grass: **grass is drawn wherever the view shows grass ground,** out to the far edge of the view, chosen by the camera's frustum (it was the tiles within 30 m of the view's focus); as dense as before within 45 m of the camera, thinner further off by a level's most tufts to a square meter (10 past 45 m, 5 past 90 m, 2.5 past 180 m: the tilesets' 5 all drawn out to 180 m), each level the first of a tile model's tufts (put in no order of place) from its own buffers; far off, the grass of 4 by 4 tiles is put together into one mesh on a thread of its own, the tiles drawn one by one until it is (the same tufts). Every view of a 32 by 32 field within the 144 fps budget at densities 5 and 20 with the Shaders switch on (the default); with it off, dense grass near the camera stays over it, as before. The game draws grass within 30 m of its camera by default (`Grass Render Distance`). [The plan](PLAN.md) §5.5 says grass is drawn; [the deferred list](deferred.md): the levels' bands, grass from high up, dense grass's cost with Moonglow's shader, far grass lit by the chunk, regrowing at once, tufts' blending order. Pictures looked at: a field across from low down, from halfway up, from high up and close, before and after, both shader paths.
* **Update**: The owner's report that Tile Properties' Height and Turn didn't show in the area until OK: [Areas](manual/04-areas.md) › Tiles: **every change in Tile Properties shows in the area as it is made** (lights, loops, Height, each step of a height dragged, Defaults), all of them one step to undo while the window is open (its step taken back and written again on each change: `Workspace::withdraw`, mg-edit's new call, an undo that leaves nothing to redo); **OK keeps them, Cancel takes them back** (the tiles and what stands on them byte for byte), and the close question's Don't Save takes them back. **All tiles' Turn turns the tiles chosen at once,** each its own tile and lights, put in as it is, one step a turn. A variant or a tile from the pictures is still its own step, made under the window's, which keeps its lights and height on it; Cancel then takes off only the window's, and after a command made elsewhere puts back its own fields in a step of its own ("Cancel Tile Properties"). With several tiles chosen, only the controls set are written (OK wrote every shown value into all of them). What stands on a tile raised is read from the GIT as it is (`tile_height_edits`, `AreaObject::placed`), not from the view. [The deferred list](deferred.md): what is left. Pictures looked at: a tile raised with the window open, a cliff corner turned.
