* **Update**: [The NUI Creator](manual/18-nui-creator.md), [the command line](manual/11-command-line.md) and [the deferred list](deferred.md): The Domains of Dread Design session's review of the NUI tools (their probe's section 8, on 2.1.0). **`mg nui render` and `layout` draw a window otherwise than as it opens,** with the same options so the rectangles are the picture's: `--state STATE[:ID]` (normal, hover, pressed or disabled, for one control by ID or every control; repeatable, an ID's winning; a control disabled by ID disables what is in it), `--binds FILE.json` (binds as `NuiSetBind` sets them, so a list has a character's rows; a bind no property reads, or given another kind of value, is said) and `--variant NAME` (a swap layout variant in its group). `layout` gives each control's `state` and a list row's text from its bind. **A handler's `return` fits the event script's function:** a window switched to `event_function` kept code ending a handler with `return;`, which `NAME_OnEvent()` can't compile; Build makes it `return TRUE;` in the handlers' sections only (and back, `return;`), saying the section and line (`mg nui generate`'s `returns`, the Creator's log), and a return of another type is explained beside the compiler's error. **Widths said exactly:** `mg nui schema --layout` and the manual say what a control without a width is (150 points, `NUI_STYLE_PRIMARY_WIDTH`, for buttons, fields and the like; its column's for labels, lists and layouts) and that a column is as wide as its widest control, not as the window; a new window's controls stay without widths, as the Creator's own new window and `nw_inc_nui` make them. A test fixture beside the crier: its list of cries, with binds and a variant.
* **Update**: [Build and test](manual/09-build-and-test.md), [Options](manual/10-options.md), [the command line](manual/11-command-line.md) and [the deferred list](deferred.md): the Domains of Dread builders' report (`mg verify` took about 65 s on their module and its 144 haks, `--skip tilesets,models,2das` too). **A group left out of Verify isn't checked** (`mg verify --skip`, Options › Verify): the tilesets and models skipped were all of the minute, as left-out groups were still examined and only taken out of the report. The summary line and the JSON's `skipped` now name a group left out (`null`, not counted), and `--skip` takes several separated by commas. **The rest is quicker too:** each custom tileset, 2DA, material and model is examined in parallel (a tileset's tile models too), the custom resources are found in parallel, and the shader check reads only materials and shader files (it read every custom resource, the haks' textures with them). Measured on the builders' module (a copy, with their haks): with the skips 72.9 s to 0.5 s, a whole Verify 69.9 s to 9.2 s; the findings are byte for byte the same JSON. `mg verify --timings` prints each part's time to stderr. Held by `a_group_left_out_is_not_checked` (nothing of a skipped group is read, nor a texture), `findings_examined_in_parallel_keep_their_order` and `verify_fails_on_errors_and_names_them_in_json`; what is left of the 9 s (models kept as text, read at 4 MB/s, and tile models read twice) is in the deferred list.
* **Update**: [Modules](manual/03-modules.md), [Areas](manual/04-areas.md), [Build and test](manual/09-build-and-test.md), [Command-line tools](manual/11-command-line.md), [Writing plugins](manual/16-writing-plugins.md), [Plugin API reference](manual/17-plugin-api.md), [the plugin API's changes](plugins/CHANGES.md) and [the deferred list](deferred.md): the Domains of Dread's notes on setting up their project on 2.1.0, five gaps, for 2.1.1. **Runtime-only variables:** `variables.toml`'s `runtime = true` describes a variable the scripts alone set (state, a test's hook): `undescribed` doesn't say it, the Variables window doesn't offer it (and says so when it is typed), `mg vars` marks it, and Verify's new `var-runtime` (an error, a check of its own: its remedy is to take it out) finds one kept in a file. **Struct ids:** `mg.struct_id(struct)`, read beside a `gff()` table (its metatable, where its path was), never among its fields, as any label is a GFF label (`__struct_id` too). **A plugin's tests bring their own game data:** a folder `game/` beside the test file (or `game = "…"`), read over the game's as its development folder is, so a test runs with no game; `root = "…"` names a game; a test that read what its game data lacks says which and where to put it (`Host::not_in_game`). **A list's item** in `[[test.field]]`, there or `absent`. **Tags unique in each area:** `[tags] unique_in_area`, which wins over a broader `unique` glob (TDOD's `KZ_*` takes in `KZ_SQUARE_STORE_1`), in Verify's `tag-unique` (each area said apart, its retag fix re-finding such a finding), a paste's and an area copy's warning (counted in the area made in). Plugin API still 0.4.
* **Update**: [Scripts](manual/07-scripts.md) and [Conversations](manual/06-conversations.md): Sacha's report ("in Aurora, after looking for some text, F3 would find the next occurrence"). F3 (Find Next, or the key Options › Keyboard gives it) finds the next match from the Find Text window's field and the conversation's Search pane field too, as in Aurora: it typed nothing there and went unheard until a click back into the script or the conversation. Held by `script_editor_completion_find_bookmarks` and `conversation_search_bookmarks_and_test`, which fail without it.
* **Update**: [Areas](manual/04-areas.md), [Options](manual/10-options.md) and [the deferred list](deferred.md): Jasperre's tile feedback. **Tile Properties shows only the tile's own lights and loops,** as Aurora does (seen on the oracle: a light or loop the tile's model hasn't is greyed out, a loop unchecked; several tiles, what any has): read from the model (`<model>ml1`/`ml2` light nodes, `<model>sl1`/`sl2` nodes, `animloop01..03` animations: `mg_area::TileFeatures`), since the game's tilesets flag all seven on every tile; each tile is written only what its own model has, a value in the file for a light it hasn't kept. **Closed with changes not applied** (its ✕, Escape) it asks Save, Don't Save or Cancel, as a side editor's tab does (Aurora's ✕ discards silently); its Cancel discards. **A group or a feature is one thing:** Next and Previous Tile Appearance, Rotate Tile, Refine Tile, Shift + click, Shift + right click and Tile Properties' variants leave its tiles as they are (the status line says why and how, also in Select Tiles now, and its tiles flash red); a group placed over part of another is refused, as Aurora refuses it (it took the other away); Delete takes it whole, as the Eraser does; painting terrain that would change its tiles was already refused. Membership is worked out from the tileset's groups (`TileIndex::is_grouped`, `group_at`): the area's file doesn't record it. **Tools › Options › Area › Allow editing tiles of groups and features** (off) lets each tile change alone (`Grid::free_groups`), with a warning on the page and on the status line when a change leaves a group part changed. The plugin API's `place_group` keeps its documented replacing. Pictures looked at: Tile Properties of tin01 tiles 18 (one light) and 134 (two lights, three loops), the closing question, a barn over a barn refused, the Area page.
* **Update**: [Plugins](manual/15-plugins.md), [Writing plugins](manual/16-writing-plugins.md), [the plugin API reference](manual/17-plugin-api.md), [the command line](manual/11-command-line.md), [the plugin API changes](plugins/CHANGES.md), [the plugins' guide](plugins/README.md), [the plugin proposal](plugin-proposal.md) and [the deferred list](deferred.md): The Domains of Dread's builder proposal (A4, A6), the last of plugin API 0.4. **A4, a resource's own events:** `resource-saving` calls a plugin's handler for each resource a save would write, with `ctx.event.old` and `new` (plain values, or text) and `changed` (a GFF's differences as lines); it warns (`ctx.event:warn`) or refuses (`ctx.event:refuse`), each with a fix made with `ctx.fix`, and the new Before saving window shows them before anything is written (Save Anyway or Cancel; a refusal stops the save; a fix is made as a step of Undo and the save asked again). `resource-saved` reads each resource written, after. `object-placed` and `object-pasted` give the objects placed or pasted. **A6, the after-create hook:** `resource-created` for each resource the Area Wizard, the creature and blueprint wizards, Add to Palette, Setup Store or a copy makes. **The decision,** recorded in the reference and the proposal's Safety: a plugin edits only on the user's word, so the handlers of a placing, a paste or a making may edit, their edits joining the user's own command (one Undo takes both back; Moonglow's own work, the builder code and the project's store rules, first), while around a save a plugin only reads, warns, refuses and offers fixes. The events that run within what the user does have 5 seconds a plugin and nobody to ask; one that fails is left out and said, and before a save is a warning, never a refusal. `mg set` and `mg apply` take `--plugins` (and the project's allowed plugins) and run a save's events (a refusal is an error); `mg plugin run --event` takes `--was`, `--select` and `--by`; a plugin's tests take `was`, `by`, `warned` and `refused`. New example: `house-rules`. The deferred list's entry on one event a save and none per resource is closed, with what A4 and A6 left undone.
* **Update**: [Areas](manual/04-areas.md), [Coming from Aurora](manual/14-coming-from-aurora.md), [the deferred list](deferred.md) and [the EE fields notes](research/notes_ee_fields.md): a builder's tile feedback. A right click on a tile opens the tile menu without Select Tiles: with a terrain brush, the Eraser, Refine Tile or the new tile tools (the brush kept; Escape or the menu's Put Down lets it go, where Aurora's right click did), and out of Select Tiles on the ground with no object there, where the area's menu starts with the tile's items (as Aurora's has Tile Properties for a tile). Select Tiles is also at the top of the Tiles palette's tools, and turning it on puts the brush down. New tools in the Tiles palette and the tile menu (which replace its Next and Previous Variant; Shift + right click still cycles): Next and Previous Tile Appearance (another tile of the same terrain and crossers, turned as before where it fits) and Rotate Tile (a quarter turn, Shift the other way, only where the same tile still fits its neighbors, keeping its lights); each one undoable step (`mg_tiles::paint::step_appearance`, `turn_fit`). Refine Tile's tip says what it does: Aurora's order, look and rotation together, not random. Tile Properties loses Replacement Texture (BioWare's unused `Tile_ReplaceTex`: no tileset of the game's has a tile for it, no campaign module writes it, the client not seen drawing it; kept in files and the raw fields), and its variants get a Size slider (64 to 224 points, remembered) and a larger picture on hover.
* **Update**: [Main window](manual/02-main-window.md), [Blueprints](manual/05-blueprints.md), [the command line](manual/11-command-line.md), [findings](findings.md) and [the deferred list](deferred.md): two of dansen9191's reports on 2.0.0. **The resource browser lists every copy:** of a portraits.2da in two haks it listed only the one the game reads (`ResMan::entries` keeps the highest copy of each resource). Each copy that copy overrides is now listed under it, set in and dimmed, "hak:low, overridden by hak:top" (on by default; unticking **Overridden copies** hides them); one opens read-only as its layer has it (a 2DA from that copy down), is copied to the module or saved, and a table in one of the builder's own haks has **Edit in name.hak**. Export as Files writes only the copies the game reads. `mg which` names every copy too, and `--module` puts the module's haks in its load order (it read the game's alone). **An armor's parts can be 0:** the part lists were the parts_*.2da rows with an `ACBONUS`, and the game's tables leave row 0's blank in most of them; every part offers **0 (none)** first now, as a creature's body parts did. In the client, armor whose parts are 0 shows the wearer's own parts there (the finding "An armor's part 0 leaves its wearer's own part"), as the previews already drew it; deferred: the armor class of a torso 0, a head of 0, and what a copy's tab doesn't do.
* **Update**: [Modules](manual/03-modules.md), [Main window](manual/02-main-window.md), [Areas](manual/04-areas.md), [Build and test](manual/09-build-and-test.md), [the command line](manual/11-command-line.md) and [the deferred list](deferred.md): The Domains of Dread's builder proposal (C6, C7, C8), with the hand-back proposal's sections 8.2 to 8.4. **C6, claimed areas:** `moonglow.toml`'s `[claims] file` names the team's claims table, a 2DA in the checkout as the hand-back proposed (`Area`, `Builder`, `Status` claimed/free/merged, `Date`, `Tag`; other columns kept). With a builder code, the module tree marks each claimed area (✔ yours, 🔒 another's, 📌 merged, who and since when on hover); an area another builder holds says so above its view and Properties and is either kept from every change (`others = "read-only"`: `Moonglow::apply` refuses it, whatever window asks) or, by default (`"warn"`), a save that would write a change to it asks first (**Areas Not Yours**, Save Anyway); `unclaimed = "warn"` treats an unclaimed area the same. **Claim This Area** and **Release Claim** (the area's menu in the module tree and on its tab) change one row's line of the table, or add one, leaving every other line as written, and read it again at once. `mg claims PROJECT` lists, `claim`, `release` (`--force` for another's) and `merged` (recording the Tag) write it. **C7, protected names:** an area the table says is merged, or `[claims] protected` names, asks before its Tag changes, before it is renamed or deleted (**Protected Area**: Make a Copy Instead…, or change it anyway); Verify's `claims-merged` and `claims-tag` (errors) find a merged area renamed or deleted, or with another Tag than the table records. **C8, the project's commands:** `[commands]` names the team's commands (`update = "bin/update"`, or a table with `windows`, `unix` and `label`), run from **File › Project**: the module saved first (asked as on closing), the command allowed the first time and whenever its text changes (**Run a Project Command?**, kept in Moonglow's own settings by the project folder's canonical path, the command's name and its exact text, never in the project's files, which a repository could carry: an `[allowed_commands]` found there is said and not read, `commands_allowed_by_a_committed_user_file_are_still_asked_about`; never run on open), the folder's watch stopped and the haks closed so that a checkout may write over them, the command run in the project's folder by `sh -c` or `cmd /C` with `MOONGLOW_PROJECT` and `MOONGLOW_BUILDER`, its output in the log as it comes and its exit code said, then the haks opened again and the module's files read again as changes made outside are (C12's path), the claims table with them. Held by unit tests of the table and the rules (`claims`, `project_file`), `a_merged_area_renamed_or_retagged_is_found`, the CLI's `claims_are_listed_claimed_released_and_merged`, and UI tests of read-only and warned areas, Claim and Release, the protected-name question, and a command run after it is allowed. What is left is on the deferred list.
* **Update**: [Plugins](manual/15-plugins.md), [Writing plugins](manual/16-writing-plugins.md), [the plugin API](manual/17-plugin-api.md), [Modules](manual/03-modules.md) (the project file's `[plugins]`), [Command-line tools](manual/11-command-line.md), [the API's changes](plugins/CHANGES.md), [the plugin authors' page](plugins/README.md), [the plugins proposal](plugin-proposal.md) and [the deferred list](deferred.md): The Domains of Dread's builder proposal (A2, A5), plugin API 0.4. A project's own plugins: those in its `plugins/` (or `[plugins] folder` of `moonglow.toml`) run only once the builder allows each, asked when the project is opened with what each adds and keeps; the answer is kept in Moonglow's own folder (`project-plugins.toml`, by the project's canonical path, the plugin's id and a SHA-256 digest of its files, which it runs from), never read from the project (a `[project_plugins]` section a checkout carries is said and allows nothing), so a changed plugin is asked about again and a no stays a no; `mg plugin allow` answers from a terminal; Manage Plugins lists them as the project's. `mg verify` runs them as allowed, or every one with `--allow-project-plugins` (a build pipeline); `mg plugin list --project` lists them. Settings: `[setting]` in the manifest (string, int, bool, choice, path; scope user, project or user-project), read as `ctx.settings` and set with `ctx.settings:set`, kept in Moonglow's settings, the team's `moonglow.toml` (`[plugins."<id>"]`) or the builder's `.moonglow/user.toml`; a form in Manage Plugins (Settings…); `mg plugin run --setting`, a test's `settings` and `set`. New example: `builder-tags`.
* **Update**: [Writing plugins](manual/16-writing-plugins.md), [the plugin API](manual/17-plugin-api.md), [Plugins](manual/15-plugins.md), [Build and test](manual/09-build-and-test.md), [Command-line tools](manual/11-command-line.md), [the API's changes](plugins/CHANGES.md) and [the deferred list](deferred.md): The Domains of Dread's builder proposal (A3, A8), plugin API 0.4. Quick fixes: a check gives a finding a fix with `ctx.fix(label)` (`ctx.edit`'s methods, recording edits on a copy of the module as the check reads it); Verify Module shows a button beside the finding and makes the edits only when it is clicked, as one step of Undo, and only while what they change is as Verify read it (`mg_edit::fix::Guard`). Moonglow's own `tag-unique` (the others retagged) and `area-variables` (moved to the area's GIT) have fixes; `pc-faction` has none, the faction being the builder's choice. `mg verify --json` gives each finding's fix as an edit file, and `mg verify --fix CHECK` makes a check's fixes (`--dry-run`). A plugin's own tests: `tests/*.toml` in its folder name a fixture module (a folder, a .mod or a nasher project) and the checks, commands and events to run on it with what is to come of each (findings and their fixes, fixes made and findings left, fields, edits, the log, an error); `mg plugin test` runs them with a line a test, a summary and a failing exit for a build pipeline, and `mg plugin pack` leaves `tests/` out. The `tag-conventions` example (now API 0.4) has a fix for each finding and tests of its own on a nasher project of JSON files.
* **Update**: [Modules](manual/03-modules.md) and [the deferred list](deferred.md): The Domains of Dread's builder proposal (C12), large checkouts reload without stalling. A `git pull` on a large nasher project (the persistent world kept as one, 21,000 files; 970 rewritten, added or deleted) held the window for 1.6 s in one frame: every file's time looked at and the changed ones read and converted, on the window's thread. Now the watch waits for a burst of changes to settle, a thread of its own reads what changed (`mg_module::outside`: what is known of the files taken, looked at anywhere, what still stands taken back; changes to files Moonglow saved or took meanwhile, and files found deleted that are back, are left for the next look; a file is taken with the time it had when it was read, so a write after that is found), areas' locks are found there too, and the window takes it all at once; the game data's module layer is changed in place, its tables forgotten only for a 2DA, and a project's files' times are noted at open. Conflicts are asked about as before. Longest frame 1,630 ms before, 3.5 ms after (`frame_perf` `reload_frames_at_144`, new), all in 4.6 s from the pull's start; a pull of 2,870 files (every area, 2,000 blueprints): 6.5 ms, all in 6.2 s; the status bar says *Reading files changed outside Moonglow…* meanwhile. Tests: `a_pull_s_burst_of_changes_is_read_off_the_window_s_thread` (UI), `a_look_apart_hands_back_only_what_still_stands` (mg-module). What is left is on the deferred list.
* **Update**: [Modules](manual/03-modules.md), [Main window](manual/02-main-window.md), [Areas](manual/04-areas.md), [Blueprints](manual/05-blueprints.md), [the command line](manual/11-command-line.md) and [the deferred list](deferred.md): The Domains of Dread's builder proposal (C1, C2). **C1, a builder code:** `[builder] code` in a builder's `.moonglow/user.toml` (set by **Edit › Builder Code…** or `mg builder-code PROJECT CODE`, which keep the rest of the file and make the folder's `.gitignore`) begins the ResRefs Moonglow proposes, by the team's `[resrefs] pattern` (`{code}_{name}` by default) in `moonglow.toml`: the Area Wizard (which then shows the ResRef), the Creature Wizard and the blueprint wizards, Add to Palette, Setup Store's conversation and script, Copy… and Edit Copy…, the Script Wizard. The name is cut to keep the code within 16 characters, and a name that has the builder's code already doesn't get it twice. **C2, described variables:** a project's `variables.toml` (beside `moonglow.toml`, in the manner of `name.2da.toml`) gives each local variable (by name or glob) its type, values (written, a script's constants or a table's rows), range, the objects it goes on and a line of help; `undescribed` names the project's own, for typos. The Variables window offers the described names as one is typed, sets the type of the one chosen, offers its values, shows its help, and says what is wrong under the list without keeping OK from writing. Verify's new checks `var-type` (an error), `var-value`, `var-object` and `var-name`; `mg vars` lists the descriptions and `mg areas --var` takes a described type. An engine test (`engine_local_variables`) settles what C2 rests on: a variable read as another type is that type's empty value, and names are told apart by case.
* **Update**: [The shaders proposal](shaders-proposal.md): released in Moonglow Toolset 2.0.0, and M6 done: Moonglow Viewer 0.2.0 is on toolset 2.0.0 with the game's shaders (a Shaders switch in its window, on by default; `mgv`'s `--no-game-shaders`). The plan is complete.
* **Update**: [Modules](manual/03-modules.md), [the command line](manual/11-command-line.md) and [the deferred list](deferred.md): The Domains of Dread's builder proposal (C9), a three-way GFF merge usable as git's merge driver. **`mg merge-gff BASE OURS THEIRS`** (git's `%O %A %B`, with `--path %P`) merges two copies of a GFF resource made from a third, as binary GFF or nasher's JSON (told by content: git hands a driver files without their names), and writes the merge over OURS in OURS's form. A value one side changed is taken; two different changes are a conflict named by its path (`/Placeable List[plc_chest "CHEST" at 10, 4.5]/Tag`): exit 1 and nothing written, unless `--prefer ours` or `theirs`. Lists merge by item, by a rule per list (`mg_schema::merge::list_rule`; the engine is `mg_gff::merge`): an area's placed objects by object (the same in every field, then `ObjectId`, then blueprint, tag and position, then the only one left of its blueprint and tag, or of its blueprint and position; both sides' additions kept), `Mod_Area_list` as a union (the base's order, ours' additions, theirs'), `VarTable` by name and type, feats, classes, spells, haks, journal entries and faction pairs by what they are, equipped items by slot, anything else by place. A merge equal to OURS leaves its bytes, one equal to THEIRS is THEIRS's bytes, any other JSON is written as nasher writes it (the project's float places and codepage). The manual's new **Merging in git** has the `.gitattributes` and `.git/config` lines. A test sets up a scratch repository and merges two builders' branches, each adding an object to one area and an area to `module.ifo`, which git alone calls a conflict (the hand-back's measurement). What's left (inventories and conversations merged by place, reordering, strings per language, a conflict file, NWNT) is in the deferred list.
* **Update**: [Modules](manual/03-modules.md), [areas](manual/04-areas.md), [the command line](manual/11-command-line.md), [differences from Aurora](manual/12-differences.md) and [the deferred list](deferred.md): The Domains of Dread's builder proposal (C10, C11, C13). **`mg haks --usage`** says, hak by hak, which resources the module reaches and which nothing does (used, unused, shadowed by a higher layer, not judged), with bytes, and with `--json` each used resource with the module resource that reached it first: the walk follows Verify's references, scripts' includes and string literals, areas' tilesets (the tiles placed, their walkmeshes and map images), skyboxes, loading screens, ambient sounds and music, objects' looks as the area view draws them (placeables' and doors' sounds, creatures' bodies, portraits and sound sets, items' icons, armor parts for every wearer) and every model's files (`mg_preview::files::Gatherer`, which no longer reads a texture to learn its type), against the game a player has (no override). On The Domains of Dread (3 areas, 143 haks, 29.5 GB) 4–5 s and 417 MB: 1,957 of 236,209 hak resources used (427 MB), nothing reaching 125 haks (23 of them holding what is not judged). **Export stamps its base**: `mg export` and File › Export write the module's git commit (and whether files were uncommitted), Moonglow's version and the module's name after the ERF's description (an extra resource would be imported into the module); `mg import` and File › Import show it and warn when the module's checkout is at another commit (older work, other work, a commit it doesn't have). **`MG_Locked` kept out of a nasher project's files**: the window keeps a project's locks in Moonglow's data folder (`locks/`), each found again by list place, blueprint, tag and position, and puts them back on opening and on reading an area changed outside (the files still stand for the area: no false conflict); a file that has the byte keeps it while the object stays locked. Module folders and `.mod` files keep the byte. Held by `a_module_reaches_into_its_haks`, `usage_says_what_the_module_reaches_in_each_hak`, `the_commit_is_stamped_and_compared`, `the_stamp_is_in_the_description`, `a_project_keeps_its_locks_apart` and `a_project_s_locks_stay_out_of_its_files`.
* **Update**: [Modules](manual/03-modules.md), [areas](manual/04-areas.md), [build and test](manual/09-build-and-test.md), [command-line tools](manual/11-command-line.md), [findings](findings.md) and [the deferred list](deferred.md): The Domains of Dread's builder proposal (C3, C4, C5). **The project file**: a `moonglow.toml` beside `nasher.cfg` (or at a module folder's root) holds a team's rules, read when the module opens and again when it changes; what it doesn't understand is said by file and line, and a builder's own settings go in `.moonglow/user.toml`, a folder that keeps itself out of version control. **Make Placeables Static** leaves dynamic a placeable with variables, and those the project's `[make_static]` names, and says why for each, from one function the window and `mg areas` share. The engine settled that a static placeable is still an object to scripts (found by its tag, its variables read, made Plot), which the client does not show changing (`engine_static_placeables`). **Duplicate tags**: a paste or an area's Copy… that puts a tag on a second object says so and offers **Retag**; by default (`paste_warning = "custom"`, the owner's decision) only of tags a builder set (not the object's blueprint's own) and those that must be unique, never of a static placeable's, so copies of scenery ask nothing (the tag with a number, unique in the module, one undo); `[tags] unique` names tags Verify holds to one object (`tag-unique`). **Setup Store** follows `[store]`: the project's open script (no `openstoreNNN`), the store's tag pattern made unique, the blueprint chosen at first, and the shopkeeper's variable with the store's tag; without it, as Aurora's.
* **Update**: [Writing plugins](manual/16-writing-plugins.md), [the plugin API reference](manual/17-plugin-api.md), [Plugins](manual/15-plugins.md), [the API change list](plugins/CHANGES.md), [plugins for authors](plugins/README.md), [the plugin proposal](plugin-proposal.md) and [the deferred list](deferred.md): plugin API 0.4, the first item (A1) of The Domains of Dread's builder proposal, the owner's decision to make it API 0.4, still experimental. **`ctx.selection`** is what a command is run on: each entry says what it is (`kind`, `blueprint`, `module`), where (`resource` and `path`, as `ctx.edit` takes them, so one handler serves a placed object and a blueprint; `area`, `index`) and its `tag` and `resref`. **`ctx.open`** is the area or editor in front. Both are read as `ctx.module` is, with nothing asked (reads are not granted; only folders, haks and confirmations are). **A command's `for`** in the manifest (`"placeable, creature"`, `"area"`) puts it in those kinds' right-click menus (the area view's on the objects selected, or the area with nothing selected; the module tree's object, blueprint and area rows; a palette's blueprints), after the menu's own commands and above Variables and Properties, run on what was clicked. From the Plugins menu or a key it gets the area view's selection, else the palette's. 0.1 to 0.3 plugins run unchanged; `for` under an earlier `api` is refused with the reason. `mg plugin run` takes `--select` and `--open`. A new example, `set-local`, and the type file's `Selected`. Held by `a_command_says_what_it_is_for`, `a_plugin_reads_what_is_selected_and_open`, `set_local_acts_on_the_selection`, `a_plugin_s_command_runs_on_what_is_selected` (mg) and three UI tests (the module tree, a palette, the area view, whose menu was drawn to a picture and looked at). What is left is in the deferred list ("Commands on what is selected").
* **Update**: [The NUI Creator](manual/18-nui-creator.md), [the command line](manual/11-command-line.md), [the main window](manual/02-main-window.md), [the doctor's notes](research/notes_doctor.md) and [the deferred list](deferred.md): The Domains of Dread's NUI Creator asks (the module project's probe of how agents could make its windows, seven items the owner approved). **`mg nui render`** draws a window into a PNG as the Creator's canvas does (the game's skin, pictures and fonts, `--scale`, `--size`, `-o`), and **`mg nui layout`** gives each control's rectangle in the window's points and whether it is clipped, out of view, without room, overlapping or cutting its text: both are one frame of the canvas itself (`preview::still`, which notes where it put each control), so they agree with each other and with the editor (`the_layout_off_the_screen_is_the_canvas_s`, `a_window_drawn_off_the_screen_is_where_its_layout_says`); a `.jui` file alone works too. Seen in pictures: a new window, and the town crier's window at 100% and 150%, whose fixed-width column used half the window and whose first label was cut, as `layout` said. **A nasher project that leaves windows out** (sources `src/**/*.{nss,json}`): `mg nui new`, `validate` and `generate`, New NUI Window, a save and Verify's new `nui-sources` check say so with the `include` line to add. **The settings file** (`moonglow.nui/1`) has a section: every key, the defaults, the crier's settings as the example; **`mg nui schema`** prints it as a JSON Schema made beside `Settings` and held to it by tests (`--layout`: the Creator's controls). **`mg nui import`** brings windows (layout, settings, event script's source) from files, folders, ERFs and zips into a module. **AccessKit is on** in the GUI for screen readers and desktop agents: built only while one is connected (0.10 ms a frame more on average then, 0.55 at most, over the campaign's views), the Flatpak may ask for it (`org.a11y.Bus`); on Linux it waits for `ScreenReaderEnabled`, which at-spi2-core 2.62 no longer has, so it starts there only with a later eframe. **One event script for several windows**: a window's event script can be an include with `NAME_OnEvent()` for the module's own OnNuiEvent dispatcher (Bindings › Client delivery; `event_function` in the settings).

## 2026-10-21
* **Update**: [Scripts](manual/07-scripts.md), [the main window](manual/02-main-window.md), [options](manual/10-options.md) and [the deferred list](deferred.md): El Grillo's three script-editor requests. **Ctrl+S saves the script, not the module** ("When I try to save when editing a script I would expect the script to save, rather than the entire module"): the Save key, while a script editor's text has the keyboard (the rule its Undo and Redo go by), is that script's toolbar Save (its text into the module, compiled with Automatically Compile Scripts on Save, the Compiler tab's note when not), through the keymap, so a Save key chosen in Options › Keyboard does the same; elsewhere, and File › Save always, it saves the module. As in Aurora, whose script editor has its own Save on Ctrl+S (`TdlgScriptEditor`'s `actSave`, the [inventory](parity/aurora-ui-inventory.md)). The manual says what a script's Save writes: no file; File › Save writes the `.mod`, or the script's own file in a module folder or nasher project. **Open Script selects several and opens them together** ("single-click should not open, but just select them"): a click selects, Ctrl+click adds or takes away, Shift+click selects a range, the arrows move the selection (Shift widens it), Open or Enter opens every one selected, each in its tab, and a double click opens that one (`widgets::select_in_list`, the hak editor's rule). Seen in a picture (`open_script_several` in `screens.rs`). **To Scratch for a script that doesn't compile** ("e.g. include scripts"): the source is copied whatever the compiler says, its compiled script only when it compiled just now (not the module's older one), and the Compiler tab says what was copied, that it wasn't compiled (an include file, or the compiler's error) and whether the folder holds an earlier compiled script of the name. Held by `the_save_key_in_a_script_editor_saves_the_script_not_the_module`, `open_script_opens_the_scripts_selected_together` and `a_script_goes_to_the_scratch_folder_compiled`.
* **Update**: [Main window](manual/02-main-window.md), [areas](manual/04-areas.md) and [blueprints](manual/05-blueprints.md): Jasperre's report, the owner's decision. **Properties** is the last item of every right-click menu that has one, as Aurora's menus end (`pmViewerArea`, `pmMainArea`), with **Variables…** just before it where the menu has that, the two set off from the rest by a separator: a placed object's menu in the area view (Properties was first, Variables… among the type's items; now after Delete, Set Start Location Here and the Test From Here items), a tile's (**Tile Properties…** after Next Variant, Previous Variant and Delete), a placed object's row in the module tree (Go To, Preview, Copy, Delete, then Variables…, Properties), an area's row there (Properties after Copy to Scratch Folder), an inventory row's (Copy, Paste, Remove, then Properties) and an equipped item's (To Backpack, Copy, Remove, then Properties). Every other item keeps its order and what it does. Seen in pictures before and after (`context_menus` in `screens.rs`) and held by `properties_is_the_last_item_of_the_area_and_tree_menus` and `properties_is_the_last_item_of_an_inventory_row_s_menu`.
* **Update**: [Scripts](manual/07-scripts.md), [options](manual/10-options.md) and [the deferred list](deferred.md): Jasperre's report on the script editor, with the owner's decisions. The error found as you type has a line of its own under the toolbar, there with no error too (one line, the whole message on hover), so the text no longer jumps down as an error comes and goes; **Options › Script Editor › Check for errors as you type** turns the check off; the error's own line alone is underlined (tokens of one color are one section over several lines, and the lines around it were underlined too: seen in a picture). Tab puts in four spaces by default, to the next multiple of the width (egui's own tab no longer gets in), or a tab: **Indent with** Spaces or Tabs and **Indent width**; Shift+Tab outdents a step, Tab on several lines indents them (empty lines stay empty). **Background** joins the editor's colors. Help shows a function's documentation as the source has it (`//`, spaces and columns, in the editor's colors and font, then the declaration), from `doc_source` kept beside `doc` in mg-script's spec, outline and analysis. The Compiler tab's messages start with the local time they were said, and a Save that doesn't compile says so there (jiff, a new dependency, reads the time zone). A CRLF script's `\r` was in the editor's text at every line's end, where the cursor stopped and a copy took it ("trailing spaces" on every line): the editor and the read-only view work with plain line ends, and a script is written back with its own (untouched byte for byte; new lines in the file's kind; a file with both kinds keeps the ends around the change), pasted CRLF text included; Rename Symbol and every view of a script's text count positions in the same form. Seen in pictures before and after (`script_error_line`, `script_help`, `options_window` in `screens.rs`) and held by `a_crlf_script_has_no_carriage_returns_in_its_editor`, `a_save_that_does_not_compile_says_so`, `help_shows_documentation_as_the_source_has_it`, `tab_indents_selected_lines_in_the_script_editor` and `script_navigation_definition_references_rename_and_live_errors` (CRLF files, the editor's top before and after an error).
* **Update**: [Blueprints](manual/05-blueprints.md), [journal and factions](manual/08-journal-and-factions.md), [build and test](manual/09-build-and-test.md), [differences from Aurora](manual/12-differences.md), [findings](findings.md), [the GFF notes](research/notes_gff_changelog.md), [the doctor's notes](research/notes_doctor.md) and [the deferred list](deferred.md): Jasperre's two reports. **A placeable's illumination**: the game keeps it in `LightState` (a byte Aurora neither shows nor writes), settled in the engine (`engine_light_state.rs`): read from a placed placeable's GIT entry and from a blueprint created by script, 0 off, any other value on, without the field on; a placeable whose appearance has no light is never lit. A placeable's Advanced page has **Illuminated**, written only when changed (an untouched placeable keeps no field), grayed for an appearance without a light (`mg_rules::placeables`). **No PC faction for objects**: settled in the engine (`engine_factions.rs`), a creature of faction 0, placed or created, is put in Hostile as it loads; a placeable, door or trigger keeps 0 and creatures regard it as they regard players. The faction lists (creatures, placeables, doors, triggers, encounters) and the Store Wizard's leave it out; a file that has it keeps it, shown as "PC (not for objects)" until another is picked; Verify warns of it (`pc-faction`, in the objects group; none in the shipped modules or the game's blueprints). Seen in pictures (`illumination_and_pc_faction` in `screens.rs`); held by `a_placeable_s_illumination_is_written_only_when_changed`, `an_object_s_factions_leave_out_the_pc_faction`, `objects_of_the_pc_faction_are_warned_of` and `verify_warns_of_an_object_of_the_pc_faction`. Drawing the light off, and what is left of faction 0, are in the deferred list. Verify leaves triggers out of `pc-faction` (the owner's decision): Aurora writes faction 0 for a trigger whose blueprint has none.
* **Update**: [Options](manual/10-options.md), [differences from Aurora](manual/12-differences.md) and [the deferred list](deferred.md): El Grillo's question ("is there a way to have Moonglow load userpatch content?"), the owner's decision: **Read userpatch.ini's haks** (Options › General), off by default as in Aurora (Jasperre's ask: a subscribed item or a userpatch hak a builder doesn't want in the module makes a mess); **Read Steam Workshop content**, which was on by default, is off by default too (the setting is now `read_workshop`, read anew: off for everyone until turned on). The haks the user's `userpatch.ini` names (`[Patch]`, `PatchFile000=…`, the highest number winning) are read from the folder `nwn.ini` aliases as `PATCH` (else `patch/` in the user folder), at the game's priority for them: under the module and its haks, over `override/` (`GameInstall::userpatch`, `userpatch_haks`; layers `userpatch:NAME`). Aurora doesn't read them. Verify reports a reference only they satisfy as a warning (`Missing::only_userpatch`: "only in userpatch.ini's haks (players without them won't have it)"; `mg verify --json` has it as `only_userpatch`), and no longer checks their files as the module's custom content. `mg` doesn't read them, as it doesn't read the Workshop. Held by `userpatch_haks_are_read_when_asked_for` and `a_reference_only_a_userpatch_hak_has_is_a_warning`.
* **Update**: [Scripts](manual/07-scripts.md): Sacha's report ("in the script editor, the keys for page up or down do not work"). Page Down and Page Up move the cursor by the lines in sight, in the same column (or at a shorter line's end; past the first line the text's start, past the last its end), and scroll the text as far, so the cursor keeps its place on the screen; with Shift they select. egui's text field has no page keys of its own. Seen in pictures (`script_page_down` in `screens.rs`) and held by `page_keys_move_through_the_script_editor` and `script_tools::line_moved`'s test.
* **Update**: [The shaders proposal](shaders-proposal.md) ("After M5: the lights") and [the deferred list](deferred.md): M5's light-dense water view brought back within the budget with the Shaders switch on, 7.5 to 6.5 ms a frame (off 5.6; area092 zoomed out 5.7–6.1 to 5.2, off 4.0), so M3's rule holds again and the default stays on. The time was the CPU's, writing the game draws' values: a draw's lights built and hashed per draw and its 1,040-byte lights block written an element at a time, 1,524 such blocks a frame. The game's programs now read `lightPosition[i]` and `lightColor[i]` by the draw's places in the frame's lights (`mg_shader`'s `index_lights`, a SPIR-V pass: 144-byte blocks, the same values, zeros past a draw's lights as before); a draw's lights are written as indices, a vector at a time; its model-view matrix worked out once; the light sets hashed eight bytes at a time; the regions indexed by number. `MAX_NUM_LIGHTS` stays the client's 32 (the loops are bounded by `numLights`). The lit oracle and the water area's views are unchanged; area092's at a fixed clock too. Every stock program that reads the lights reads them by index (7 of 65; TDoD's 26 of 102). What is left (0.9 ms of encoding, the places not yet run on Direct3D 12 or Metal) is in the deferred list.
* **Update**: [The shaders proposal](shaders-proposal.md) (M5 built), [the shader notes](research/notes_shaders.md) (§A.3, §A.4 and §J: the special surfaces read in the client), [areas](manual/04-areas.md) and [the deferred list](deferred.md): water, grass, the skybox, additive meshes, decals with an envmap and particles marked in the client (`surfaces_in_the_client`). The skybox (`vsvt`/`fst`, at the far plane), the area's grass (`vsgrass`/`fsgrass`), additive meshes and particles (`vsparticle`/`fsparticle`, by blend) draw with the game's programs; shiny water stays Moonglow's, as `fswater` without the framebuffer draws it opaque and pale (TDoD's fountain 16.4 of 255 from the client against 11.7, a rural lake 19.4 against 12.6). On is as close as off for every surface's region, closer on the grass. See-through game draws are batched (a water-and-fires area 7.9 to 7.5 ms, still over the budget as at M4; area092 6.2 to 5.8). M4's macOS failure fixed: the cube map's level worked out in the shader, not read with gradients.
* **Update**: [The shaders proposal](shaders-proposal.md) (M4 built), [the shader notes](research/notes_shaders.md) (§A.4 settled in the client, §B, §C, §M.10 and §M.12 answered), [areas](manual/04-areas.md), [differences from Aurora](manual/12-differences.md) and [the deferred list](deferred.md): every mesh drawn by the game's shaders with the program the engine chooses and its material's NO_DISCARD, read from the client by marking every stock program in a scratch override (`program_choice_in_the_client`: Moonglow chooses as the client for each of 34 kinds of mesh read). What the engine ignores, Moonglow's own shader now ignores too: `bumpyshinytexture`, maps without a render hint, a DXT1's alpha; every lit mesh reflects the area's map; a tile's `rotatetexture` turns its texture. Moonglow's shader samples the environment map as the game does (`_sm` 0.73 to 0.00 of 255 from the stock programs). A hak's `inc_lighting` changes the area view through the load order (TDoD: 259,475 of 330,000 pixels). Against the client's reference scenes, the switch on is as close as off on the stock scenes and closer on TDoD's room; TDoD's woods seen whole 52 ms on (56 at M3), 28 off.
* **Update**: [The shaders proposal](shaders-proposal.md) (M3 built), [areas](manual/04-areas.md) and [the deferred list](deferred.md): the **🎨 Shaders** switch within the frame budget and **on by default** (`GAME_SHADERS_BY_DEFAULT`; a builder's choice is kept). A program's uniforms are split in IR into a draw's, its lights', the frame's (written once a frame) and the bones'; the game's draws are instances of storage arrays their places pick, as Moonglow's own draws are; the programs are compiled and their pipelines made on a worker thread (an area draws with Moonglow's shader until they are ready) and kept compiled in Moonglow's cache folder. On the campaign's largest area zoomed out, 5.97 ms a frame on (12.37 before M3), 3.97 off; every view within 144 fps off stays within it on. The Domains of Dread's synthetic 24 by 24 area seen whole is over the budget either way (54 ms on, 29 off): in the deferred list. CI's platforms run an authored pair (`an_authored_glsl_pair_draws_on_this_platform_s_gpu`) and print the adapter. With the default on, `frames_at_144` (the campaign's views) has nothing over the budget; the Gallery's first pictures wait for the pipelines (deferred).
* **Update**: [The shaders proposal](shaders-proposal.md) (M2 built), [areas](manual/04-areas.md), [the main window](manual/02-main-window.md), [the shader notes](research/notes_shaders.md) §A.4 and §B.4, and [the deferred list](deferred.md): tier A of the game's shaders. A **🎨 Shaders** switch on the area view's toolbar and in the model viewer (one setting for every 3D view, off by default until M3 meets the frame budget: `GAME_SHADERS_BY_DEFAULT`) draws a mesh whose material names `customshaderVS` and `customshaderFS` with that pair, compiled from the module's load order, and the rest with the stock lit pair; see-through meshes in Moonglow's two parts (the fragment program discards by its alpha in IR), the MTR's parameters filled by name, the clock, and the area's `Flags` and `WindPower` as `areaFlags` and `areaGlobalWind`; tangents made where a model has none (the stock normal-mapped programs drew such a mesh black). A material naming one program alone draws with the stock pair, as the game does (250 of The Domains of Dread's name `fslit_nm` alone as `customshaderVS`); Verify's new `shader-pair` warns of them, and `shader-compile` no longer calls them magenta. A program that fails is drawn with Moonglow's shader and said once in the log. TDoD's grass sways, its fountain's water slides and its roof shader shows a roof by the camera's height, with the switch on only; beside the client, the floor of the reference room under TDoD's `inc_lighting` is 73.4 against the client's 73.5 with the switch on (87.9 off), the fountain differs from the client by 11.7 of 255 on average with the switch on (13.8 off; its shiny water is Moonglow's). What it leaves is in the deferred list.
* **Update**: [The shaders proposal](shaders-proposal.md) (M1 built), [the shader notes](research/notes_shaders.md) §B.4 and [the deferred list](deferred.md): the game's own lit shaders drawn. `Renderer::game_shaders` (off by default, nothing in the UI yet) draws a solid lit mesh with the stock pair the engine would choose (`vslit`/`fslit`, `_nm`, `_sm`, `_sm_nm`; skinned ones too), compiled from the install by `mg-shader`, and the rest, or a pair that fails (reported once), with Moonglow's WGSL shader. The engine's OpenGL matrices are uploaded as they are and `mg-shader` takes the position's depth to wgpu's range in IR (no y flip); vertex inputs are bound by the engine's names from the reflected locations (`vIndex` read from u32 in IR), varyings matched by name, the fragment program's resources in bind group 1, the uniform blocks filled by name from the renderer's values and an MTR's parameters. The WGSL port's first oracle: on plain lit meshes (a textured sphere and ground with an area light, a point light and fog, a tile, a placeable, the skinned wolf) the stock shaders and Moonglow's agree within 1 in 255; where an environment map shows (`_sm`, `_nm`) they differ by up to 15 at the 99th percentile, because the game samples it with the roughness as a bias over the derivatives' level and Moonglow's shader at that level (in the deferred list). The time uniforms read back from the client: `worldtimerTimeOfDay` counts real milliseconds and `sceneCurrentFrame` frames.
* **Update**: [The shaders proposal](shaders-proposal.md) (M0 built), [the architecture](PLAN.md), [build and test](manual/09-build-and-test.md) and [the deferred list](deferred.md): the compiler for the game's own shaders, `mg-shader` (after `mg-image`, so that Verify can use it). A program is assembled as the engine does it (the preamble as a struct of its defines, `#include`s spliced through the load order, each line keeping its file and line), compiled by glslang (glslang-rs: its C++ vendored and built with `cc`, no CMake or network, so the Flatpak builds offline), its combined image samplers split and its inputs and outputs given locations by a pass of Moonglow's on the SPIR-V words (SPIRV-Tools' pass is in `spirv-tools-sys` but not reachable, and a patched copy would be a git dependency the Flatpak refuses; `shaderc` has no relaxed rules), read by naga 30, bound in group 0 and validated, with the uniform block's members, the textures and their samplers, and the varyings by name. Errors name the `.shd` and line glslang's message is at. Unit tests on authored shaders (and bad input as errors, not panics); a corpus test over every stock program and every program in the installed haks (`MOONGLOW_HAKS` adds a builder's): 65 of 65, and 102 of 102 with The Domains of Dread's haks (its 37 and the stock ones over its `inc_lighting`), in 0.7 s. Verify's `shader-compile` check reports a module's programs that don't compile, a stock program broken by a hak's include among them, at the custom file and line. What it leaves out is in the deferred list. The third-party notices carry glslang's own license. CI is green on ubuntu, windows and macos, the Release workflow built the AppImage, the Windows installer and the macOS disk image, and the Flatpak built offline.
* **Update**: [The shaders proposal](shaders-proposal.md) accepted by the owner: the game's shaders for every mesh in 2.0 (tier B, tier A on the way), native glslang and SPIRV-Tools, on by default once the frame budget is met, a program that doesn't compile drawn with Moonglow's own shader and reported by Verify (not magenta), and the `shaders` branch deleted, its note kept in `develop`. [The architecture](PLAN.md) §5.5 says so.
* **Creation**: [The shaders proposal](shaders-proposal.md): the game's own shaders in the area view as Moonglow 2.0's centerpiece. The tabled `shaders` branch's note is brought into develop as [deprecated](research/notes_custom_shaders.md), superseded by the proposal. A spike (`tools/shaders/spike.py`) took all 102 programs on this machine (65 stock, and The Domains of Dread's 37 custom shaders over its own `inc_lighting`) through glslang (`-V -R --amb --aml`), `spirv-opt --split-combined-image-sampler` and naga 30 (bindings renumbered in its IR) to valid shader modules; nothing is drawn yet. Two tiers (custom shaders, then the stock programs throughout), the uniform contract by name, costs (5–9 KB uniform blocks per draw), milestones M0–M6 and five decisions for the owner. The deferred list's two custom-shader entries point to it.
* **Update**: [The deferred list](deferred.md): the Conversation Editor saves a script parameter in the game's codepage, the one it shows it in: a value typed with "é" was saved as UTF-8 (two bytes, shown back as "Ã©"), now as the byte 0xE9, shown back as "é". Parameters are read and written in one place (`dialog::params_in` and `params_value_in`, with a codepage), which the editor and the Ink and Twine exports share.
* **Update**: [Conversations](manual/06-conversations.md) and [the deferred list](deferred.md): dansen9191's report ("script parameters in a conversation are lost on export of a dialogue to Ink"). Condition and action parameters go out to Ink and Twine and are read back with the conversation: after the script's name, `name=value` separated by spaces, quoted where a space is (`# do:a_give item="gold coins"`); a condition's in Ink as a tag naming it (`# if:c_has item=gold`, on the choice, or on its own line before a conditional divert), an NPC line's action in a Twine passage's tags as a query (`do:a_give?item=gold%20coins`); `%`, quotes and what Ink or Twine reads as syntax percent-escaped, so the story stays valid Ink and Twine. Names and values, and the speaker, quest and comment, are read and written in the game's codepage (they were taken as UTF-8). The round trip of every shipped conversation compares the parameters too. Not checked with inklecate (not on this machine). Seen on the way: the Conversation Editor saves the parameters it shows in the game's codepage as UTF-8 (in the deferred list).
* **Update**: [Modules](manual/03-modules.md), [the main window](manual/02-main-window.md) and [the deferred list](deferred.md): dansen9191's reports on the 2DA editor. A column's width is set by dragging the edge at the right of its name, and a double click there fits it to its widest cell; a drag on the rest of the name still moves the column. Widths are kept for the table's name in the settings (between runs), and the resource browser's read-only view of the table shows them too. That view (a game's table, not editable) had no vertical scroll bar in sight: its rows' scroll area was as wide as the table, its bar off at the far right, and the rows' numbers scrolled away sideways. It is now laid out as the editor's grid is: one scroll area for the rows with both bars, the column names and the rows' numbers following it. Each seen in pictures before and after (`table_read_only_and_column_widths` in `screens.rs`: spells.2da read-only, appearance.2da widened and fitted), and held by `a_table_s_column_is_widened_by_the_edge_of_its_name` and `a_game_table_read_only_scrolls` (which finds the scroll bar in the rendered picture, and fails on the old view).
* **Update**: [Blueprints](manual/05-blueprints.md) and [the deferred list](deferred.md): dansen9191's report. An appearance with no model to draw is said where the builder looks: a line in red under a placeable's Appearance Type, a door's appearance, a creature's Appearance and an item's Base Type Name, and in the model's place in the page ("No model: plc_x99 (placeables.2da row 451, ModelName) isn't in the module, its haks or the game"; a row the table lacks, one that names no model, one reserved as `USER`). The page's model kept showing the appearance before when one with no model was chosen (seen in a picture before and after: the chest stayed). The Appearance Gallery frames such rows in red. One rule for it, `mg_rules::appearance`, which the previews, Verify's `object-row` check and the warnings go by; they had differed: the previews had no `USER` rule (they said "no model user"), Verify called a row that names no model one that isn't there, and checked no model for a door without an `Appearance` field or a creature whose row has no `MODELTYPE` (the previews' reading kept: a generic door, a single model). Held by `an_appearance_s_row_must_name_a_model_that_is_there`, `doors_creatures_and_items_by_their_own_tables` and `a_placeable_s_appearance_without_a_model_is_said`; what the rule leaves out is in the deferred list.
* **Update**: [Areas](manual/04-areas.md): builders' asks. Area Properties › Visual lists the lighting schemes in as many columns as the page is wide, every one in sight (Heed's ask: one column, scrolled, left the page's width unused; seen in a picture before and after). Under them, **Copy From Area** gives this area another's Visual settings (lighting, fog, shadows, sky and weather), and **Saved Lighting** keeps them under a name, to give to any area of any module (dansen9191's ask), as files in Moonglow's data folder (`lighting`). Variable sets, prefabs and lighting are kept by one piece of code (`saved.rs`), which the first two had each written.
* **Update**: [Main window](manual/02-main-window.md), [modules](manual/03-modules.md), [areas](manual/04-areas.md) and [the deferred list](deferred.md): the side editors (a hak's, a 2DA's, a tileset's) share one path for their unsaved work, their closing and their Undo, where each had its own (the deferred list's "The side editors' unsaved work, close and undo"). One question for a tab closed with unsaved changes, as the module's asks it (**Save**, **Don't Save**, **Cancel**: they said Discard), with the tabs waiting asked of in turn: Close All with two changed haks asked only of the last. Undo and Redo go through the keymap to the editor under the pointer (the talk table's too) or the 2DA grid that has the keys: Ctrl+Z over a hak's or a tileset's editor undid the module's last change, and the 2DA grid read Ctrl+Z and Ctrl+Y by hand, so a key set in Options › Keyboard and Ctrl+Shift+Z did not reach it. With the pointer over one of these editors and nothing of its own to undo, nothing is undone (the talk table's fell through to the module's). `side_doc.rs`: the `SideDoc` trait, the one closing window and `Moonglow::local_undo`; held by `closing_two_changed_haks_asks_of_both`, `undo_over_a_hak_editor_is_the_hak_s` and `a_rebound_undo_key_reaches_a_table_s_grid`.
* **Update**: [Differences from Aurora](manual/12-differences.md), [the main window](manual/02-main-window.md), [areas](manual/04-areas.md), [blueprints](manual/05-blueprints.md), [options](manual/10-options.md), [the plugin examples](plugins/README.md) and [the deferred list](deferred.md): the owner's decision. Stores are called Stores everywhere, on purpose unlike Aurora, which says Merchants in some places: the area view's toolbar and its tips, the module tree's group (now in its alphabetical place, after Sounds), Find Instance's kinds, the palettes' **New Store…**, the Script Wizard's **Open a Store** (Aurora's form names it so; its label said Start a Merchant), **Options › Area › Show stores as $ signs**, the manual, and the example plugin (now `store-markup`). One name is left in the code (`ObjectKind::label`; `plural` is gone). A toolbar that hid "Merchants" in saved settings still hides stores, and saves them as "Stores"; the $ signs option's saved name is unchanged. Kept: the game's Merchant faction, and the Merchants category of the game's store palette (with the Store Wizard's name that follows it, "Merchants 001").
* **Update**: [The architecture](PLAN.md) and [the deferred list](deferred.md): the deferred list's "GIT's lists, spelled out about ten times" made one. `mg_rules::ObjectKind` is the nine types of placed object: blueprint type, GIT list and struct id (the generated schema's `git::*_LIST`, not a copy), names, and the fields that name a blueprint, its category and its name; `mg_area::ObjectKind` and `mg_module::palette::BlueprintKind` are that one type, and `verify::Category` takes its nine from it. Through it now: `instances::git_list` and Update Instances (`GIT_LISTS` is gone), references and what satisfies a blueprint's, Rename's kinds, family and a blueprint's own name field, Verify's categories, the content doctor's walk, `mg find`, Advanced Search's places, `mg update-instances`, the 2DA rows' kinds (mg-rules now builds on mg-schema), the area's objects, Find Instance and the Preview window, and Update Instances' window; the eight `if UTM { "ResRef" } else { "TemplateResRef" }` (and five in the corpus tests) are `resref_field`. Nothing shown changed: the one copy that was wrong, Update Instances' window calling an object of a list it doesn't know a "waypoint" where the others say "object", never met one. The window's own tables, and "Stores" against "Merchants", are in the deferred list.
* **Update**: [Blueprints](manual/05-blueprints.md) and [the deferred list](deferred.md): the first of the review's structural unifications. Every list that chooses a row of a table is one widget (`widgets::row_choice`), the blueprint fields' list as its source: a list of more than 12 rows by name (parts, costs and the palette's categories keep their order), a Filter field past 30 that narrows by name or to a row's number, opened at the row chosen, the arrow keys as in Aurora. The copies that had some of it now have all of it: an item property's sub-property (a potion's Cast Spell: its spells were unfiltered and opened at the top; seen in a picture before and after, `cast_spell_subtypes_opened` in `screens.rs`), cost and parameter, a creature's classes, domains, school, familiar and sound set type, a door's generic appearance, an encounter's difficulty, the palette category (no arrow keys before), the portrait list, the raw fields' rows, Advanced Search's rows, the 2DA editor's "a row of X.2da" (its rows' names made only while it is in use, there when a click opens it: seen in a picture, `table_row_list_opened`) and the Waypoint Wizard's appearance. The "name, else `(n)`" text is `mg_rules::choice_text`, in the level-up wizard, the special abilities and the store's restrictions too. Held by `an_item_property_s_spells_have_a_filter`, `arrow_keys_step_through_a_palette_category` and `a_table_s_row_list_opens_at_the_row_named`; what is left is in the deferred list.
* **Update**: [Areas](manual/04-areas.md), [blueprints](manual/05-blueprints.md) and [the deferred list](deferred.md): a review for logic written more than once (archvillainette's word on the builders' Discord: "one source of truth"), by five readers of the code. Copies that had drifted into a wrong result were made one, each with a test: the Variables window wrote an object's or a location's variable back as a string (OK rewrote every row; they are kept now, shown and not edited); `mg areas --set` and `--var` wrote text as UTF-8 and took any float (`Value::from_text`, which `mg set` shares, writes it in the codepage; `mg_module::vars` types a variable for both and the window); a tag was read as UTF-8 in Find Instance, the module tree, the palettes, Edit Together and Create Waypoint (which wrote the replacement character into the waypoint's tag); a 2DA closed by its tab's × with a cell being typed asked nothing, and lost it; a script or conversation name typed into a blueprint's, the module's or a conversation's field was lost when another page was clicked (the fix of 2026-10-12 had missed that field); Verify read a generic door's model from `GenericType`, which EE's doors lack (`GenericType_New`); the area view's grass left another override for the same material where `mg areas` takes it away; the conversation editor's Script Preview read a script in the game's codepage, not the scripts' (Polish: Windows-1250), and past the editor's unsaved text; an object copied from the module tree was pasted on the ground, however high it stood; a creature part's texture named past 16 characters was taken for missing in a preview; a plugin's `mg.image` turned a top-left TGA upside down; the tests looked for the game in fewer places than the toolset (no GOG, snap or second Steam library); Rename recompiled with the built-in compiler, past Options › Script Editor; the script editor's Replace refused what Find found ("É" for "é", whole words); one Enter pressed Next and Finish both in the store and blueprint wizards, and the Variables window's Save Set took its OK's too; and Delete typed in a field deleted the area's selected objects under the pointer. What was left, by file and line, is in the deferred list (Written more than once).
* **Update**: [Main window](manual/02-main-window.md) and [options](manual/10-options.md): Taro49's report. A window of tabs (a conversation, Properties, scripts…) moves by its tab bar alone: a drag on a part of its body that isn't a control moved it (egui_dock's windows have no title bar, so egui moves them by any part), and a click beside a control could move the conversation editor. **Options › General › Move windows by dragging anywhere in them** brings that back. Done once, behind every tab a window shows (`tabs.rs`); the toolset's dialogs already moved by their title bars alone.

## 2026-10-20
* **Add**: [Areas](manual/04-areas.md), [the command line](manual/11-command-line.md), [differences from Aurora](manual/12-differences.md), [the parity checklists](parity/checklists.md), [the GFF research notes](research/notes_gff_changelog.md) and [the deferred list](deferred.md): Bee AI's and Leoendethas's asks on Discord. **Area Properties › Grass** sets what the game's `SetAreaGrassOverride` and `SetAreaDefaultGrassDisabled` set while it runs: the tileset's grass on Grass faces or not, and grass of the area's own on any surface material (texture, density, height, ambient and diffuse colors), each change one step to undo, grown in the area's view as it changes (a drag or a color picker as it goes). The area view draws grass (**🌿 Grass** on its toolbar), which it did not: tufts on the tiles' walkmesh faces of each material, on the tiles within 30 m of the view's focus, near the camera within 3 of the client's mean color for the defaults, sparse, dense, tall and red grass (`grass_overrides_look`). They are kept where the engine reads them when the module loads, the GIT's `AreaProperties` (`GrassDefDisabled`, `GrassOverrides` › `GrassOvrList`): a server's save of a module read back (`engine_grass`, with the testkit's `save_game`); the research notes had them as ARE savegame fields, and the ARE's are not read. The manual names the fields for scripters. `mg areas --grass`, `--remove-grass` and `--tileset-grass` set them on many areas.
* **Add**: [Modules](manual/03-modules.md), [the command line](manual/11-command-line.md) and [the deferred list](deferred.md): builders' asks on Discord. **Edit › Advanced Search…** (a tab, docked as any other) and `mg search` find blueprints, placed objects (and the items they hold, in inventories, equipment and stores), areas and conversations by what they hold: variables by a wildcard name (`*sDeidad*`), type and value; item properties by type, subtype, value and parameter (Skill Bonus: Hide); creatures by class, feat, spell (known, memorized, special ability) and skill ranks; appearances; scripts attached; any field by label, text or a whole number (every field holding 103). Named through the game's 2DAs and talk table, the module's haks' too; an item blueprint found is also found where blueprints carry it. Held to a plain count of the fields of every shipped module and campaign (`module_search`); what it doesn't do is in the deferred list.
* **Update**: [Main window](manual/02-main-window.md), [areas](manual/04-areas.md), [blueprints](manual/05-blueprints.md), [build and test](manual/09-build-and-test.md), [options](manual/10-options.md), [the command line](manual/11-command-line.md) and [the deferred list](deferred.md): builders' reports. What the area view's toolbar hides (Triggers, Encounters…) is hidden in every area view, the open ones and those opened later, as in Aurora, and kept in the settings (Sacha's report). The log has **Clear** in its corner and View › Clear Log (Taro49's ask: a second Verify's report was mistaken for the first's). **Options › Verify** leaves groups of Verify Module's report out (missing references, tilesets, models and materials, 2DAs, objects' rows and variables, talk table and archives, plugins), and its summary line names what it left out with their counts; `mg verify --skip GROUP` does the same, and `mg checks` names each check's group (Taro49's ask: a hak's tileset errors hid the missing references). **Options › General › List a blueprint's categories alphabetically** sorts a blueprint's Category list by name; off by default, the list keeps the palette's order (Sacha's report).

## 2026-10-19
* **Update**: [Modules](manual/03-modules.md): Taro49's report. A NUI window moved in a nasher project to a folder its include leaves out was taken for deleted, and not read after a restart either (the project reads what its include takes in, as nasher packs it). Opening a project, and when a file moves there, the log now names the files in the source folders that the include leaves out and Moonglow would read; **Build › Add Left-Out Files to nasher.cfg** adds an include line for their kinds beside the project's own, and they are read.
* **Update**: [nwn-mdl-spec against Moonglow](research/notes_mdl_spec.md), [findings](findings.md) and [the deferred list](deferred.md): the spec's findings applied (its suite from 27 to 46 of 46 compiles, 44 to 45 of 46 decompiles, 13 to 14 of 14 stock models). The reader: `setfillumcolor` lights nothing (seen in the client: 28 of the game's meshes, the lanterns of `tni01` tiles, glowed in Moonglow alone), a light's values when left out are the game's, `parent` in lower case only, vertices merged by their values and walkmeshes through a neighbouring face, degenerate faces dropped, normals from smoothing groups and key lists in the geometry as the game makes them. The compiler: animated meshes animate (the animation node's vertex count), walkmesh planes, tangents (mirrored seams, a material's hint, found among a hak's files, the haks and the game), the children of see-through meshes in libstdc++'s order. The decompiler writes a bare `detonate`. The linter notes keywords the game does not read.
* **Creation**: [nwn-mdl-spec against Moonglow](research/notes_mdl_spec.md): the shared model specification's conformance suite run on Moonglow (27 of 46 compiles and 44 of 46 decompiles pass, as its published report says), each disagreement placed in the reader, the compiler or the decompiler; added to [the deferred list](deferred.md). [notes_models.md](research/notes_models.md) corrected: `setfillumcolor` is not read by the engine, and a light's defaults when absent. A body part's skin bones are found by a depth-first walk of the skeleton (the spec's MDL-SKIN-011), not by part number; [findings](findings.md) names the HD neck's bones rightly (it named `rbicep_g` for a bone that is `pelvis_g`).
* **Update**: [Areas](manual/04-areas.md): Sacha's report. An area's variables are its GIT's: the engine reads them from the GIT's root and none from the ARE (`engine_area_variables`), where the Advanced page's Variables… and `mg areas --var` wrote them, so a script read 0. Both now write the GIT, and move there what is in the ARE; Verify Module reports variables left in an ARE (`area-variables`).
* **Update**: [Main window](manual/02-main-window.md), [areas](manual/04-areas.md) and [blueprints](manual/05-blueprints.md): Sacha's asks. A placed object's row in the module tree has **Variables…** on its right-click menu, as Aurora's list has. The Variables window lists the variables by name (each case alike), and saves them in the order they were stored, new ones after: an untouched list is written as it was. Ctrl + wheel scales the selection only with the pointer on a selected object; elsewhere it zooms slowly, as with nothing selected (a builder moving the view with Ctrl + drag and then turning the wheel to zoom scaled the placeable selected).
* **Update**: [Areas](manual/04-areas.md): August's reports on the Tiles palette (a city tileset of a hak, `tkc02`). A feature's or group's picture in the Gallery shows it as it stands once placed in open ground, with the tiles around that its placement changes: a castle's gate, a group of one tile whose corners are castle, showed as that tile alone, where the area builds the castle around it. A tile's picture is seen from the corner that shows much more of it where the south-west shows little: a chasm bridge's wall, facing into the chasm, showed only its lip and the floor far below. What is drawn see-through (a tile, a group or a blueprint about to be placed) lays the depth of its solid meshes first, so its nearer surfaces hide its farther ones: a bridge tile's water was drawn over its bridge. Each seen in pictures before and after (`zz` shots of the hak's tileset, not kept) and held by tests (`group_placed`, `a_see_through_instance_s_near_surfaces_hide_its_far_ones`).
* **Update**: [Blueprints](manual/05-blueprints.md) and [findings](findings.md): Heed's reports on 1.34.0. An armor or a creature in Beamdog's HD bodies showed only its head and pelvis: the HD pack's other human male parts are skins compiled against the creature's skeleton (their bind pose one entry per node of `pmh0`, their bones its nodes), which Moonglow took for a compiled body part's skin the game does not draw. They are drawn now, following the skeleton's bones as it plays its animation, in previews and areas, and clicked where they are drawn (seen in Moonglow's pictures before and after); the client, with the HD parts in a scratch override, draws them the same (`creatures_look`). A body or armor part's list steps to the next part with the mouse wheel down over it, to the one before with it up, without opening (Heed's ask).
* **Add**: [Areas](manual/04-areas.md): the Tiles palette has the blueprint palettes' tools (a search of brushes and tiles' models, Expand and Collapse All, a count, their List or Gallery switch and the pictures' size); its terrains and crossers are headings that open and close in the Gallery; a tile's picture double-clicked opens the tile's view (its model, and its corners, edges, lights, loops, doors and sounds), and one dragged into the area is placed where it is dropped; its features and groups are pictures of their tiles together, to click or drag in too. The Tiles palette's **Gallery** shows, under each terrain and crosser, every tile it can make as a picture (each variant and transition, none of a group's); one chosen is painted as it is, by a click or along a drag, turned by a right click, the tiles around fitting themselves to it. **Refine Tile** dragged makes every tile passed of the first's kind that tile (the first stepped to its next), and with a Gallery tile chosen first makes the tiles it fits that one (August's ask).

## 2026-10-18
* **Update**: [Conversations](manual/06-conversations.md), [build and test](manual/09-build-and-test.md) and [NUI Creator](manual/18-nui-creator.md): builders' reports on 1.33.0. The module tree marks one object, the one selected last: an object's row clicked in the tree stayed marked when another was selected in the area. A conversation's link goes to the line it links to by a double click, as in Aurora, and by its menu's **Go to Linked**, the branches above opening out; a bookmark goes there the same way. Verify Module says its errors first, then its warnings, and objects placed from a blueprint the module no longer has last, one line a blueprint with its count and areas, said as no fault (a builder had some 200 lines of them among the errors). The NUI Creator's Build says that its scripts are made in the module and written by File › Save (into a nasher project's sources): it said "written", read as on disk; Save writes them where the project's rules put them, as a test holds. But a project Moonglow made took in only scripts and JSON (`src/**/*.{nss,json}`, as `nasher init` writes): the window's `jui` and `txt` were written and then neither read back nor packed. A project Moonglow makes now takes in NUI windows and the module's other kinds of file, and a save that writes a file the project's sources leave out says so and what to add. A source file moved within the project while it is open is read from where it is now; it was taken for deleted (a builder's NUI window, moved, was not found). In the preview, a helmet and a shield show as worn, a shield from its front: they were stood up as an item modeled lying is, and came up on their sides.
